8.3. Plans for Pediatric Drug Development
The Applicant has an agreed upon pediatric study plan that includes deferred pediatric studies in
patients 12 to <18 years of age and 6 to <12 years of age and a partial waiver of studies in
children under 6 years of age because studies would be highly impracticable in this age group.
Although CSU development programs may evaluate adolescents in the phase 3 trials in adults,
the Agency advised the Applicant to defer pediatric studies until the benefit/risk in adults was
better characterized given the novelty of the mechanistic target for this indication, the
symptomatic and self-limited nature of the condition, and the potential risks associated with
BTKi.
Modeling and Simulation Activity Part 1 will use adult data to select appropriate doses for
adolescents, while Part 2 will incorporate data from both adult and adolescent studies to
determine optimal dosing for children aged 6 to 11 years.

(b) (4)
(b) (4)

For adolescents aged 12 to less than 18 years, the plan includes Study CLOU064F12301, a
double-blind, placebo-controlled, nonconfirmatory study designed to evaluate pharmacokinetics,
efficacy, and safety of remibrutinib. This study will enroll at least 75 adolescent subjects with
CSU who have inadequate response to H1-antihistamines, with coprimary endpoints of absolute
change from baseline in ISS7 and HSS7 at Week 12. The study includes a 24-week treatment
period followed by a 4-week follow-up period and was ongoing at the time of the original NDA
review.

Pregnancy, Lactation, and Reproductive Potential Assessment
The review team consulted the Division of Pediatrics and Maternal Health (DPMH) to evaluate
the proposed pregnancy and lactation labeling. For more details, refer to the DPMH consult
memo dated August 28, 2025, in DARRTS (Reference ID 5649755).
The phase 3 trials excluded pregnant and lactating women from enrollment; however, nine
pregnancies occurred across ongoing and completed studies, with treatment discontinued upon
discovery. Five of the nine pregnancies had a known outcome: two full-term healthy infants, one
spontaneous abortion in a 33-year-old subject with risk factors (obesity and oral contraceptive
use) that was not attributed to study treatment, and two elective abortions due to subject decision
with no additional information provided.
The results of DPMH’s literature search using terms related to BTK inhibitors and pregnancy
outcomes revealed limited data to inform remibrutinib use during pregnancy. Based on the
literature review and animal data, DPMH concluded that available clinical pregnancy data are
insufficient to identify drug-associated risks. DPMH did not recommend including language in
Warnings and Precautions for embryo-fetal toxicity, but they recommended including the
nonclinical data in Subsection 8.1 “Risk Summary” and “Data-Animal Data.” Additional
recommendations were to include a statement on the U.S. background risk of major birth defects
and miscarriage at the end of the “Risk Summary” in Subsection 8.1, per the Pregnancy and
Lactation Labeling Rule.
For lactation considerations, the DPMH found no nonclinical or clinical data on remibrutinib's
presence in animal or human milk, nor any data on its effects on breastfed infants or milk
production. Despite the likelihood of remibrutinib transfer into human milk based on the drug's
characteristics, DPMH determined that remibrutinib does not share the same severe adverse
event profile as other BTK inhibitors and recommended including standard developmental
benefit-risk language in labeling Subsection 8.2.
DPMH did not recommend including labeling Subsection 8.3 because there are no concerns for
embryo-fetal toxicity when remibrutinib is used at clinically relevant doses and no fertility
concerns based on animal fertility studies.
94
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Because animal reproduction studies with remibrutinib suggested malformations at high dose
multiples, animal reproduction studies in other drugs in the class have demonstrated embryofetal toxicity at clinically relevant doses and remibrutinib is systemically absorbed and likely to
be transferred to the fetus, DPMH recommended issuing PMRs for a pregnancy registry and
database study. Additionally, there is the potential for remibrutinib use during lactation, for
which DPMH recommended a PMR for a clinical lactation study (see Section 24).

Biopharmaceutics
The Applicant is seeking approval for the proposed drug product remibrutinib film-coated
tablets, 25 mg, via the 505(b)(1) pathway. It is an immediate-release dosage form for oral
administration. The clinical development of remibrutinib for CSU has been conducted under
IND 131325. Remibrutinib is a new molecular entity, and it is the free form of a weak base
showing high permeability and low solubility at physiological pH (Biopharmaceutics
Classification System Class 2). The Applicant developed 25-mg, 50-mg, and 100-mg strengths
but this NDA only includes the 25-mg strength. All strengths have formulation proportionality.

acceptance criterion. The proposed dissolution method and acceptance criterion are acceptable
from biopharmaceutics perspective.

The Applicant states that all studies were conducted in full compliance with the International

Clinical Site Inspections
Two clinical trial sites from each pivotal trial (A2301 and A2302) were selected for inspection.
All four trial sites are in the United States. Several unreported protocol deviations were identified
at one site (Site 5007, Dr. Gogate); however, none of the deviations caused harm or were
expected to impact the trial’s efficacy or safety results. Based on the results of the inspections,
the Office of Scientific Investigations concluded that Trials A2301 and A2302 were conducted
adequately, and the trial data submitted, including the primary efficacy endpoint data, appear
acceptable in support of the respective indication. Please refer to the Clinical Inspection
Summary from the Office of Scientific Investigations, dated August 4, 2025.

recommended in the guidance for industry, Financial Disclosure by Clinical Investigators
(February 2013), and by 21 CFR 54.4 (see Section 25). The investigator financial disclosures do
not raise questions about the integrity of the data. The primary efficacy endpoints are based on
validated measurements that are not vulnerable to investigator bias. In conclusion, the likelihood

13. Pharmacology Toxicology
13.1. Summary Review of Studies Submitted With
the Investigational New Drug Application
The Applicant has conducted a comprehensive program of pharmacology, PK, and toxicology
studies to support clinical development and the NDA submission of remibrutinib (referred to
during development as LOU064). All pivotal toxicity studies have been submitted and reviewed
previously under IND 131325. The impurities as well as some pharmacology studies have been
reviewed herein under NDA 218436. The key findings from the nonclinical studies are cited and
summarized in this review to support the approval of NDA 218436.
Pharmacological studies demonstrated that remibrutinib inhibited both unphosphorylated and
phosphorylated BTK with a >200-fold selectivity for BTK over BTK related enzymes TEC and
BMX based on IC50 evaluation in enzymatic kinase assays. In cell-based assays, the inhibition of
BTK was associated with reduced lymphocyte activation, inhibition of FcγR and FcεR, and
reduced secretion of IL-6. Studies also demonstrated remibrutinib inhibition of collagen-induced
platelet aggregation. In vivo, remibrutinib showed a response in two models of skin mast cell
degranulation induced by FcγR and FcεR mediated hypersensitivity, a mouse reverse passive
Arthus model, and a mouse passive cutaneous anaphylaxis model. These models are relevant for
CSU.
The Applicant has conducted subchronic and chronic toxicology studies with remibrutinib in
both rats and dogs. Target organs of toxicity were identified as the lymph nodes (underdeveloped
germinal centers, sinusoidal red blood cells), thyroid gland (follicular hypertrophy), liver
(hepatocellular hypertrophy, multinucleated hepatocytes, pigment), pancreas (islet fibrosis,
pigment), and blood (prolongation of bleeding, inhibition of platelet aggregation). The
nonclinical findings were not considered clinically dose-limiting. The nonclinical general
toxicology studies provided sufficient support for the clinical development program.
In animal reproduction studies, remibrutinib was nonteratogenic and did not affect fertility in
rats. Pregnant rats dosed with remibrutinib during the period of organogenesis did not show fetal
malformations at doses up to 1000 mg/kg/day (approximately 126x the MRHD). In pregnant
rabbits, remibrutinib treatment resulted in signs of maternal toxicity (clinical signs of toxicity,
weight loss, and decreased food consumption) and adverse developmental outcomes
(open/opaque eyes, small jaws, hyperflexion of forelimbs) at doses ≥300 mg/kg/day
(approximately 141x the MRHD). In a pre- and postnatal development study in rats, potentially
remibrutinib-related findings were observed at the highest dose of 1000 mg/kg/day dose
(approximately 194x the MRHD). Toxicity in two maternal rats resulted in early euthanasia of
these animals with one animal exhibiting reduced food consumption, reduced body weight, and
poor clinical signs followed by delivery of five stillborn pups and five small, cold, weak pups
that were terminated on Postnatal Day 0 to 1. In the second animal, poor clinical signs were
observed along with total litter loss. Overall, the 1000 mg/kg/day dose group exhibited a reduced
litter size (−11.8% compared to controls), an increase in the number of stillborn pups (15 versus
1 in the control and 300 mg/kg/day dose groups), and an increase in the number of pup deaths.
No adverse remibrutinib-related changes were observed in maternal rats and offspring at doses of
≤300 mg/kg/day (approximately 58x the MRHD).
102
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Remibrutinib was not mutagenic in in vitro or in vivo assays. A 2-year oral study in Wistar rats
and a 6-month oral study in Tg.rasH2 mice were conducted to assess the carcinogenic potential
of remibrutinib. No evidence of tumorigenicity was observed in male and female Tg.rasH2 mice
at doses up to 1500 mg/kg/day or in rats at exposures approximately 8x and 98x the MRHD in
males and female rats, respectively.
To conclude, a complete and adequate nonclinical program was submitted, and NDA 218436 is
recommended for approval from the nonclinical perspective.
Pharmacology
Remibrutinib is a small molecule inhibitor of BTK that inhibits human BTK with nanomolar
potency (IC50: 0.6 to 7.5nM) and was generally more selective for BTK compared to the BTKrelated enzymes TEC and BMX (IC50: 124 to 141nM).
In vivo, optimized-for-aging (OFA)-1 and Wistar rats were used to assess BTK target occupancy
in the spleen and blood following changes to dose, exposure, and time. Following doses up to
10-mg/kg remibrutinib, these studies demonstrated high occupancy in the spleen and blood, and
corresponding blood levels ranging from 2 to 71nM. Blood levels of remibrutinib were maximal
by 0.5 hours postdose while BTK occupancy exhibited different kinetics in the blood and spleen
with an estimated BTK half-life of 87 hours in the rat blood and 5 to 8 hours in the spleen.
In a mouse reverse passive Arthus model of a local type III hypersensitivity reaction, oral doses
up to 100 mg/kg of remibrutinib were administered prior to induction of the Arthus response. In
this model, remibrutinib pretreatment resulted in reduced skin swelling. In a BALB/c mouse
passive cutaneous anaphylaxis (PCA) model, oral remibrutinib administration at doses up to
100 mg/kg inhibited PCA at all doses while a minimal effect was observed at high-dose IgE
sensitization. These results suggest that remibrutinib was effective following low-dose IgE
sensitization in this model of anaphylaxis-driven skin edema relevant to urticaria.
In a model of B-cell response to sheep red blood cells (SRBC) in OFA rats, remibrutinib was
administered at daily doses up to 3 mg/kg for 3 days following immunization with SRBC.
Following dosing, remibrutinib dose dependently decreased the number of SRBC-specific IgM
secreting B-cells by 66 to 96%. This model showed correlation between remibrutinib
pharmacokinetics, tissue BTK (spleen and blood) occupancy, and a PD effect.
Safety Pharmacology
A complete battery of safety pharmacology studies has been conducted with remibrutinib. The
remibrutinib IC50 at human ether-à-go-go-related gene channels expressed in HEK293 cells was
calculated to be 1.4μM. The effects of remibrutinib on central nervous system function and the
respiratory system were examined using a functional observation battery in a single-dose oral
gavage study in Wistar rats. There were no effects on autonomic, neurologic, behavioral
observations or for respiratory rate, tidal volume, minute volume, or peak inspiratory or
expiratory flow observed at up to 1000 mg/kg. The effects of remibrutinib on cardiovascular
system function were examined in a single-dose oral gavage telemetry study in Beagle dogs. No
effects on body temperatures, heart rates, arterial pressures, or ECG parameters (including PQ,
RR, QRS, QT, and QTv) were observed at doses up to 450 mg/kg.

103
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Absorption, Distribution, Metabolism, Excretion
In rats, remibrutinib was rapidly absorbed, with a bioavailability of 7 to 29% after oral
administration. A single oral dose of remibrutinib resulted in broad distribution in all tissues
except for the brain, lens of the eye, spinal cord, and white fat, with a Tmax of 0.5 to 8 hours.
Similar distribution was noted in both sexes. Specific retention of total radiolabeled components
in the pigmented uveal tract/retina was observed. The highest exposures (based on AUClast) were
observed for the kidney medulla, small intestine mucosa, skin, cecum mucosa, kidney cortex,
liver, and uveal tract/retina. Following IV or oral administration in rats, remibrutinib appeared to
be eliminated via metabolism with <4% of the parent compound detected in excreta and a pattern
of metabolites that was qualitatively and quantitatively similar.
In dogs, a single oral dose of remibrutinib resulted in moderate absorption (41.9%) with a
bioavailability of 13.5% and a median Tmax of 2 hours. Remibrutinib was distributed into tissues
(volume of distribution at steady state [Vss] =1.26 L/kg) and exhibited high clearance ([CL] =187
L/h/kg). Excretion occurred mainly in the feces, with excretion by urine representing a minor
fraction.
Remibrutinib was associated with rapid metabolism in the mouse, rat, dog, and human, with 31
metabolites identified across all species. The major metabolic pathways consisted of Ndemethylation, followed by glutathione or cysteine adducts and hydrogenation leading to M11
and M17 formation observed in the mouse, rat, and human, or O-dealkylation leading to M22
observed in all species, and glucuronidation leading to M7 observed in all species. Two
metabolites formed by C-hydroxylation (M25) and C-hydroxylation and N-hydroxylation (M27)
were only detected in in vitro human hepatocyte incubation with only M25 also observed in vivo
at levels of 1.12% of the exposure based on AUC0-24h. The were no major human metabolites
identified.
Very low levels of degradation were observed for remibrutinib following incubation in blood or
plasma from the rat, dog, or human for up to 2 hours with preferential distribution to the plasma
in all tested species and blood distribution appearing to be concentration independent.
Remibrutinib highly bound to plasma proteins with no concentration dependence: 89.8%, 92.8%,
and 95.4% in the rat, dog, and human, respectively. Formation of covalent drug-protein adducts
was observed in human liver microsomes and human hepatocytes. Coincubation with glutathione
in human liver microsomes resulted in a 55% decrease in covalent drug-protein adducts,
suggesting that glutathione-mediated activation of remibrutinib contributes to the formation of
these adducts. Formation of covalent drug-protein adducts was time-dependent in human, dog,
and rat blood and plasma, with adduct formation higher in plasma than blood, and with adduct
formation in rat and dog plasma higher than that observed in human plasma. Remibrutinib
covalently bound plasma proteins without metabolic activation at high levels. Using recombinant
enzymes or selective enzymatic inhibitors (ketoconazole and azamulin), CYP3A4 was
determined to be the major human liver enzyme implicated in oxidative metabolism of
remibrutinib in the liver with potential contributions for CYP1A1 and CYP2C19.
General Toxicology
As listed in the table below, general toxicity studies with remibrutinib were conducted in rats for
up to 26 weeks and in dogs for up to 39 weeks. The pivotal studies have been reviewed
previously under IND 131325 and are listed below.

Repeat-Dose Toxicology Studies in Rats
A 2-Week Rat Study (1470733; 42325 TSR)
In a 2-week oral repeat-dose toxicity study in Wistar rats (10/sex/group), animals were
administered doses of 0 (vehicle), 30, 100, 300, and 1000 mg/kg/day of remibrutinib. Recovery
groups (5/sex for the control and 1000 mg/kg/day dose groups) were included to assess
reversibility of the findings. Mortality was observed in one female administered 100 mg/kg/day
of remibrutinib which was considered potentially related to test article induced prolongation of
bleeding during a tail-tip bleeding test. Target tissues were identified as the blood, lymph nodes
(decreased paracortex in mesenteric, sinusoidal red blood cells), spleen (decreased periarteriolar
lymphoid sheaths and decreases in marginal zone), thyroid (minimal follicular hypertrophy), and
liver (increased mitosis, periportal vacuolation, focal necrosis, and inflammatory cell foci). Mild,
reversible increases in WBC, lymphocytes (including B-cells and T-cells) were observed in both
sexes administered ≥100 mg/kg/day of remibrutinib. Administration of remibrutinib was also
associated with inhibition of collagen-induced platelet aggregation and prolonged bleeding time
observed in both sexes at all doses. Due to changes in hemostasis at ≥30 mg/kg/day, the
Applicant did not establish any no-observed-adverse-effect level (NOAEL) in this study;
however, given the hemostatic effects are related to the pharmacology of remibrutinib and are
monitorable, a NOAEL was considered to be 30 mg/kg/day with an associated Day 14 AUC0-24h
of 13000 ng*h/mL in males and 31900 ng*h/mL in females.
A 13-Week Rat Study (1570128; 43328 TCR)
In a 13-week good laboratory practice (GLP) toxicity study, Wistar rats (10/sex/group) were
dosed by oral gavage with 0 (vehicle), 10, 100, and 1000 mg/kg/day remibrutinib. No recovery
groups were included in the study. Following administration of remibrutinib, rats exhibited mild
immune suppression manifested by decreased circulating B-cells (6 to 32%) in all doses in both
sexes and microscopic changes in lymphoid tissues, including decreased cellularity in the
mandibular and mesenteric lymph nodes coupled with increased frequency of sinusoidal red
blood cells and decreased periarteriolar lymphoid sheaths and reduction in marginal zones in the
spleen. These effects were attributed to the pharmacology of the test article and are monitorable
in the clinical setting. In addition to the immunosuppression-related findings, the rats dosed with
≥100 mg/kg/day of remibrutinib exhibited changes in the thyroid (follicular hypertrophy) and
liver (hepatocellular hypertrophy). No NOAEL was established by the Applicant in this study;
however, given the hemostatic effects are related to the pharmacology of remibrutinib and are
105
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
monitorable, a NOAEL was considered to be 1000 mg/kg/day with an associated Day 89
AUC0-24h of 4780 ng*h/mL in males and 52800 ng*h/mL in females.
A 26-Week Rat Study (1670372; 45306 TCR)
In a 26-week GLP toxicity study, Wistar rats (20/sex/group) were dosed by oral gavage with 0,
30, 300, and 1000 mg/kg/day remibrutinib. The 300 and/or 1000 mg/kg/day groups showed
dose-related increases in liver weights and microscopic changes in the liver, lymph nodes,
pancreas, and thyroid glands, with females showing more pronounced effects than males. The
following findings were noted in the females: the liver showed minimal hepatocellular
hypertrophy, multinucleated hepatocytes, and pigment generally in the 1000 mg/kg/day dose
group. The percent of rats with hepatocellular hypertrophy was 10% (2/20) and 65% (13/20) in
the 300 and 1000 mg/kg/day dose groups, respectively, accompanied by increases in absolute
mean liver weights (by 18% and 51%, respectively). The lymph nodes (both mandibular and
mesenteric lymph nodes) showed less developed germinal centers and sinusoidal red blood cells.
The pancreas showed minimal islet fibrosis and pigment, which have been reported to be ratspecific effects of BTK inhibitors. The thyroid glands showed follicular hypertrophy. The
NOAEL was 30 mg/kg/day with an associated Day 176 AUC0-24h of 5970 ng*h/mL in males and
30900 ng*h/mL in females.
Repeat-Dose Toxicology Studies in Dogs
A 2-Week Dog Study (1470732; 42326 TSC)
In a 2-week GLP oral repeat-dose toxicity study in beagle dogs, animals (3/sex/group) were
administered doses of 0 (vehicle), 50, 200, and 450 mg/kg/day of remibrutinib. No recovery
groups were included in the study. Target tissues were identified as the blood and lymph nodes,
with alterations in hemostasis including prolongation of bleeding time and inhibition of ADPand collagen-induced platelet aggregation. Microscopic findings considered potentially related to
the test article were limited to sinusoidal red blood cells observed in both the mandibular and
mesenteric lymph nodes in both sexes in all dose groups that correlated with macroscopic
observations of red discoloration. Exposures of remibrutinib increased in a generally doseproportional manner from 50 to 200 mg/kg/day with no obvious increase in exposure levels from
200 to 450 mg/kg/day suggesting saturation of the test article. High variability was observed;
however, exposures were higher in females than males (by 1.9- to 5.4-fold). The NOAEL was
considered the highest dose of 450 mg/kg/day, though higher exposures were noted at
200 mg/kg/day, resulting in a mean AUC0-24h of 10400 ng*h/mL in males and a mean AUC0-24h
of 56300 ng*h/mL in females on Day 13.
A 13-Week Dog Study (1570127; 43329 TCC
In a 13-week GLP oral repeat-dose toxicity study in beagle dogs, animals (3/sex/group) were
administered doses of 0 (vehicle), 10, 50, and 300 mg/kg/day remibrutinib. No recovery groups
were included in the study. Following administration of remibrutinib, dogs exhibited mild
immune suppression manifested by decreased circulating B-cells (42 to 80%) in all doses in both
sexes and microscopic changes in lymphoid tissues in all doses in both sexes, including
increased frequencies of sinusoidal red blood cells in the mandibular and mesenteric lymph
nodes and decreased germinal center development in the gut-associated lymphoid tissue. These
effects were attributed to the pharmacology of the test article and are monitorable in the clinical
106
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
setting. Exposures increased in a less than dose-proportional manner with slightly (<2-fold)
increased exposures noted in females at ≥50 mg/kg/day. The NOAEL was considered the highest
dose of 300 mg/kg/day with an associated mean AUC0-24h of 6350 and 9310 ng*h/mL in males
and females, respectively, on Days 87 to 89.
A 39-Week Dog Study (1670373; 45307 TCC
In a 39-week GLP oral repeat-dose toxicity study in beagle dogs, animals (4/sex/group) were
administered doses of 0 (vehicle), 10, 50, and 300 mg/kg/day remibrutinib. No recovery groups
were included in the study. Microscopic findings were observed in the mesenteric lymph node
(decreased cellularity and decreases in germinal center development) and gut-associated
lymphoid tissue (decreases in germinal center development). Red discoloration was observed in
the cecum, duodenum, ileum, iliac lymph nodes, and mesenteric lymph nodes in several animals
from remibrutinib-treated dose groups and a red mass observed in the gastric lymph node in a
single male from the 300-mg/kg/day dose group. In the mandibular and mesenteric lymph nodes,
this observation correlated with microscopic findings of minimal to moderate sinusoidal red
blood cells in both sexes and at all doses (including controls) with increasing severity generally
associating with dose. Increased fibrinogen was observed in some animals at ≥50 mg/kg/day. In
the liver, there was increased incidence and severity of pigment in remibrutinib-treated groups
compared to controls with increased intensity of positive Schmorl’s stain (indicates the presence
of melanin, argentaffin, chromaffin, bile, and formalin pigment) in the 300-mg/kg/day dose
group compared to controls, suggestive of lipofuscin, which is considered nonadverse. The
NOAEL was considered to be 300 mg/kg/day with an associated combined male and female Cmax
and AUC0-24h of 4735 ng/mL and 43550 ng*h/mL, respectively.

Adequate exposure margins between the nonclinical general toxicology studies and proposed
human doses of remibrutinib were obtained throughout the clinical development program.

Genetic Toxicology
Remibrutinib was considered negative for genotoxicity in an Ames test, an in vitro micronucleus
assay in peripheral human lymphocytes, and an in vivo micronucleus assay in rats.

Carcinogenicity
Mouse Carcinogenicity Study (1870307; 8411537)
A 26-week oral carcinogenicity study of remibrutinib in Tg.rasH2 mice showed no evidence of
carcinogenicity in mice (25/sex/dose) dosed by oral gavage with 0 (water), 0 (vehicle), 150, 500,
and 1500-mg/kg/day remibrutinib once daily. The Agency concurred with the dose selection for
the study on December 2, 2019. Remibrutinib had no effects on survival rates. The remibrutinib
treatment groups did not exhibit increases in the incidence of tumor findings. Non-neoplastic
findings included decreases in germinal center cellularity in the spleen and mesenteric lymph
nodes and findings of hepatocyte cytoplasmic eosinophilic inclusions, karyocytomegaly, and/or
multinucleated hepatocytes in the liver. A positive control group (N-methyl-N-nitrosourea;
75 mg/kg/day) showed expected increases in tumor incidences in the hemolymphoreticular
tissue, stomach, and skin. Remibrutinib was negative for carcinogenicity under these study
conditions.
Rat Carcinogenicity Study (1870308; 8425081)
A 104-week oral carcinogenicity study of remibrutinib in Wistar rats (50/sex/dose) showed no
evidence of carcinogenicity at oral doses of 0 (water), 0 (vehicle), 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day
remibrutinib once daily. The Agency concurred with the dose selection for the study on
September 23, 2019. Remibrutinib had no effects on survival rates. No remibrutinib-related
neoplastic changes were noted up to the highest tested dose of 300 mg/kg/day. Non-neoplastic
microscopic findings included findings in the lymphoid tissues (sinusoidal red blood cells,
sinusoidal dilatation, focal angiomatous hyperplasia, diffuse reticuloendothelial
hypertrophy/hyperplasia, and/or pigmented macrophages), thyroid (follicular cell hyperplasia),
and pancreas (islet fibrosis). Remibrutinib was negative for carcinogenicity under these study
conditions.

Reproductive Toxicology
Remibrutinib did not affect fertility and was nonteratogenic in rats. In rabbits, remibrutinib
treatment resulted in maternal toxicity and adverse developmental outcomes at doses
≥300 mg/kg/day. In the rat pre- and postnatal development study, remibrutinib treatment resulted
in increased number of stillborn pups, dead pups, and a smaller litter size at 1000 mg/kg/day.
Fertility Study (1770589)
In a fertility study, male and female rats (Wistar, 24/sex/dose) were dosed by oral gavage with 0,
100, 300, or 1000 mg/kg/day remibrutinib. Males were dosed for 9 weeks starting from 28 days
108
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
prior to mating and through mating. Females were dosed for 14 days prior to mating, through
mating, and until Gestation Day (GD) 6. Females were sacrificed on GD 13 for uterine content
examinations. No treatment-related findings were observed in any of the fertility parameters
evaluated. Therefore, the NOAEL was 1000 mg/kg/day.
Embryo-Fetal Development Studies (1670380; 1670382)
In an embryo-fetal developmental study (1670380), pregnant Wistar rats (22/dose) were dosed
by oral gavage with 0, 100, 300, or 1000 mg/kg/day remibrutinib during GDs 6 to 17, sacrificed
on GD 21, and examined for fetal malformations and variations. No treatment-related effects
were observed in any of the key parameters. The NOAEL was 1000 mg/kg/day, corresponding to
a Cmax of 4370 ng/mL and AUC0-24h of 49200 ng*h/mL.
In another embryo-fetal developmental study (1670382), pregnant rabbits (New Zealand White,
20/dose) were dosed by oral gavage with 0, 100, 300, or 450 mg/kg/day remibrutinib during GDs
7 to 20, sacrificed on GD 29, and examined for fetal malformations and variations. Mortality
(euthanasia) occurred at ≥300 mg/kg/day. One doe administered 300 mg/kg/day and the entire
group of does administered 450 mg/kg/day were terminated during the period of GD 14 to 22
due to severe toxicities including abortion, clinical signs, weight loss, and decreases in feed
consumption. The clinical signs included subdued/decreased activity, cold to touch, irregular
respiration, decreased fecal output, pale pinnae, partially closed eyes, and red liquid material.
The 450-mg/kg/day dose group was excluded from the analysis of fetal malformations and
variations due to severe maternal toxicity. The 300-mg/kg/day dose group showed slight but
statistically nonsignificant increases in a few external malformations such as eye opening, small
jaws, and hyperflexion of forelimbs. No increases in the incidence of malformations or variations
were observed in the 100-mg/kg/day dose group. The NOAEL was 100 mg/kg/day,
corresponding to a Cmax of 2360 ng/mL and AUC0-24h of 8990 ng*h/mL.
Pre- and Postnatal Development Study (2070356)
In a pre- and postnatal development study, pregnant rats received daily oral doses of remibrutinib
at 0 (vehicle), 100, 300, and 1000 mg/kg/day from GD 6 to Lactation Day 21. Potentially
remibrutinib-related findings were observed for the 1000-mg/kg/day dose group. Toxicity in two
maternal rats resulted in early euthanasia of these animals, with one animal exhibiting reduced
food consumption, reduced body weight, and poor clinical signs followed by delivery of five
stillborn pups and five small, cold, weak pups that were terminated on Postnatal Days 0 to 1. In
the second animal, poor clinical signs were observed along with total litter loss. Overall, the
1000-mg/kg/day dose group exhibited a reduced litter size (−11.8% compared to controls), an
increase in the number of stillborn pups (15 versus 1 in the control and 300-mg/kg/day dose
groups), and an increase in the number of pup deaths. No adverse remibrutinib-related changes
were observed in maternal rats and offspring at doses of ≤300 mg/kg/day. Therefore, the
NOAEL was identified at 300 mg/kg/day.

13.2. Individual Reviews of Studies Submitted
With the New Drug Application
Pharmacology
In Vitro Binding and Cellular Activity Study (RD-2014-00811)
Pharmacology studies submitted by the Applicant evaluated the effect of remibrutinib in vitro
and in vivo. In these studies, remibrutinib generally demonstrated inhibition of both
unphosphorylated and phosphorylated BTK with IC50s that were >200-fold lower that those
observed for the related enzymes TEC and BMX (0.6 to 7.5nM versus 124 to 141nM), with no
demonstrated inhibition of ITK or TXK. In the same study using a kinase panel, remibrutinib
demonstrated inhibition of BTK with an IC50 of 1.1nM and PI4Kb with an IC50 of 3.1µM with no
apparent inhibition of other protein and lipid kinases. In an in vitro off-target screening panel,
remibrutinib exhibited minimal off-target effects with inhibition of NaV1.5, human BSEP, and
the rat transporter VMAT-2 observed with IC50s >3µM.
In Ramos B-cell cultures, remibrutinib inhibited human phosphorylated BTK (pY551), cluster of
differentiation (CD69 and CD86, and IL-6 secretion. In a human monocytic cell line, Tohoku
Hospital Pediatrics-1 (THP-1) cells, remibrutinib inhibited FcγR signaling, while less potent
inhibition was observed in polymorphonuclear leukocytes. This finding is consistent with the
relative BTK expression levels in these cell types. Remibrutinib exhibited only minimal effects
on proliferation of THP-1 cells (IC50 of 35µM), and in murine Ba/F3 cells including IL-13independent subclones (IC50 >11µM; except for Ba/F3 transformed by BMX [IC50 of 5.5µM]),
and mouse bone marrow cells (IC50 of 1.97µM). Remibrutinib inhibited the mouse and human
mixed lymphocyte reaction with IC50s of <4.6 and 3nM, respectively. The epidermal growth
factor receptor signaling did show evidence of inhibition (IC50 >60µM) after prolonged in vitro
incubation (5 hours).

Remibrutinib also inhibited collagen-induced platelet aggregation at low but not high collagen
concentrations, suggesting that BTK inhibition blocks collagen-induced platelet aggregation at
low agonist concentrations.
In an evaluation of tissue staining for human BTK using commercial antibodies, moderate to
strong signals for BTK were generally observed in B-lymphocytes in the lymph node, spleen,
tonsil, and thymic medulla with occasional staining of mast cells and plasma cells. Nonlymphoid
tissues included epithelium (breast, renal tubules, respiratory, spleen), hepatocytes, and
endometrial glands.
In Vivo PD Effects (BTK Target Occupancy; RD-2014-00816)
In vivo, OFA-1 and Wistar rats were used to assess BTK target occupancy in the spleen and
blood using different doses, exposures, and times. Following doses of 1-, 3-, and 10-mg/kg
remibrutinib, these studies demonstrated 75 to 99% occupancy in the spleen and 13 to 96%
occupancy in the blood, with corresponding blood levels ranging from 2 to 71nM. Blood levels
of remibrutinib were maximal by 0.5 hours postdose while BTK occupancy exhibited different
kinetics in the blood and spleen with an estimated BTK half-life (t1/2) of 87 hours in the rat blood
and 5 to 8 hours in the spleen. BTK occupancy in the spleen was not affected by the route of
administration (subcutaneous or by mouth).
In Vivo Models Relevant to CSU
In a mouse reverse passive Arthus model of a local type III hypersensitivity reaction (Study RD2014-00813) using oral doses of 3, 10, 30, and 100 mg/kg of remibrutinib administered prior to
induction of the Arthus response, remibrutinib pretreatment resulted in dose-dependent increases
in exposure that increased in a less than dose-proportional manner for 3 to 10 mg/kg but in a
greater than dose-proportional manner from 10 to 100 mg/kg. Dose-dependent increases in BTK
occupancy in the spleen were also evident. Reduced skin swelling in the mouse reverse passive
Arthus model was observed with 30- and 100-mg/kg remibrutinib. Inhibition of swelling
compared to vehicle was observed when remibrutinib was administered 2 to 16 hours prior to
induction of the Arthus response with a maximal response observed at 2 hours and returning to
vehicle control levels at 45 hours.
In a BALB/c mouse PCA model (Study RD-2014-00912) where mast cells are activated via the
FcεR by IgE and hapten-specific IgE, remibrutinib was administered at doses of 0 (vehicle), 3,
111
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
10, 30, and 100 mg/kg (by mouth) at two time points (4 hours after sensitization and 2 hours
prior to hapten challenge). Following low dose IgE sensitization, remibrutinib administration
inhibited PCA at all doses while a minimal effect was observed at high dose IgE sensitization for
the high dose of 100-mg/kg remibrutinib. However, comparable blood concentrations of
remibrutinib that were dose-proportional and comparable ~100% BTK occupancy in the spleen
were observed with either approach. These results suggest that remibrutinib was effective
following low-dose IgE sensitization in this model of anaphylaxis-driven skin edema relevant to
urticaria.
In Vivo Correlation of Remibrutinib Pharmacokinetics, Tissue BTK Occupancy, and
Mechanistic Pharmacodynamics
A model of B-cell response to SRBC in OFA rats (Study RD-2014-00858) was used to assess the
correlation of remibrutinib pharmacokinetics, tissue BTK occupancy, and mechanistic PD
efficacy. Remibrutinib was administered at daily doses of 0.3, 1, and 3 mg/kg for 3 days
following immunization with SRBC. Following dosing, remibrutinib concentrations in the blood
increased in a dose-proportional manner and were highest at 0.5 hours postdose with <10%
remaining at 5 hours postdose. Dose dependent increases in BTK occupancy in the spleen were
evident with increasing doses (31 to 71.6%). Remibrutinib dose-dependently decreased the
number of SRBC-specific IgM secreting B-cells by 66 to 96%.

DMPK R2300145:

DMPK R1400850:

These findings suggest that remibrutinib exhibits high passive permeability at
higher concentrations, while at lower concentrations, its permeability is
reduced due to an apically directed P-gp-mediated efflux process (apparent
Km of 13.8µM). The contributions of BCRP and MRP2 to this efflux process
appear to be negligible
Human hepatocyte uptake of remibrutinib (0.38µM) occurs mostly by a
passive permeation without modulation by a solute-carrier system. The
measured (nonsaturable, passive) membrane permeability for remibrutinib
was 47.3 μL/min/ mg protein and the maximum transporter-mediated
membrane permeability for remibrutinib was 0 μL/min/106 cells.
Inhibitors of OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT family, OCT family, NTCP, and
lysosomal trapping did not impact the permeation of remibrutinib into
hepatocytes.

The protein binding of [14C]-remibrutinib in human plasma at concentrations of
5, 50, 500, and 5000 ng/mL were 98, 95.3, 95.1, and 95.2%, respectively.

Remibrutinib contains a reactive acrylamide moiety by design. LTG751 is an
analog of remibrutinib where the reactive acrylamide has been inactivated
(double bond saturated).
The plasma protein binding of [14C]-remibrutinib at concentrations of 10, 100,
1000 and 10000 ng/mL was 94.4, 95.2, 95.5, and 96%, respectively.

Report/Objectives

Metabolism
DMPK R1500124:
Evaluation of [3H]remibrutinib oxidative
metabolite profile in
human liver
microsomes (HLM),
human hepatocytes,
liver cystosol, and S9
fractions and using
recombinant human
enzymes

Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
The mean in vitro human blood/plasma distribution of [3H]-remibrutinib and
[3H]-LTG751 for human were 0.813 (over a concentration range of 100 to
10,000 ng/mL) and 0.858 (over a concentration range of 10 to 1000 ng/mL),
respectively. At a concentration of 10 ng/mL, the blood/plasma distribution for
remibrutinib was 0.955.
CYP3A4 inhibitors ketoconazole and azamulin inhibited the metabolism of
remibrutinib up to 96% and 100% in HLM with an IC50 value of 0.04μmol/L
and 0.005μmol/L, respectively. The fraction metabolized (fm) by CYP3A4 was
estimated to be 0.95.
CYP3A4 mainly formed metabolites M24 (dihydrodiol), M22 (O-dealkylation;
LTP763), and M28 (demethylation). Metabolite M28 was also formed by
CYP2C8 (6.3%), CYP2B6 (2.9%), CYP2C9 (1.4%) and CYP3A5 (1.8%).
CYP1A1 and CYP2C19 formed a single metabolite (M15; C-hydroxylation)
with an abundance >30%.
[3H]-remibrutinib at 1μmol/L incubated with HLM at 1 mg/mL was extensively
metabolized (83%), where M24 (19%), M28 (14%), LTP763 (8%), M23 (7%),
M29 (7%) and M30 (6%) were the most abundant metabolites. These
metabolites were the predominant oxidative metabolites formed by liver S9
fraction as well. Liver cytosolic enzymes were not found to have a major
impact on the metabolic clearance of remibrutinib.
[3H]-remibrutinib at 10μmol/L incubated in human cryopreserved hepatocytes
was metabolized (50%) to M31 (11%), M24 (8.8%), M15 (6.1%), M29 (4%)
and LTP763 (3%). The M28 metabolite (the major metabolite in HLM) was no
longer detectable in hepatocytes after 3 hours.
CYP3A4 is the predominant enzyme in the hepatic oxidative metabolism of
remibrutinib with minor contribution from CYP1A1 and CYP2C19.

DMPK R1600559:
Identifying the human

remibrutinib in human

by recombinant

Recombinant microsomes expressing CYP3A4 or CYP1A1 showed the
highest metabolic activities of [3H]-remibrutinib at a concentration of 3µM.
Metabolic activities were 29.43% (12.24% M28 metabolite) with CYP3A4, and
23.08% (21.92% M15 metabolite) with CYP1A1. The metabolic activity of
CYP2C19 was 2.19%. Low metabolic activity was observed with other
recombinant microsomes expressing other CYP and flavin-containing
monooxygenase (FMO) enzymes. The metabolic activity of HLM with [3H]remibrutinib 3µM was 11.06%.
After accounting for the relative abundance in human liver microsomes,
CYP3A4/5 is the major enzyme involved in the hepatic oxidative metabolism
of [3H]-remibrutinib.

DMPK R1300944:
Evaluating the
induction of CYP1A2,
CYP2B6, CYP2C9
and CYP3A4
(activities and mRNA)
by remibrutinib

Remibrutinib was an inhibitor of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9,
CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4/5.
In human liver microsomes, time-dependent inhibition of CYP3A4/5 by
remibrutinib was observed in the presence of NADPH (KI =8.8μM,
KI,u =6.9μM, kinact =0.0102 min-1).
No time-dependent inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9,
CYP2C19 and CYP2D6 by remibrutinib was observed at concentrations up to
100μM.
Remibrutinib is a time-dependent inhibitor of CYP3A4/5.
In primary human hepatocytes, remibrutinib at 10, 20, 30 and 50μM increased
CYP2B6 mRNA expression by 2.2-, 3.2-, 4-, and 6.9-fold, respectively, and
enzyme activity by 2.8-, 3.78-, 3.61-, and 5.53-fold, respectively. The EC50
value for half-maximal induction was 25µM for CYP2B6.
Remibrutinib at 1, 5, 10, 20, 30 and 50μM did not increase CYP2C9 mRNA
expression by >2-fold, but increased the enzyme activity by 2.4-, 2.87-, 2.2-,
2.65-, 5.25-, and 3.43-fold, respectively.
Remibrutinib at 5, 10, 20, 30 and 50μM increased CYP3A4 mRNA expression
by 3.4-, 9.7-, 27.7-, 45.6-, 104.6-fold, respectively, and enzyme activity by
1.06-, 1.42-, 1.78-, 2.14-, 2.05-fold, respectively. The EC50 was 33µM for
CYP3A4.
The mRNA expression and activity of CYP1A2 were not >2-fold across the
studied remibrutinib concentration range (up to 50µM).

DMPK R1600703:
Evaluating the
induction of CYP1A2,
CYP2B6, CYP2C9
and CYP3A4
(activities and mRNA)
by remibrutinib

Based on the in vitro data using cryopreserved human hepatocytes from a
single donor, remibrutinib may induce CYP2B6, CYP2C9 and CYP3A.
remibrutinib is not expected to induce CYP1A2.
The potential for remibrutinib at concentrations up to 10µM to induce CYP
enzymes was determined in primary human hepatocytes derived from three
individual donors. Note that incubation of remibrutinib with hepatocytes for 24
hours showed a time-dependent decrease of remibrutinib concentrations,
likely due to metabolism. Thus, induction effects may be underestimated.

Report/Objectives

Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
CYP1A2:
Remibrutinib (≥2.5μM) showed dose-dependent induction (>2-fold) of
CYP1A2 gene expression in all three donors. The average EC50 and Emax for
fold-induction were 4.55µM and 5.47, respectively.
More than 2-fold increase in enzyme activity in Donor 1 was observed at
remibrutinib 10μM. Donor 2 showed no increase in enzyme activity. Dosedependent induction was observed in Donor 3, with >2-fold induction
observed at concentrations ≥0.63μM with maximum induction of 8.33-fold
observed at remibrutinib 5μM.
CYP2B6:
Remibrutinib (≥5μM) showed dose-dependent induction (>2-fold) of CYP2B6
gene expression in all three donors. The average EC50 and Emax for foldinduction were 5.47µM and 5.45, respectively.
Donor 3 showed 2.17-fold (15.9% of the maximal response in the positive
control) increase in enzyme activity at remibrutinib 10μM. No induction of
enzyme activity was observed for Donors 1 and 2.
CYP2C9:
Remibrutinib (>10μM) showed dose-dependent induction (>2-fold) of
CYP2C9 gene expression in all three donors. The average EC50 and Emax for
fold-induction were 5.1µM and 3.02, respectively.
Donor 3 showed 3.08-fold (84% of the maximal response of positive control),
2.12-fold (45.2% of the maximal response of positive control), and 3.13-fold
(86% of the maximal response of positive control) increase in enzyme activity
at remibrutinib 2.5, 5 and 10μM, respectively. No induction of activity was
observed for Donors 1 and 2.
CYP3A4:
Remibrutinib (>0.31μM) showed dose-dependent induction (>2-fold) of
CYP3A4 gene expression in all three donors. The average EC50 and Emax for
fold-induction were 5.08µM and 28.08, respectively.
Donor 3 showed 1.98-fold (21.9% of the maximal response in the positive
control) increase in enzyme activity at remibrutinib 10μM. No induction of
enzyme activity was observed for Donors 1 and 2.

DMPK R2301196:
Evaluation of the
inhibitory potential of
remibrutinib on the
human BCRP and
MDR1

Remibrutinib at concentrations up to 10μM induced CYP1A2, CYP2B6,
CYP2C9, and CYP3A4 gene expression. Induction of catalytic activity for
CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C9 was only observed in one donor.
The inhibition potential of remibrutinib on BCRP and MDR1 (P-gp) was
determined using vesicular transport assays with inside-out membrane
vesicles prepared from HEK293 cells overexpressing BCRP or MDR1.
Remibrutinib showed significant loss due to nonspecific binding with an
average of 31.1% in transport buffer. Nominal concentrations were therefore
corrected by a factor of 0.689 at all concentrations.
Remibrutinib inhibited BCRP-mediated (E3S) accumulation by 100% at
concentrations ≥17.2μM. The calculated IC50 was 0.533μM, the calculated Ki
value was 0.467μM.

DMPK R1500199:
Evaluation of the

Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
Remibrutinib inhibited MDR1-mediated (N-methyl-quinidine) accumulation by
about 91% at the highest tested concentration of 34.5μM. The calculated IC50
was 0.513μM, the calculated Ki value was 0.382μM.
Remibrutinib has the potential to inhibit BCRP and MDR1 efflux transporters
The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit BCRP and P-gp, and potential for
LTG751 to inhibit BCRP were evaluated in recombinant MDCKII and LLCPK1 cell overexpressing BCRP and P-gp, respectively. Note that nominal
concentrations were corrected for nonspecific binding to glass and plastic (up
to 37.4%).
Remibrutinib inhibited P-gp with IC50=29.4μM, Ki =29.4μM, and a maximal
inhibition of 101.7% (relative to positive control) and inhibited BCRP with an
IC50=4.3μM and a maximal inhibition of 118.3% (relative to positive control).
LTG751, an analog of remibrutinib in which the reactive acrylamide moiety
has been inactivated via saturation of the double bond, inhibited BCRP with
an IC50=3μM and a maximal inhibition of 106.6%.

DMPK R1500200:
Inhibitory effects of
remibrutinib and
LTG751 on
OATP1B1/1B3
transporters

Remibrutinib has the potential to inhibit BCRP and P-gp efflux transporters.
Based on inhibition of BCRP by LTG751, BCRP inhibition by remibrutinib is
not likely related to the irreversible-binding potential of remibrutinib
The potential for remibrutinib and LTG751 to inhibit OATP1B1/3 was
determined in HEK293 cells overexpressing OATP1B1/3. The maximum
inhibition of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 relative to positive control at the highest
remibrutinib concentration (66.6µM, measured) was 99.9% and 100.7%,
respectively.
The maximum inhibition relative to positive control of OATP1B1 and
OATP1B3 at the highest LTG751 concentration (100µM, nominal) was 99.4%
and 115.4%, respectively.
The IC50 values of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 inhibition were 2.9μM and 9μM
(for remibrutinib) and 5.2 and 8.4μM (for LTG751), respectively.
The Ki values of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 inhibition were 2.8μM and 8.8μM
(for remibrutinib) and 5.1 and 8.3μM (for LTG751), respectively.

DMPK R1500824:
Evaluating the
inhibitory effects of
remibrutinib on OAT1
and OAT3
transporters

Remibrutinib and LTG751 have the potential to inhibit hepatic OATP1B1 and
OATP1B3 uptake transporters. Based on LTG751 inhibition of OATP1B1 and
OATP1B3, inhibition by remibrutinib is not likely related to the irreversiblebinding potential of remibrutinib.
The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit OAT1 and OAT3 was determined in
HEK293 cells overexpressing OAT1 and OAT3. The IC50 and Ki values of
OAT1 inhibition were 39.4μM and 38.6μM, respectively. The IC50 and Ki
values of OAT3 inhibition were 14.1μM and 10.6μM, respectively.
The maximum inhibition of OAT1 and OAT3 relative to positive control at the
highest remibrutinib concentration (66.6µM, measured) was 71.2% and
85.3%, respectively.
Remibrutinib has the potential to inhibit renal OAT1 and OAT3 uptake
transporters.

DMPK R1700782:
Evaluating the
inhibitory potential of
remibrutinib on BSEP,
MRP2, MATE1,
MATE2-K, OCT1 and
OCT2

Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit BSEP and MRP2 were determined in
vesicular transport inhibition assays with inside-out membrane vesicles
prepared from HEK293 cells overexpressing BSEP or MRP2. The potential
for remibrutinib to inhibit MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1, and OCT2 were
determined in uptake transporter inhibition assays using HEK293 cells stably
expressing the respective uptake transporter.
Relative to control, the maximum inhibition at the highest remibrutinib
concentrations was 97%, 29%, 88%, 56%, 97%, and 51% for BSEP, MRP2,
MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1, and OCT2, respectively. The highest nominal
remibrutinib concentrations tested in inhibition assays for BSEP, MRP2,
MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1, and OCT2 were 50, 100, 75, 75, 75, and 75µM,
respectively.
The IC50 for transport inhibition was 8.69, 14.4, 68.8, 7.21, and 68.92μM for
BSEP, MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1, and OCT2, respectively.
The Ki for transport inhibition was 7.8, 13.6, 68.3, 7.19, and 68.7μM for BSEP,
MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT, and OCT2, respectively.
The IC50 and Ki for MRP2 inhibition were not determined.

DMPK R2000485:
Evaluating the
inhibitory potential of
remibrutinib on
MATE1, MATE2-K,
OAT1, OAT3,
OATP1B1, OATP1B3,
OCT1 and OCT2
transporters

Remibrutinib is an inhibitor of BSEP, OCT1, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2-K
The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3,
OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, and OCT2 were determined in uptake
transporter inhibition assays using HEK293 (OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1,
OATP1B3, OCT1, OCT2) or MDCKII (MATE1, MATE2-K) cells stably
expressing the respective uptake transporter.
Nonspecific binding of remibrutinib was 67.4% in HBSS pH7.4 media
(correction factor 0.326 was used for nominal concentrations used for OAT1,
OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 assays) and 70% in KH pH 8
media (correction factor of 0.296 was used for nominal concentrations used
for MATE1 and MATE2-K assays).
Relative to control, the inhibition at the highest remibrutinib measured
concentrations (7.4μM for MATE1 and MATE2-K and 8.15μM for the other
tested transporters) was 70%, 32%, 41%, 90%, 83%, 44%, 88%, and 44% for
MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2,
respectively.
The IC50 values for transporter inhibition were 1.71, 1.12, 2.62, and 1.1μM,
and Ki was 1.7, 1.09, 2.36, 1.09μM for MATE1, OAT3, OATP1B1 and OCT1,
respectively.
The IC50 for OAT1, OATP1B3 and OCT2 was estimated to be 9.97, 7.81, and
9.41μM and Ki was estimated to be 9.87, 7.55, and 9.41μM. Values were
estimated because the maximum inhibition at the highest remibrutinib tested
concentration was <50%. The IC50 and Ki for MATE2-K inhibition were not
determined.
Remibrutinib has the potential to inhibit MATE1, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1,
OATP1B3, OCT1, and OCT2.

Investigating the
inhibition potential of

Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit NTCP was determined in uptake
inhibition assays using HEK293 cells expressing NTCP. The remibrutinib
nominal concentrations were corrected by specific factors based on
nonspecific binding assays.
Remibrutinib at concentrations up to 58.7μM inhibited NTCP by 33%. The
IC50 and Ki are >58.7μM.
Remibrutinib inhibited NTCP by less than 50% at the highest test
concentrations.

14.2.1. Single-Dose, Multiple-Dose, and Food-Effect
Studies in Healthy Subjects
14.2.1.1. Study X2101
Study X2101 was a six-part, first-in-human study of remibrutinib using the hard gelatin capsule
(HGC) drug product. The overall design of this study is illustrated in Figure 16.


Part 1 was randomized (6:2, remibrutinib:placebo), double-blind, placebo-controlled to
evaluate the pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of single doses of remibrutinib in
healthy subjects. Doses of 0.5, 1.5, 5, 15, 30, 60, 100, 200, 400, and 600 mg were evaluated.
Blood samples for PK assessment were collected at predose and up to 168 hours postdose.



Parts 2 and 4 were randomized (6:2, remibrutinib:placebo), double-blind, placebo-controlled
to evaluate the pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of multiple doses of remibrutinib in
healthy subjects with asymptomatic atopic diathesis. Part 2 was multiple-ascending dose
(MAD) given as 13 doses over 12 days and Part 4 was MAD given as 25 doses over 12 days.
Dosages in Part 2 were 10, 25, 50, 100, 400, and 600 mg QD. Dosages in Part 4 were 100
and 200 mg BID. Blood PK sampling timepoints for Parts 2 and 4 were at predose (Day 1, 2,
3, 5, 7, and 12) and up to 24 hours after first dose and up to 168 hours after the last dose on
Day 12.

125
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)


Part 3 was a single-dose, open-label, crossover, food-effect study with 18 days washout
period between two single 60-mg doses of remibrutinib in healthy subjects. On Day 1,
following an overnight fast of at least 10 hours, subjects were given a high-calorie, high-fat
meal (i.e., breakfast consisting of two eggs fried in butter, two strips of bacon, two slices of
toast with butter, 4 oz [100 g] of hash brown potatoes, 8 fl. oz [about 240 mL] of whole
milk). Subjects were instructed to consume the breakfast within 30 minutes and to administer
remibrutinib within 5 minutes after completion of the meal. Blood samples for PK
assessment were collected at predose and up to 96 hours postdose.



Part 5 was a double-blind, single-dose, two-sequence, relative bioavailability study to
compare the pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of remibrutinib after administration of
(b) (4) or
a single oral dose of 50 mg of the capsule drug product containing either
(b) (4) drug substance in healthy subjects. Blood samples for PK assessment were
collected at predose and up to 48 hours postdose. The relative bioavailability of the (b) (4)
(b) (4) drug substance compared to the
(b) (4) drug substance is not relevant for
the current review. Therefore, Part 5 will not be discussed in this review.



Part 6 was a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, 4-week multiple-dose study of
remibrutinib 100 mg BID in subjects with atopic dermatitis. As atopic dermatitis is not a
proposed indication for remibrutinib, Part 6 will not be discussed in this review.

Pharmacokinetic Results
Part 1: Single-Ascending Dose
Following single-dose administration, remibrutinib exhibited rapid absorption with median Tmax
values ranging from 0.5 to 1.25 hours across all dose levels. At lower doses (<100 mg), the blood
concentrations declined below the lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) of 1 ng/mL before
entering the terminal phase (Figure 17). At the lowest doses of 0.5, 1.5, and 5 mg, t1/2 could not
be estimated. The mean t1/2 ranged from 0.5 to 3.2 hours across the dose levels of 15 to 200 mg.
At higher doses, a longer half-life was observed. Specifically, at 600 mg of remibrutinib, the
terminal t1/2 extended to 14.1 hours (Table 40). Both the Cmax and the AUC increased
nonproportionally with escalating doses throughout the range from 0.5 mg to 600 mg. For
instance, a 20-fold increase in dose from 30 mg to 600 mg resulted in only a 7.6-fold increase in
Cmax and a 15-fold increase in AUClast. Full dose-linearity is not anticipated due to timedependent clearance, attributed to formation of a covalent bond between remibrutinib and BTK.
Based on a linear regression analysis of log-transformed parameters, over the dose range of 30 to
200-mg single dose, the increase in Cmax was less than dose-proportional (slope 90% CI: 0.68
[0.52, 0.85]), the increase in AUClast and AUC0-inf was approximately dose-proportional and less
than dose-proportional, respectively (slope 90% CI: 0.94 [0.78, 1.11] for AUClast and 0.90: [0.75,
1.05] for AUC0-inf; data derived from Table 11-2, Interim clinical study report for Study X2101).
The apparent volume of distribution increases with higher doses of remibrutinib, which may be
due to saturable covalent binding of remibrutinib to BTK.

Part 2: MAD
The summary statistics of the remibrutinib PK parameter values for Part 2 are presented in Table
41. Analogous to Part 1, the terminal elimination phase following the final dose was discernible
only at higher doses (i.e., exceeding 100 mg) (Figure 18). Accumulation was observed with an
increase observed in both Cmax and AUC from Day 1 to Day 12. This may be attributed to the
covalent binding of remibrutinib to BTK. However, the contribution of target-mediated clearance
to the overall clearance of remibrutinib is more pronounced at lower doses. At doses of 400 mg
and 600 mg, accumulation becomes negligible. For instance, the exposure difference, as
measured by AUC0-24h, was 4.86-fold between Day 1 (AUC0-24h =4.94 ng*h/mL) and Day 12
(AUC0-24h =24 ng*h/mL) at a dosage of 10 mg QD. The accumulation ratio diminishes to 1.14fold at 600 mg (Day 1 AUC0-24h of 1080 ng*h/mL and Day 12 AUC0-24h of 1230 ng*h/mL),
likely due to saturation of target binding. Consequently, remibrutinib exhibits both time and
dose-dependent clearance.
Similar to Part 1, Cmax and AUC increased in a nonproportional manner with escalating doses
across the entire range from 10 mg to 600 mg. Based on linear regression analysis with logtransformed parameters, over the dose range of 10 to 100 mg, the increase in Cmax, AUClast, and
AUC0-24h was less than dose-proportional with slope (90% CI) values of 0.64 (0.55, 0.74), 0.81
(0.69, 0.93), and 0.80 (0.68, 0.91), respectively.

Part 4
The summary statistics of the remibrutinib pharmacokinetic parameter values for Part 4,
encompassing doses of 100 mg and 200 mg administered BID, are presented in Table 42. A lessthan-dose-proportional increase in both Cmax and AUC was observed when comparing the 100mg BID and 200-mg BID doses. From Day 1 to Day 12, the accumulation ratios for Cmax were
1.7 and 1.5 at the 100-mg and 200-mg BID dosages, respectively. The accumulation ratios for
AUC0-12h were 1.5 and 1.9 for 100 mg and 200 mg, respectively. Notably, at a dose of 100 mg, a
similar accumulation ratio of 1.5 was observed for AUCtau (0 to 24 for QD, 0 to 12 for BID)
irrespective of whether remibrutinib was administered QD or BID. The data support that

Part 3: Food Effect
The blood concentration-time profiles for the treatment arms involving remibrutinib at a dose of
60 mg, under both fasted and fed conditions, are illustrated in Figure 19. The administration of a
high-fat breakfast resulted in a 2-hour delay in the absorption of remibrutinib, with the Tmax
increasing from 0.767 hours under fasted conditions to 2.78 hours under fed conditions.
Additionally, the AUClast was augmented by 30%, while Cmax was reduced by 20%. The
statistical analysis of the food effect on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib is detailed in Table
43.

Pharmacodynamic Results
BTK Occupancy in Peripheral Blood
Part 1: Single-Ascending Dose Regimen
BTK occupancy exceeding 95% was observed at a dose level of 30 mg and above following a
single-dose administration of remibrutinib. This level of occupancy was sustained before
beginning to decline 48 hours postadministration (Figure 20).
Part 2: Multiple-Dose QD Regimen
In the multiple-dose QD regimens, the BTK occupancy on Day 1 was comparable to that
observed in the single-ascending dose (SAD) regimen. By Day 12, BTK occupancy exceeded
95% across all tested dose levels, ranging from 10 mg to 400 mg. This occupancy began to
decline 48 hours after the final dose on Day 12 (Figure 20).
Part 4: Multiple-Dose BID Regimen
In Part 4 of the study, near-complete BTK occupancy was achieved at both the 100-mg and 200mg doses in the multiple-dose BID regimens. This occupancy persisted until 48 hours after the
last dose on Day 12 (Figure 20).

Ex Vivo Activation of CD203c and CD63
Ex vivo inhibition of basophil activation was assessed by measuring the surface expression of
cell-surface markers CD203c and CD63.
Part 1
The inhibition of CD203c activation exceeded 50% at a dose of 60 mg and above. The peak
inhibition of stimulated basophils (79.3% inhibition) was observed 8 hours following
administration of 200 mg on Day 1 (Figure 21). A more pronounced and sustained inhibition of
CD63 activation was evident at doses ≥60 mg. At doses of 100 mg and higher, the inhibition of
CD63 activation was maintained at or above 88% for up to 48 hours post-treatment (Figure 21).

Parts 2 and 4
On Day 1, the highest percentage inhibition of CD203c activation was observed 8 hours postdose

The concentration ratios of 6βOH-cortisol to cortisol were measured in urine to probe for
modulation of intrinsic CYP3A4 activity. There was no dose-dependent or time-dependent
increase in the ratio, suggesting that remibrutinib may not modulate CYP3A4 activity (Figure
22).

14.2.1.2. Study X1101
Study X1101 was a randomized, placebo-controlled study designed to assess the safety,
tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of remibrutinib using SAD and MAD
regimens in healthy Japanese male subjects. The study was divided into Part A and Part B. Part
A comprised five cohorts, each receiving a single dose of remibrutinib at 5 mg, 30 mg, 100 mg,
200 mg, or 400 mg. Part B consisted of a single cohort that was administered remibrutinib at
100 mg BID for 5 days. Remibrutinib was administered as 5-mg and 50-mg HGCs.
Pharmacokinetic Results
Part A: SAD
Remibrutinib exhibited rapid absorption, with Tmax occurring between 0.5 and 1.5 hours across
all doses (Figure 23 and Table 44). Based on a linear regression analysis of log-transformed
parameters, across the dose range of 30 to 400 mg, Cmax increased in an approximately doseproportional manner (slope 90% CI: 0.99 [0.80, 1.18]) and AUClast increased in a greater than
dose-proportional manner (slope 90% CI: 1.16 [1.00, 1.32]). The terminal t1/2 ranged from 0.841
to 24.3 hours, with longer t1/2 observed at higher doses. The apparent terminal elimination phase
was not fully established until 12 hours postdose and was only measurable at doses exceeding
100 mg. Urine samples indicated that the amount of remibrutinib excreted in urine over 72 hours
136

Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
following a single 100-mg dose was 0.345 mg, with a renal clearance (CLr) of 1.12 L/h (0.28%
of total apparent clearance).

14.2.2. [14C]-Labelled Absorption, Metabolism, and
Excretion Study, Study X2104
Study X2104 was a single-center, open-label study conducted in seven healthy subjects to
evaluate the ADME of remibrutinib under fasted conditions. The study comprised three distinct
arms: an oral arm and two IV arms (A and B). As recovery of total radioactivity from the first
four subjects in the oral arm was deemed incomplete (~60%), an assessment of exhaled air was
introduced for the fifth subject in the oral arm. As recovery of radioactivity did not improve after
accounting for exhaled air, an additional cohort of two subjects was enrolled in which
radiolabeled remibrutinib was administered IV to ensure receipt of the complete dose.


Oral arm (N=5): On Day 1, subjects received 100 mg of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib BID
as the HGC drug product. On the morning of Day 2, subjects were administered a single oral
dose of an oral solution containing 100 mg of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib and 0.074 MBq
(2 μCi) of [14C]-remibrutinib. To maintain steady-state conditions, the administration of
nonradiolabeled remibrutinib 100-mg BID capsules was resumed in the evening of Day 2 and
continued until the evening of Day 5.



IV Arm A (N=1): On Day 1, the subject received nonradiolabeled 100 mg of remibrutinib
HGC BID. On the morning of Day 2, 1 hour after the oral morning dose of 100 mg of
nonradiolabeled remibrutinib, the subject received a single IV microdose of 100 μg and
0.074 MBq (2 μCi) of [14C]-remibrutinib. To maintain steady-state conditions, the
administration of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib 100-mg BID capsules was resumed in the
evening of Day 2 and continued until the evening of Day 5.



IV Arm B (N=1): One subject received nonradiolabeled remibrutinib HGC at a dose of
100 mg BID for 5 consecutive days. On the morning of Day 6, 1 hour after the oral morning
dose of 100 mg of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib, the subject received a single IV microdose
of 100 μg and 0.074 MBq (2 μCi) of [14C]-remibrutinib. To maintain steady-state conditions,
the administration of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib 100-mg BID capsules was resumed in the
evening of Day 6 and continued until the evening of Day 9.

Pharmacokinetic parameters for total radioactivity in plasma and whole blood were derived from
the concentration-time data for total radioactivity. Urinary, fecal, and exhaled air recovery, as
well as total recovery, were calculated. Concentrations below the LLOQ were reported as “zero,”
and missing data points were explicitly labeled as such.
PK Mass Balance Results
Oral Arm
The cumulative excretions of total radioactivity in urine, feces, and total recovery are presented
in Figure 27 and Table 46. The recovery of total radioactivity was incomplete, amounting to only
56.2% of the administered dose. The reasons for incomplete recovery of radioactivity are
unclear. Of the excreted radioactivity, 31.8% was recovered in feces, with 24.9% recovered in
the feces within the first 72 hours. In urine, 24.4% of the total radioactivity was recovered, with
20.7% recovered in urine within the first 24 hours. The percentage of unchanged remibrutinib
141
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
recovered in urine was 4.1% of the dose, suggesting that a significant portion of the total
radioactivity recovered in urine consists of metabolites. Exhaled air was evaluated in one subject.
No radioactivity was detected in exhaled air.
The Applicant attributed the incomplete recovery of radioactivity to technical issues, supported
by the following observations:


The metabolic profiles in blood, urine, and feces obtained after oral and intravenous
administration were comparable.



The relative contributions of clearance pathways were also comparable between oral and IV
administration.



No long-lasting radioactivity was detected in blood or excreta.



No volatile radioactivity was detected in exhaled air after oral administration, which would
have suggested the formation of volatile metabolites or CO2.

IV Arm
The recovery of the administered dose of [14C]-remibrutinib was 96.8% in IV Arm A and 97.2%
in IV Arm B. Fecal excretion accounted for 67.1% of the dose in IV Arm A, with 57.4%
recovered within the first 72 hours. In IV Arm B, fecal excretion was 71.7%, with 65.7% of this
amount recovered within the first 48 hours (Figure 27 and Table 46). Urinary excretion
constituted 29.7% of the dose in IV Arm A and 25.5% in IV Arm B, the majority of which was
recovered within the first 12 to 24 hours. The percentage of unchanged remibrutinib recovered in
urine was 2.2% for IV Arm A and 5% for IV Arm B.
Note that as near-complete recovery of radioactivity was obtained after IV dosing, it is not likely
that the missing radioactivity after oral dosing was retained within the body. The data further
support that technical issues contributed to incomplete recovery of radioactivity after oral dosing.

Pharmacokinetics of Total Radioactivity in Plasma and Whole Blood
Oral Arm
Following administration of [14C]-remibrutinib, total radioactivity concentrations were
quantifiable in plasma for up to 312 to 600 hours and in whole blood for up to 216 to 408 hours
postdose. The AUC0-inf of total radioactivity in plasma was marginally higher than that in whole
blood (14900 h*ng-eq/mL versus 12700 h*ng-eq/mL) (Table 47 and Table 48, as well as Figure
28). The median elimination t1/2 of [14C]-remibrutinib was 250 hours in whole blood and 288
hours in plasma.
IV Arm
Total radioactivity concentrations were quantifiable in both plasma and whole blood up to 408
hours postdose. In IV Arm A and IV Arm B, the total radioactivity, as measured by AUC0-inf, in
whole blood and plasma were comparable (AUC0-inf 16.9 versus 18.5 h*ng-eq/mL, respectively,
in IV Arm A and 13.2 versus 12.8 h*ng-eq/mL, respectively, in IV Arm B), suggesting an equal
distribution of total radioactivity between plasma and red blood cells (Table 47, Table 48, and
Figure 28). The elimination t1/2 of total radioactivity in whole blood was 374 hours in IV Arm A
and 435 hours in IV Arm B. The elimination t1/2 of total radioactivity in plasma was 291 hours in
IV Arm A and 363 hours in IV Arm B.

and 2.06 hours in IV Arm B. A summary of the pharmacokinetic parameters of remibrutinib at
steady state in blood is presented in Table 49.

Metabolic Profile of Remibrutinib in Human Subjects
Metabolite patterns in blood, urine and feces were evaluated as AUC0-12h (IV) or AUC0-24h (oral)
in blood, for up to 48 hours in urine, and for up to 96 (IV) or 144 hours (oral) in feces. The pool
covering the period from 0 to 48 hours postdose in urine represented 95% of the mean total
radioactivity excreted in urine in the oral arm (~24% of the dose) and 98% of that in the IV arms
(~28% of the dose). The pool covering the period from 0 to 144 hours postdose in feces in the
oral arm represented 94% of the mean total radioactivity excreted in feces (~32% of the dose).
The pool covering the period from 24 to 96 hours postdose in feces in the IV arms represented
99% of the mean total radioactivity excreted in feces (~69% of the dose).
Oral Arm
Upon oral administration of 100-mg [14C]-remibrutinib, the parent remibrutinib was the
predominant species in blood, constituting 16.7% of the blood 14C-AUC0-24h (Table 50).
Metabolic profiling in healthy subjects revealed that remibrutinib is metabolized into 18 distinct
metabolites in blood, with none of these metabolites accounting for ≥10% of the total. The most
abundant metabolites, accounting for a total of 11.2% of blood AUC0-24h, were a mixture of two
diastereomers, M68a and M68b. As the diastereomers are present in a 3:1 ratio, neither
metabolite is considered major (i.e., accounts for >10% of total exposure). Remaining
metabolites accounted for ≤6.3% of blood AUC0-24h. The unchanged remibrutinib in excreta
represented 1.86% of the administered dose.

147
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
In urine, a total of 31 metabolites were identified, with metabolite M24 being the most abundant,
comprising 3.49% of the dose (refer to Table 6-7, Study X2104 clinical study report Amendment
3). The amount of unchanged remibrutinib excreted in urine was 0.65% of the dose, suggesting
negligible renal excretion of remibrutinib.
In feces, a total of 21 metabolites were detected, with M24 being the most prevalent,
representing 7.22% of the dose (refer to Table 6-7, Study X2104 clinical study report
Amendment 3). The amount of unchanged remibrutinib in feces was 1.21% of the dose,
suggesting near complete absorption of remibrutinib (Fa >98%, assuming the compound is stable
in the intestine) and extensive metabolism.

IV Arm
Following intravenous administration of 100-µg [14C]-remibrutinib, the parent compound
remibrutinib was the most abundant component in blood, constituting 21.7% of the blood 14CAUC0-12h. Thirteen metabolites were identified in the blood, with none representing ≥10% of the
total radioactivity (Table 51). Notably, metabolites M68a and M68b were detected and
accounted for 6.8% of total radioactive components.
A total of 27 metabolites were detected in urine, with none exceeding 10% of the total
radioactivity (refer to Table 6-8, Study X2104 clinical study report Amendment 3). The amount
of unchanged remibrutinib excreted in urine was 2.93% of the administered dose.
In feces, a total of 21 metabolites were characterized, with M24 being the most prevalent,
accounting for 16.9% of the dose. The remaining metabolites in feces each represented less than
10% of the dose (refer to Table 6-8, Study X2104 clinical study report Amendment 3).
Unchanged remibrutinib was not detected in feces, indicating that remibrutinib is primarily
eliminated through metabolic pathways.

14.2.3. Relative Bioavailability Study Comparing HGC
and FCT Drug Products, Study X2105
Study X2105 was a randomized, open-label, three-period study designed to evaluate the relative
bioavailability of two formulations of remibrutinib: a FCT and a HGC (Figure 29). The HGC
formulation was utilized in phase 1 and phase 2 clinical studies of remibrutinib. The selected
FCT variant (variant selected [VS]) was employed in phase 3 trials and was compared against
(b) (4)
the HGC formulation. The FCT (VS) contains remibrutinib that was
(b) (4) range as that in the HGC. Additionally, this study assessed the bioavailability of FCT (VS),
with a particle size of approximately (b) (4) nM, in comparison to an alternative FCT variant
(variant alternative [VA]), with a particle size of approximately (b) (4) nM. This comparison aimed
(b) (4) , could
to determine whether minor differences in the manufacturing process, specifically
influence the absorption rate and extent of remibrutinib.
All three remibrutinib formulations were administered to healthy adult subjects (aged 18 to 55
years) in the fasted state as a single oral dose of 100 mg (2x50 mg). Subjects were randomized
1:1 to one of two treatment sequences that varied the order of administering remibrutinib HGC
and FCT (VS). Pharmacokinetic samples were collected in each period at predose and at 0.25,
0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 8, 12, 24, 36, and 48 hours postdose. Bioequivalence between FCT (VS) and
HGC remibrutinib 100 mg was established if the least-squares geometric mean ratio and its 90%
CI fell within the predefined bioequivalence range of 80% to 125%. A washout period of 7 to 14
days separated each treatment period.

Statistical analysis of the pharmacokinetic parameters of remibrutinib revealed that the Cmax,
AUClast, and AUC0-inf of the remibrutinib FCT (VS) formulation were 28%, 16%, and 20%
lower, respectively, compared to those of the remibrutinib HGC formulation (Table 54 and Table
55). Additionally, the AUClast and AUC0-inf of the remibrutinib FCT (VS) formulation were
26.8% and 7.2% higher, respectively, while the Cmax was 4.6% lower compared to the
remibrutinib FCT (VA) formulation (Table 54 and Table 55). Notably, the elimination phase was
determined for only six subjects who received the FCT (VA) formulation. It was also noted that
for nine subjects who received the FCT (VA) formulation, the 48-hour postdose PK sample was
missing as blood collection tubes were not available. As a result, the AUClast determined for the
FCT (VA) formulation may have been underestimated, especially as blood concentrations at 48
hours postdose were quantifiable in the seven subjects from whom samples were collected. No
change in median Tmax was observed between the three formulations (about 1 hour for all
formulations).

Overall, the systemic exposure to remibrutinib following oral administration of 100 mg of the
FCT (VS) formulation was lower than that following administration of the HGC formulation.
However, high variability in the PK parameters was observed. The AUC of the FCT (VS)
formulation was greater than that observed with the FCT (VA) formulation. Results should be
interpreted with caution due to missing samples among subjects who received the FCT (VA)
formulation and inability to characterize the terminal elimination phase in most subjects.
Although the magnitude of effect is unclear, differences in particle size due to changes in the
manufacturing process do not appear to yield substantial differences in the systemic exposure.

14.2.4. Food-Effect Study Following Multiple Doses of
Remibrutinib, Study A02104
Study A02104 was a randomized, open-label, two-cohort, two-period, two-sequence, crossover
study designed to evaluate the effect of food on the steady-state pharmacokinetics of multiple
153
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
doses of remibrutinib FCT 25 mg and 100 mg in healthy Chinese adult subjects. Although the
exclusion criteria did not restrict the gender of enrolled subjects, only male subjects were
enrolled. The study design is illustrated in Figure 31. Subjects were randomized to one of two
cohorts divided by dose (i.e., 25 mg or 100 mg). Within each cohort, subjects were randomized
to one of two treatment sequences that varied the timing of prandial states. Remibrutinib was
administered BID from Days 1 to 5. On Day 6, only the morning dose was administered. The test
drug was provided as remibrutinib FCT 25 mg and 100 mg.
In the fed state, subjects received a high-fat, high-calorie breakfast (800 to 1000 calories with
150, 250, and 500 to 600 calories from protein, carbohydrate, and fat, respectively).
Remibrutinib was administered within 5 minutes after completion of the meal. For drug
administration on Day 4 under fed conditions, only the morning dose of remibrutinib FCT 25 mg
and 100 mg was taken after a standard high-fat breakfast. The evening dose was taken following
a fast of at least 2 hours. For fasted and fed administration, subjects fasted for at least 10 hours
before the morning dose (or the high-fat breakfast), and for at least 2 hours before the evening
dose.
PK samples were collected after the Day 4 morning dose and the Day 6 dose, with PK sampling
conducted up to 24 hours postdose.

Source: Study A02104 Clinical Report, Figure 9-1
Per Study A02104 protocol, the breakfast was high-fat (approximately 50% of total caloric content of the meal) and high-calorie
(approximately 800 to 1000 calories), derive approximately 150, 250, and 500 to 600 calories from protein, carbohydrate, and fat,
respectively.
Abbreviations: b.i.d., twice daily; D, day; EOS, end of study; N, number of subjects; PK, pharmacokinetic; q.d., once daily

Day 6 were pooled across treatment sequences.
