14.2.5. Hepatic Impairment Study A2101
Study A2101 was an open-label study designed to evaluate the pharmacokinetics, safety, and
tolerability of remibrutinib administered at a dose of 25 mg twice daily in adult subjects with
mild (Child-Pugh Class A, score 5 to 6), moderate (Child-Pugh Class A, score 7 to 9), and severe
(Child-Pugh Class A, score 10 to 15) HI compared to matched healthy adult subjects with normal
hepatic function. The study also aimed to assess the plasma protein binding (unbound fraction
[fu]) of remibrutinib in subjects with mild, moderate, or severe HI and in matched healthy control
subjects to investigate the potential impact of reduced plasma protein binding on the
pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib.
An exploratory objective of this study was to examine the relationship between remibrutinib PK
parameters and baseline measures of impaired hepatic function (e.g., prothrombin time, INR,
bilirubin, albumin, AST, and Child-Pugh score, as appropriate) using a linear regression model.
The study was conducted in two parts:


Part 1: Enrolled subjects with mild (Group 1) and moderate (Group 2) HI and matched
healthy control subjects with normal hepatic function (Group 4)



Part 2: Enrolled subjects with severe HI (Group 3) and matched healthy control subjects with
normal hepatic function (Group 4)

Each subject in the healthy control group was matched with respect to gender, age (±10 years),
and body weight (±20%) to at least one and up to three evaluable subjects in the HI groups. Each
control subject in Group 4 could be matched to any HI group (Groups 1, 2, and 3) but could not
be matched to more than one subject from the same HI group.
156
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Key inclusion criteria included males and females between 18 and 70 years of age (inclusive).
Subjects with an estimated glomerular filtration rate of <45 mL/min/1.73 m² were excluded from
Groups 1, 2, and 3 (i.e., HI groups).
All subjects received remibrutinib 25 mg (1x25 mg) BID orally under fasting conditions on Day
1 and Day 2, and a morning oral dose of remibrutinib 25 mg on Day 3 to achieve steady state.
The PK samples were collected on Day 3 at predose, and 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 48, and 72
hours postdose. Blood collection for the determination of unbound drug was performed at 1 hour
postdose on Day 3.
The PK parameters Cmax,ss,u, AUCtau,u, and AUClast,ss,u were calculated using unbound plasma
remibrutinib concentrations. The total plasma concentration of remibrutinib was calculated based
on the measured blood concentrations of remibrutinib and the blood-to-plasma partitioning ratio
of 0.955 determined at clinically relevant concentrations (refer to Section 14.1). The unbound
plasma concentrations were calculated from the total plasma concentration multiplied by the
fraction unbound determined from samples collected 1 hour postdose on Day 3.
Results
The study enrolled eight subjects with mild HI, eight subjects with moderate HI, seven subjects
with severe HI, and 15 matched controls with normal hepatic function. All subjects completed
the study. Among subjects with HI, the median (min, max) Child-Pugh scores were as follows:


Mild HI: 5 (5, 6)



Moderate HI: 7 (7, 8)



Severe HI: 10 (10, 11)

Whole Blood Remibrutinib Pharmacokinetics
The blood concentration-time profiles of remibrutinib following the administration of
remibrutinib 25 mg BID in subjects with normal hepatic function, and subjects with mild,
moderate, or severe HI are illustrated in Figure 33. The blood PK parameters of remibrutinib on
Day 3 for all study groups is presented in Table 58. The Cmax,ss values were elevated in all HI
groups (65, 57, and 60.8 ng/mL for mild, moderate, and severe HI subjects, respectively)
compared to the normal hepatic function group (30.1 ng/mL). Notably, Cmax,ss values were
comparable among the three HI groups. Similarly, the geometric means AUCtau and AUClast were
higher in all HI groups compared to the normal hepatic function group. Specifically, AUCtau
values were 60.5, 151, 147, and 204 ng·h/mL for subjects with normal hepatic function, and
subjects with mild, moderate, and severe HI, respectively (Table 58). The Tmax, ranging from
0.42 to 2 hours, was comparable across all hepatic function groups. It is noteworthy that the
arithmetic mean predose blood concentrations of remibrutinib on Day 3 were 0.539 ng/mL in
subjects with normal hepatic function and 2.41 ng/mL, 2.88 ng/mL, and 3.93 ng/mL in subjects
with mild, moderate, and severe HI, respectively. Furthermore, the terminal t1/2 appeared to be
prolonged, and the apparent oral clearance at steady state (CLss/F) and apparent volume of
distribution at steady state (Vss/F) were elevated in the HI groups compared to the normal hepatic
function group.

Unbound Remibrutinib Plasma Pharmacokinetics
The plasma protein binding of remibrutinib was determined by ultrafiltration. Radiolabeled
[14C]-remibrutinib was added ex vivo to clinical samples and the concentrations were determined
by liquid scintillation counting. The mean fu of remibrutinib, measured at the 1-hour postdose
time point on Day 3, was found to be comparable across subjects with normal hepatic function
(fu =0.0472) and subjects with mild (fu =0.0465), moderate (fu =0.0474), and severe (fu =0.0438)
HI. Similar to what was observed based on total blood pharmacokinetics, the PK parameters of
unbound remibrutinib in plasma, including Cmax,ss,u, AUCtau,u, and AUClast,ss,u, were elevated in
all HI groups compared to subjects with normal hepatic function (Table 60). Based on unbound
plasma remibrutinib concentrations, the geometric mean ratios for mild, moderate, and severe HI
compared to healthy controls were as follows: 1.9, 1.63, and 1.76 for Cmax,ss,u; 2.14, 2.0, and 2.78
for AUCtau,u; and 2.29, 2.2, and 3.09 for AUClast,ss,u, respectively (Table 61).

Relationship Between Remibrutinib Blood PK Parameters and Hepatic Function
Parameters
The associations between blood remibrutinib exposure and hepatic function parameters were
explored using a linear regression model to predict the natural logarithm-transformed AUClast,ss
based on logarithm-transformed baseline hepatic function measures. The hepatic function
measures included baseline prothrombin time, albumin, bilirubin, AST, and INR. The
exploratory analysis showed an apparent relationship between remibrutinib AUClast,ss and
different hepatic function measures (Figure 34 and Figure 35).

Impact of Concomitant Treatment
The Applicant restricted the enrollment of subjects on Legalon, a phytopharmaceutical
preparation derived from Silybum marianum (L.) (an extract of milk thistle). Per the European
Medicines Agency Committee on Herbal Medicinal Products, milk thistle fruit can be used to
support liver function. Notably, Study A2101 enrolled subjects from a single site in Hungary.
Thus, use of milk thistle products among subjects with HI was possible. The restriction on
Legalon usage in Study A2101 was implemented following an observation of elevated
remibrutinib exposure in subjects who coadministered remibrutinib with Legalon. Specifically,
(b) (6) ) with mild HI, two subjects (
(b) (6) ) with moderate HI, and
one subject (
(b)
(6)
two subjects
) with severe HI coadministered Legalon. Notably, subject
(b) (6) with mild HI showed significantly higher C
max and AUC of remibrutinib compared to
other subjects within the same group. One of the two subjects with moderate HI on Legalon
exhibited higher remibrutinib exposure compared to other subjects within the same HI group
(Figure 36). It is important to note that the exposure to remibrutinib in the two subjects with
severe HI and one subject with moderate HI fell within the range observed in other subjects
within their respective HI groups (Figure 36). The Applicant hypothesized that the increased
exposure to remibrutinib when coadministered with Legalon could be attributed to the inhibition
of CYP3A4 as remibrutinib was identified as a substrate of CYP3A4. Based on literature reports,
in vitro studies have indicated that milk thistle may decrease CYP3A4 activity by 50%
(Venkataramanan et al. 2000). However, clinical studies reported in the literature indicated that
silymarin products do not alter midazolam exposure (Gurley et al. 2006; Kawaguchi-Suzuki et
al. 2014).

Given the potential for milk thistle products to inhibit CYP3A4 activity, it is possible that the
true effect of HI on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib may have been overestimated. Elevated
remibrutinib exposure relative to remaining subjects in the respective HI group was only
observed in one subject with mild HI and one subject with moderate HI, both of whom were
concomitantly taking Legalon. Thus, conclusions for subjects with mild HI and subjects with
moderate HI may have been impacted. The magnitude of increase in Cmax, AUClast, and AUCtau
was higher in mild HI versus moderate HI when compared to subjects with normal hepatic
(b) (6)
function. This observation may be attributed to the higher exposure observed in Subject
in the mild HI group. For the remaining subjects on Legalon, although remibrutinib exposure
was comparable to that in other subjects with a similar HI severity, it is still possible that PK
parameters were elevated. However, given that there is conflicting evidence regarding the impact
of milk thistle products on CYP3A4 activity, the impact of HI on remibrutinib exposure will
consider data from all subjects irrespective of use of milk thistle products.

14.2.6. Drug-Drug Interaction
14.2.6.1. CYP3A4 Inhibitors (Ritonavir and Grapefruit
Juice), Study X2103
In vitro investigations demonstrated that the primary clearance pathway of remibrutinib is
oxidative metabolism mediated by CYP3A4. Study X2103 was designed as an open-label,
single-sequence, crossover study to assess the impact of ritonavir (RTV), a strong inhibitor of
both intestinal and hepatic CYP3A4/P-gp, and grapefruit juice (GFJ), a moderate to strong
inhibitor of intestinal CYP3A4, on the exposure to oral remibrutinib in healthy adult subjects
under fasted conditions. Secondary objectives of this study included the determination of the
fraction of oral remibrutinib that escapes gut metabolism (Fgut).
A detailed description of the study design is provided in Figure 37.
Period 1 (Reference Period)
Day 1 and 2 (Lead-In)
Single oral dose of remibrutinib 50 mg was administered on the morning of Day 1 and Day 2,
after an overnight fast of at least 10 hours. The remibrutinib doses on Day 1 and Day 2 were
given 24 hours apart.
Day 3
Subjects were given approximately 240 mL of 10% glucose (to mimic GFJ caloric content) after
an overnight fast of at least 9 hours. One hour later, a single oral dose of remibrutinib 50 mg was
administered, followed by IV bolus administration of 100 μg stable-isotope-labeled (SIL)remibrutinib 2 hours after the oral dose. Blood samples were collected for up to 24 hours for
remibrutinib and SIL-remibrutinib measurements.
Period 2 (GFJ Period)
Day 4 (Baseline and Lead-In)
Following baseline assessment, a single 50-mg oral remibrutinib dose was administered in the
morning of Day 4 after an overnight fast of at least 9 hours. GFJ was given orally to the subjects
15 hours postremibrutinib intake, on the evening of Day 4.
Day 5
A total of 240-mL GFJ was given in the morning after an overnight fast of at least 9 hours. A
single 20-mg oral remibrutinib dose was administered 1 hour later. Blood samples were collected
for up to 24 hours after the oral remibrutinib dosing, for remibrutinib measurements.

164
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Period 3 (RTV Period)
Day 6 (Baseline and Lead-In)
Following baseline assessment, a single 50-mg oral remibrutinib dose was administered in the
morning of Day 6 after an overnight fast of at least 9 hours. This was followed by a single 100mg oral dose of RTV 15 hours after the oral remibrutinib dose.
Day 7
RTV 100 mg was administered with approximately 240 mL 10% w/v glucose in water on the
morning of Day 7 after an overnight fast of at least 9 hours. One hour later, a single 20-mg oral
remibrutinib dose was administered. At 2 hours after the oral dose, subjects received IV bolus
administration of 100-μg SIL-remibrutinib. On the evening of Day 7, about 12 hours post the
morning RTV dose, another oral RTV 100-mg dose was given. Blood samples were collected up
to 48 hours for remibrutinib and SIL-remibrutinib measurements.
Day 8
An oral dose of RTV 100 mg was administered in the morning after an overnight fast of at least
9 hours followed by a second dose of RTV 100 mg 12 hours postmorning RTV dose.
The remibrutinib dose of 50 mg once daily was given orally in Period 1 and Period 2 to achieve
near-complete BTK receptor occupancy and steady-state conditions, thereby minimizing the
influence of target-mediated drug disposition on remibrutinib PK measurements. In the first-inhuman Study X2101, once-daily dosing of remibrutinib at 25 mg and above resulted in almost
complete BTK occupancy beginning on Day 1. This level of occupancy persisted with
administration of subsequent daily doses until about 48 hours after the last dose on Day 12 (refer
to Section 14.2.1.1). PK results from Study X2101 indicated that remibrutinib accumulates
following administration of multiple doses despite a short apparent half-life at doses ≤100 mg.
The reason for this accumulation is likely due to the influence of target binding on remibrutinib
disposition rather than incomplete clearance of remibrutinib prior to administration of the next
dose. Given the short apparent half-life, it is not expected that administration of higher doses
(i.e., 50 mg) will influence the pharmacokinetics following administration of the 20-mg dose
used to evaluate the impact of CYP3A4 inhibition. Therefore, it is reasonable for the Applicant
to administer lead-in doses of 50-mg remibrutinib prior to probing the impact of CYP3A4
inhibition on remibrutinib exposure.
RTV and GFJ are mechanism-based inhibitors of CYP3A4, exhibiting time- and concentrationdependent enzyme inactivation that is irreversible (Bailey et al. 2013; Loos et al. 2022).
Consequently, a complete washout (minimum of 2 to 3 weeks) of RTV or GFJ between
treatment periods is not feasible, as it would require the full de novo synthesis of the CYP3A
enzyme pool. A washout of 24 hours between all periods was selected to separate remibrutinib
dosing. The washout period is acceptable for clearance of remibrutinib prior to administration of
the next dose. As GFJ is expected to impact only intestinal CYP3A4, while RTV will impact
both intestinal and hepatic CYP3A4, the sequence of administration is reasonable. As an additive
effect between GFJ and RTV is not expected, full washout to accommodate resynthesis of the
CYP3A enzyme pool between the GFJ and RTV treatment periods is not required.

165
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
The dose of ritonavir to be used in the study is 100 mg administered twice daily. This dosage is
the same as that approved for use in combination with lopinavir for the treatment of HIV-1
infection (Kaletra) and the same as that approved for use in combination with nirmatrelvir for the
treatment of mild-to-moderate COVID-19 (Paxlovid). Notably, at this dosage, ritonavir is used to
inhibit CYP3A-mediated metabolism of the concomitant treatment.
Administration of SIL-remibrutinib as an IV bolus dose was intended to support determination of
the fractional contribution of CYP3A to overall clearance. The dose of SIL-remibrutinib of
100 µg is 0.5% of the lowest oral remibrutinib dose of 20 mg.
Remibrutinib for oral administration was supplied as HGCs in 20-mg and 50-mg strengths.
Remibrutinib for intravenous administration was supplied in vials containing SIL-remibrutinib (a
stable labeled isotope of remibrutinib) 100-μg powder for solution for injection.

Source: Study X2103 Clinical Report, Figure 9-1
On the morning of Day 3, subjects were administered 240 mL of 10% w/v glucose in water after at least 9 hours of fasting and oral
remibrutinib was administered thereafter 1 hour later.

9 hours of overnight fasting.

Results
DDI With Ritonavir and Grapefruit Juice
The blood concentration-time profiles and pharmacokinetic parameters for remibrutinib
following the administration of 20-mg and 50-mg doses with and without 100-mg RTV or 240mL GFJ, are illustrated in Figure 38 and summarized in Table 62. Dose-normalized PK
parameters were used to assess the impact of CYP3A4 inhibitors on the pharmacokinetics of
remibrutinib as 50-mg remibrutinib was administered in Treatment Period 1, while 20-mg
remibrutinib was administered in Treatment Periods 2 and 3. Based on Study X2101, following
administration of multiple once-daily doses of remibrutinib, exposure increased in a less than
dose-proportional manner between doses of 10 mg and 600 mg (refer to Section 14.2.1.1).
166
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Therefore, it is unclear whether comparing dose-normalized PK parameters will be appropriate.
Based on PK data from Study X2101, between dosages of 10 mg QD and 50 mg QD at steady
state, median Cmax appeared to increase in an approximately dose-proportional manner
(17.2 ng/mL and 100 ng/mL, respectively), while median AUClast increased in a greater than
dose-proportional manner (22.4 ng.h/mL and 179 ng.h/mL, respectively) (refer to Table 41).
However, between dosages of 25 mg QD and 50 mg QD, Cmax increased in a less than doseproportional manner, while AUClast increased in an approximately dose-proportional manner. On
the other hand, remibrutinib Cmax,ss and AUCtau increase in a dose-proportional manner between
dosages of 10 mg BID, 25 mg BID, and 100 mg BID in Study A2201 (see Section 14.2.7).
Specifically, the geometric mean Cmax,ss values following the administration of 10 mg BID,
25 mg BID, and 100 mg BID were 26.8, 56.6, and 188 ng/mL, respectively while geometric
mean AUCtau values were 65.1, 162, and 677 ng.h/mL, respectively. Therefore, the use of dosenormalized parameters is acceptable.
Median (min-max) Tmax values of remibrutinib when administered alone was 1 hour (0.52 hour to
2.5 hours), 0.73 hours (0.5 hours to 2 hours) when remibrutinib was coadministered with GFJ,
and 0.715 hours (0.5 hours to 1.5 hours) when remibrutinib was coadministered with RTV. The
mean elimination t1/2 was comparable for remibrutinib alone (6.58 hours) and when
coadministered with GFJ (7.13 hours) but was increased when remibrutinib was coadministered
with RTV (12.1 hours).
GFJ elevated the dose-normalized Cmax, AUClast, and AUC0-inf of orally administered
remibrutinib by 1.24-, 1.33- and 1.28-fold, respectively. RTV has a greater impact on
remibrutinib pharmacokinetics. Dose-normalized Cmax, AUClast, and AUC0-inf of orally
administered remibrutinib increased by 3.32-, 4.5-, and 4.31-fold, respectively (Table 63).

Pharmacokinetics of Intravenous SIL-Remibrutinib and Bioavailability of Remibrutinib
The blood concentration-time profiles and PK parameters of SIL-remibrutinib, following the
administration of a 100-µg IV dose with and without ritonavir, are presented in Figure 39 and
Table 64. The mean CL, t1/2, and Vc for intravenous remibrutinib were determined to be 70.1 L/h,
0.725 hours, and 72.4 L, respectively.
The mean dose-normalized pharmacokinetic parameters for SIL-remibrutinib, when
administered alone, were as follows: Cmax, 0.0389 ng/mL/mg; AUC0-24h, 14.5 h*ng/mL/mg;
AUClast, 14.3 h*ng/mL/mg; and AUC0-inf, 14.7 h*ng/mL/mg. When administered with ritonavir
on Day 7, the corresponding values were: Cmax, 0.0365 ng/mL/mg; AUC0-24h, 24.8 h*ng/mL/mg;
AUClast, 24.4 h*ng/mL/mg; and AUC0-inf, 25.3 h*ng/mL/mg.
Based on the dose-normalized AUC0-24h values for SIL-remibrutinib when administered alone
and in the presence of ritonavir, the estimated fraction of SIL-remibrutinib metabolized by
CYP3A4 (fm) was determined to be 0.42 (i.e., 1 – [14.5/24.8]).

Fraction of Remibrutinib Absorbed From the Gut
The Fgut was determined using dose-normalized AUC0-24h values. These values were
4.89 h*ng/mL/mg for remibrutinib administered alone and 6.28 h*ng/mL/mg when
coadministered with GFJ. The estimated Fgut value of 0.78 indicates a relatively lower
contribution of gut CYP3A4 to the metabolism of remibrutinib compared to hepatic CYP3A4.
14.2.6.2. CYP3A4 Inducers (Carbamazepine), P-gp
Substrates (Digoxin), OATP1B1 Substrates
(Coproporphyrin), and BCRP/OATP1B1 Substrate
(Rosuvastatin), Study A02103
Study A02103 was an open-label, two-part study to investigate the impact of carbamazepine (a
strong CYP3A4 inducer) 300 mg BID on the exposure to remibrutinib 100 mg BID, and the
impact of remibrutinib 100 mg BID on the exposure to digoxin (P-gp substrate) 0.25-mg singledose tablet and rosuvastatin (BCRP and OATP1B substrate) 10-mg single-dose tablet in healthy
adult subjects.
Part 1
Part 1 was a two-period, single-sequence study in which remibrutinib 100 mg (2x50 mg) BID
was administered alone (Period 1, Days 1 to 3), followed by coadministration of remibrutinib
100 mg (2x50 mg) BID (Period 2, Days 23 to 25) and the CYP3A4 inducer, carbamazepine
(Period 2, Days 4 to 25). Remibrutinib was administered BID on Days 1, 2, 23, and 24, and in
170
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
the morning only on Days 3 and 25. Remibrutinib was administered in Period 1 and 2 following
an overnight fast of at least 10 hours. On Days 1, 2, 23, and 24, subjects remained fasted 1 hour
postmorning dose and kept fasted for at least 1 hour prior to evening dose. On Days 3 and 25,
subjects remained fasted until 4 hours postmorning dose.
During the carbamazepine up-titration, subjects received oral doses of carbamazepine 100 mg
BID from Day 4 to Day 6, followed by carbamazepine 200 mg BID from Day 7 to Day 9, and
carbamazepine target maintenance dose of 300 mg BID from Day 10 to Day 25 (on Day 25,
morning dose only).
On Days 3 and 25, PK samples were collected up to 12 hours either alone (Day 3) or under
CYP3A4 induction by carbamazepine (Day 25). Remibrutinib PK blood concentration was also
measured on Day 24 at 1 hour postdose.
A comprehensive description of the study design and pharmacokinetic sampling times for Part 1
is illustrated in Figure 40.

within the range of approved doses per the approved labeling for carbamazepine (Carbatrol).
Administration of carbamazepine for 14 days prior to coadministration with remibrutinib is
acceptable to ensure sufficient time for CYP3A4 induction.
Part 2
Part 2 was a two-period, single-sequence study in which a drug cocktail containing digoxin and
rosuvastatin was administered alone (Period 1, Day 1), followed by coadministration of the same
cocktail (Period 2, Day 14) with remibrutinib (Period 2, Days 11 to 24). On Day 1, subjects

dose was administered on Day 24). On Day 14, subjects received a single dose of digoxin

mediated by OAT3 at the renal tubule level, was monitored to assess the inhibitory potential of
remibrutinib on OAT3 transport.

Part 1: Effect of Carbamazepine (Strong CYP3A4 Inducer) on Remibrutinib
The coadministration of remibrutinib at a dose of 100 mg BID with carbamazepine resulted in
significant reductions in the PK parameters of remibrutinib. Specifically, Cmax,ss, AUCtau, and
AUClast,ss were reduced by 74%, 77%, and 78%, respectively. Additionally, carbamazepine
decreased the CLss/F and Vss/F of remibrutinib from 1540 L/h to 362 L/h and from 2720 L to
1100 L, respectively (Table 66 and Figure 42). Notably, there was no significant impact on the
Tmax of remibrutinib.

Rosuvastatin Urine Pharmacokinetics
A summary of the PK data for rosuvastatin in urine, both in the presence and absence of
remibrutinib, is presented in Table 69. The geometric mean CLr of rosuvastatin exhibited a 6%
increase when coadministered with remibrutinib, rising from 10.0 L/h to 10.6 L/h. As renal
clearance of rosuvastatin is primarily mediated by OAT3, this observation suggests that
remibrutinib does not inhibit OAT3.

Part 2: Effect of Remibrutinib on Plasma Coproporphyrin I (OATP1B1 Substrate)
Rosuvastatin was employed as a substrate for BCRP and OATP1B to investigate potential drug
interactions. To distinguish between the inhibition of OATP1B and BCRP, changes in
coproporphyrin I, a potential endogenous biomarker of OATP1B1 activity, were measured in the
absence and presence of remibrutinib. The plasma concentration-time profiles and PK
parameters of coproporphyrin I following the administration of digoxin (0.25 mg) and
rosuvastatin (10 mg) with remibrutinib (100 mg BID) were compared to those following the
administration of digoxin (0.25 mg) and rosuvastatin (10 mg) alone. These data are presented in
Figure 45 and Table 70. Overall, Cmax of uncorrected coproporphyrin I increased by 2%, while
AUClast decreased by 2% in the presence of remibrutinib (Table 65). These findings suggest that
remibrutinib may not have the potential to inhibit the hepatic uptake transporter OATP1B1.

14.2.6.3. CYP3A4 Substrates (Oral Contraceptives and
Midazolam), CYP1A2 Substrates (Caffeine) and
CYP2C9 Substrates (Tolbutamide), Study X2102
In vitro studies have demonstrated that remibrutinib possesses the potential to both inhibit and
induce CYP3A4, CYP1A2, and CYP2C9. Study X2102 was designed as an open-label, threeperiod, single-sequence, crossover study in healthy female subjects of nonchild-bearing potential
to investigate the impact of remibrutinib 100 mg (administered as 1x100-mg HGC) BID for 18
days on the exposure to a single dose of an OC containing EE and LVG, which are substrates of
CYP3A and CYP2C. Additionally, the study examined the impact of remibrutinib on single oral
doses of midazolam (CYP3A4 substrate), caffeine (CYP1A2 substrate), and tolbutamide
(CYP2C9 substrate).
The study design is outlined as follows (Figure 46).
Period 1 (Day 1)
Subjects were administered a single dose of a probe cocktail containing 2 mg of midazolam,
200 mg of caffeine, and 500 mg of tolbutamide. This was followed by a washout period of at
least 72 hours and not exceeding 7 days.

181
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Period 2 (Day 9)
Subjects received a single oral dose of a combined OC containing 30 μg of EE and 150 μg of
LVG. This was followed by a washout period of at least 5 days and not exceeding 7 days.
Period 3
Subjects were administered daily doses of remibrutinib 100 mg BID for 18 days (Day 16 to Day
33). On Day 18 and Day 26, subjects received a single dose of the probe cocktail containing
2 mg of midazolam, 200 mg of caffeine, and 500 mg of tolbutamide approximately 30 minutes
after the morning dose of remibrutinib. On Day 30, subjects received a single oral dose of the
combined OC (EE 30 μg and LVG 150 μg) approximately 30 minutes after the morning dose of
remibrutinib.
The Applicant assumed that the within-subject variability in the PK parameters for EE and LVG
would be lower than the between-subject variability. Consequently, a lower sample size was
used for this single-sequence crossover design relative to the sample size that may have been
needed for a parallel group design.
Blood sampling timepoints were collected as follows:


Midazolam: Days 1, 18, and 26 (at predose and 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, and 24
hours postdose)



Tolbutamide: Days 1, 18, and 26 (at predose and 24 hours postdose)



Caffeine: Days 1, 18, and 26 (at predose and 6 hours postdose)



EE/LVG: Days 9 and 30 (at predose and 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.5, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, 48, 72,
and 96 hours postdose)



Remibrutinib: Days 16 and 17 (at predose and 6.5 hours postdose); Days 18 and 26 (at
predose and 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, and 24 hours postdose); Days 22 and 25 (at 6.5 hours
postdose); Day 30 (at predose and 6 hours postdose)

The Applicant utilized tolbutamide C24h and caffeine C6h to assess drug interactions between
remibrutinib and tolbutamide and caffeine. The Applicant’s rationale for measuring tolbutamide
and caffeine concentrations at a single time point is based on prior studies in the literature, which
have suggested that tolbutamide C24h levels may reflect CYP2C9 activity (Fuhr et al. 1996;
Jetter et al. 2004). Similarly, caffeine C6h levels may reflect CYP1A2 activity (Fuhr et al. 1996).
Additionally, the paraxanthine/caffeine C6h ratio may also reflect CYP1A2 activity (Fuhr et al.
1996).

182
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)

Impact of Remibrutinib on OC
The PK parameters of EE and LVG following a single dose of OC administered with and without
remibrutinib 100 mg twice daily are summarized in Table 72 and Table 73, respectively. The
coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with a single dose of OC resulted in a 1.28-fold
increase in Cmax, a 1.16-fold increase in AUC0-24h, and a 1.18-fold increase in AUC0-48h for EE.
For LVG, the increases were 1.36-fold in Cmax, 1.39-fold in AUC0-inf, 1.39-fold in AUC0-24h, and
1.41-fold for AUC0-48h (Table 71).
Despite these increases, the interaction between remibrutinib and OC does not indicate a
clinically significant impact on the safety and efficacy of OC when coadministered with
remibrutinib.

Impact of Remibrutinib on Midazolam (CYP3A4 Substrate)
The PK parameters of midazolam following the administration of a single dose of a probe
cocktail, with and without coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID, are summarized in
Table 74. On Day 18, the coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with a single dose of the
probe cocktail, which included midazolam 2 mg, resulted in a 1.36-fold increase in Cmax, a 1.45fold increase in AUC0-inf, and a 1.43-fold increase in AUC0-24h for midazolam compared to when
midazolam was administered alone. Similarly, on Day 26, the coadministration of remibrutinib
100 mg BID with a single dose of the probe cocktail led to a 1.27-fold increase in Cmax, a 1.43fold increase in AUC0-inf, and a 1.43-fold increase in AUC0-24h for midazolam compared to when
midazolam was administered alone.
In vitro studies have demonstrated that remibrutinib acts as a reversible and time-dependent
inhibitor and inducer of CYP3A4. Consequently, the net effect of remibrutinib on CYP3A4
function may exhibit time-dependent characteristics. For example, reversible inhibition is likely
to be more pronounced at the initiation of treatment, while the induction effect may be most
evident following multiple days of treatment. Based on the observed effects of remibrutinib on
midazolam exposure on Days 18 (3 days following the administration of remibrutinib) and 26
(11 days following administration or remibrutinib), remibrutinib has low potential to inhibit or
induce CYP3A4. Time-dependent changes in CYP3A4 activity were not observed as midazolam
exposure was comparable on Days 18 and 26.

Impact of Remibrutinib on Caffeine (CYP1A2 Substrate)
The C6h of caffeine and its metabolite paraxanthine showed no significant difference when the
probe cocktail was administered with or without remibrutinib 100 mg BID (Table 75). The
coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with 200 mg of caffeine, resulted in a 3% and 12%
decrease in the paraxanthine/caffeine C6h ratio on Days 18 and 26, respectively Table 71). These
findings suggest that multiple oral doses of remibrutinib 100 mg are unlikely to impact the
exposure to a single dose of caffeine.
Based on the observed effects of remibrutinib on the paraxanthine/caffeine C6h ratio on Days 18
and 26 (3 and 11 days, respectively, after the initiation of remibrutinib dosing), remibrutinib has
low potential to inhibit or induce CYP1A2.

Impact of Remibrutinib on Tolbutamide (CYP2C9 Substrate)
The C24h of tolbutamide of the probe cocktail showed no significant differences when
tolbutamide was administered with and without remibrutinib 100 mg BID (Table 76). The
coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with a single dose of tolbutamide 500 mg resulted
in 1.27- and 1.3-fold increases in tolbutamide C24h on Days 18 and 26, respectively Table 71).
While PK parameters such as Cmax and AUC are commonly used to evaluate drug interactions in
clinical studies, the Applicant utilized tolbutamide C24h to assess the drug interaction between
remibrutinib and tolbutamide. Studies in the literature suggest that tolbutamide C24h may reflect
CYP2C9 activity (Fuhr et al. 1996; Jetter et al. 2004). Jetter et al. (2004), proposed using the
natural logarithm of tolbutamide C24h as a metric for CYP2C9 activity because it is strongly
correlated with clearance. Tolbutamide C24h was found to be able to differentiate between
CYP2C9 *1/*1 and *1/*2 genotypes from *1/*3 and *2/*2 genotypes. The natural logarithm of
tolbutamide C24h for CYP2C9 *1/*3 and *2/*2 genotypes was approximately 2-fold higher than
that for CYP2C9 *1/*1. However, there was no significant difference between CYP2C9 *1/*1
and *1/*3 or *2/*2 genotypes based on Cmax, and only a 1.45-fold difference based on AUC0-inf.
Consequently, tolbutamide C24h may overestimate the magnitude of drug interaction between
remibrutinib and tolbutamide compared to that determined when using Cmax and AUC. As
multiple oral doses of remibrutinib 100 mg yielded an approximate 30% increase in tolbutamide
C24h, a lower magnitude may have been observed based on Cmax and AUC. This is further
supported by in vitro data. Although remibrutinib was identified as a CYP2C9 inhibitor in vitro,
an in vivo DDI risk is not predicted at clinical concentrations following administration of the
recommended dose.

26 (3 and 11 days, respectively, following the initiation of remibrutinib treatment), remibrutinib
has low potential to inhibit or induce CYP2C9.

14.2.7. Dose-Ranging in Subjects With CSU, Study
A2201
Study A2201 was a phase 2b, randomized, double-blind, parallel-group, placebo-controlled study
designed to evaluate the efficacy, safety, and tolerability of six different dosing regimens of oral
remibrutinib in adult subjects with CSU that remained inadequately controlled despite treatment
with second-generation H1-antihistamines. The study design and treatment arms are illustrated in
Figure 48. Subjects were randomized equally to one of six remibrutinib treatment arms or
placebo for 12 weeks. Dosages of remibrutinib ranged from 10 mg QD up to 100 mg BID.
Remibrutinib was administered in the form of hard gelatin capsules, available in strengths of
10 mg, 25 mg, and 50 mg. Randomization was stratified by prior exposure to anti-IgE biologics
(including omalizumab and ligelizumab), limited to 40% of the total study population, and by
geographical region. The primary objective of the study was to characterize the dose-response
relationship of remibrutinib administered once or twice daily in subjects with CSU with respect
to change from baseline in UAS7 at Week 4.
Figure 48. Study Design, Study A2201

Source: Study A2201 Clinical Report, Figure 9-1
1)
Background therapy was a second generation H1-antihistamine at a locally approved licensed posology that was administered
every day with a stable treatment regimen throughout the study (from Day −14 until Day 113).
2)
Rescue therapy was a second generation H1-antihistamine at a locally approved licensed posology that was eliminated primarily
via renal excretion (e.g., cetirizine, levocetirizine or bilastine). The rescue H1-antihistamine had to be different from the background
H1-antihistamine and could be given to treat unbearable symptoms (itch) of CSU on a day-to-day basis throughout the study (from
Day −14 until Day 113).
3)
H1-antihistamines used for background therapy and rescue therapy was not changed throughout the study.
remibrutinib 10 mg (Batch #1010020203, 1010018765, and 1010024908), remibrutinib 25 mg (Batch #1010020204, 1010018766,
and 1010024915), and remibrutinib 50 mg (Batch #1010020206, 1010020205, 1010018767, 1010019287, 1010024299,
1010024909, 1010024910) were used in Study A2201.
Abbreviations: BSL, baseline; CSU, chronic spontaneous urticaria; LOU064, remibrutinib; n, number of subjects; qd, once daily

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Efficacy and Dose-Response
Primary Endpoint (UAS7 Score Change From Baseline at Week 4)
The primary objective of this study was to establish a dose-response relationship and
characterize the dose-response curve for remibrutinib administered either QD or BID at doses of
10 mg, 35 mg, and 100 mg QD, and 10 mg, 25 mg, and 100 mg BID, in comparison to placebo.
The primary endpoint was the change from baseline in UAS7 at Week 4.
At the studied doses, a dose-response relationship was not observed. Based on the analysis of
changes in UAS7 scores from baseline at Week 4, all remibrutinib treatment arms demonstrated
greater reductions compared to the placebo (as detailed in and Table 77). The most substantial
LS mean treatment difference (90% CI; p-value) relative to placebo was observed in the
remibrutinib 25-mg BID arm (−14.58, 90% CI: −18.43, −10.73; nominal p<0.0001). Notably, the
LS means of treatment differences compared to placebo for the remibrutinib 10 mg QD and
30 mg QD arms were similar to those of the remibrutinib 25-mg BID arm. Specifically, the LS
mean treatment difference for the remibrutinib 10 mg QD arm was −13.66 (90% CI: −17.51,
−9.81; nominal p<0.0001), and for the remibrutinib 30 mg QD arm, it was −13.64 (90% CI:
−17.49, −9.78; nominal p<0.0001). These results indicate a flat dose-response relationship at
dosages of 10 mg QD and above.
Table 77. UAS7 Score Change From Baseline at Week 4 Estimated With a Mixed-Effect Repeated
Measurement Analysis of UAS7 Score Change From Baseline, Study A2201

Source: Study A2201 Clinical Report, Table 11-3
Baseline UAS7 score is comprised of the 7 days prior to Day 1 (Day −7 to Day −1).
MMRM: change from baseline in UAS7 score = treatment + baseline UAS7 score + prior exposure to anti-IgE biologics +
geographical region + week + baseline UAS7 score*week + treatment group*week.
Abbreviations: CI, confidence interval; LOU064, remibrutinib; LS, least squares; MMRM, mixed model for repeated measures;
N, number of subjects; n, number of subjects with observed value at the visit; q.d., once daily; SE, standard error; UAS7, weekly
urticaria activity score

Similar results were observed for the secondary endpoints of change from baseline to Week 12 in
UAS7, the proportion of subjects achieving a UAS7 score of 0 at Week 12, and the proportion of
subjects achieving a UAS7 score ≤6 at Week 12. Although all groups performed better relative to
placebo, the most pronounced change for all endpoints was observed with the 25-mg BID dosage
(refer to Tables 11-4 and 11-5, clinical study report for Study A2201). The change in UAS7 over
the treatment period was similar across all treatment groups (Figure 49).

14.2.8. Phase 3, Remibrutinib (25 mg BID) in Adult
Subjects With CSU, Trial A2301
Trial A2301 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study designed to establish the
efficacy, safety, and tolerability of remibrutinib 25 mg administered twice daily in adult subjects
with CSU inadequately controlled by H1-antihistamines, in comparison to a placebo. For full
details of the study design and efficacy results, refer to Section 6.
Sparse pharmacokinetic blood samples were collected at Week 2, Week 12, and Week 52, with
sampling limited to two time points at each visit to characterize peak exposures of remibrutinib
(45 minutes and 90 minutes postdose).
Pharmacokinetic Results
In the remibrutinib arm, the geometric mean coefficient of variation (CV%) blood concentrations
of remibrutinib at 45 minutes and 90 minutes postdose were as follows:


Week 2: 41 (142) ng/mL (N=266) and 36.4 (97) ng/mL (N=264)



Week 12: 39.4 (145) ng/mL (N=252) and 35.5 (97) ng/mL (N=253)



Week 52: 34.1 (149) ng/mL (N=219) and 32.4 (113) ng/mL (N=218), respectively

Remibrutinib concentrations remained consistent across all visits.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)

14.2.9. Phase 3, Remibrutinib (25 mg BID) in Adult
Subjects With CSU, Trial A2302
Trial A2302 mirrors the design and methodology of Trial A2301, with the additional inclusion of
a subpopulation of Chinese subjects to assess the exposure to remibrutinib. For full details on the
study design and efficacy results, refer to Section 6.
Pharmacokinetic Results
The pharmacokinetic analysis revealed that remibrutinib concentrations remained consistent,
across all visits, mirroring the findings from Trial A2301.
Sparse pharmacokinetic blood samples were collected at Week 2, Week 12, and Week 52, with
sampling limited to two time points at each visit to characterize peak exposures of remibrutinib
(45 minutes and 90 minutes postdose). The geometric mean (CV%) blood concentrations of
remibrutinib at 45 minutes and 90 minutes postdose were as follows:


Week 2: 41.3 (113) ng/mL (N=251) and 39 (91) ng/mL (N=250)



Week 12: 37.9 (154) ng/mL (N=237) and 38 (101) ng/mL (N=232)



Week 52: 37.2 (144) ng/mL (N=191) and 36 (76) ng/mL (N=193), respectively

Overall, the exposure to remibrutinib is comparable between Trials A2301 and A2302 with high
variability in PK data. Note that the primary difference between Trials A2301 and A2302 is the
inclusion of Chinese subjects with CSU in Trial A2302.

14.2.10. Phase 3, Remibrutinib (25 mg BID) in Adult
Japanese Subjects With CSU, Study A1301
Study A1301 was an open-label study designed to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and efficacy
of remibrutinib administered at a dose of 25 mg twice daily in adult Japanese subjects with CSU
who were inadequately controlled by H1-antihistamine therapy. The pharmacokinetic sampling
methodology employed in this study was the same as that used in Trials A2301 and A2302 (i.e.,
at 45 and 90 minutes postdose at Weeks 2, 12, and 52).
Pharmacokinetic Results
The geometric mean (CV%) blood concentrations of remibrutinib at 45 minutes and 90 minutes
postdose were as follows:


Week 2: 53.9 (96) ng/mL (N=70) and 44.6 (64) ng/mL (N=69)



Week 12: 50.8 (110) ng/mL (N=71) and 47.2 (60) ng/mL (N=71)



Week 52: 43.5 (150) ng/mL (N=68) and 45.7 (62) ng/mL (N=68), respectively

In comparison to Trials A2301 and A2302, the exposure to remibrutinib in Japanese subjects
with CSU in Study A1301 is slightly higher. However, a definitive conclusion on exposure
comparison can’t be made due to the observed high variability in PK data.

14.3.1.1. Quantification of Remibrutinib in Human Blood
A liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS) bioanalytical assay was
developed and validated for the quantification of remibrutinib concentrations in human blood
including methods DMPK R1600407, DMPK R1800081, and DMPK R1900112a. Method
DMPK R1800080 was developed and validated for the quantification of remibrutinib-[13C3,15N]
(i.e., SIL-remibrutinib) concentrations in human blood from Study X2103 (refer to
Section 14.2.6.1). A summary of the method validations for remibrutinib in blood is presented in
Table 80. The method validation for remibrutinib-[13C3,15N] in human blood is shown in Table
81. The methods were deemed acceptable for the determination of remibrutinib in human blood
samples.
For most studies (except for Studies X2104 and X2105), the number of samples used for
incurred sample reanalysis (ISR) fell below the number recommended in the guidance for
industry M10 Bioanalytical Method Validation and Study Sample Analysis (November 2022)
(i.e., 10% of the first 1000 samples plus 5% of the number of samples that exceed 1000).
Nevertheless, across studies, the ISR pass rate was at least 86%. In addition, for all trials with
samples analyzed using method 1800081, the total number of samples used for ISR and their
pass rates are adequate to meet the guidance-recommended criteria (refer to Table 2.2a in the
Applicant’s Information Request Response dated June 10, 2025). Given that Methods 1800081
(b) (4) ) and are based on the
and 1600407 were developed at the same laboratory (i.e.,
same assay principle (i.e., LC-MS/MS), the available data are adequate to establish reliability of
the bioanalytical methods.

remibrutinib in human urine samples.

14.3.3. Quantitation of Radiation From [14C]Remibrutinib in Human Blood, Plasma, Urine,
and Feces
Plasma and Whole Blood
The concentrations of total radioactivity in whole blood, plasma, urine, fecal and expired air
samples obtained from Study X2104 were determined using accelerator mass spectrometry
(AMS). Initially, urine and feces samples were analyzed using liquid scintillation counting
(LSC); when the radioactivity concentrations by LSC dropped below the LLOQ, AMS was used
to measure total radioactivity in urine and fecal samples.
207
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
A reference standard sucrose-8542 (C12H22O11) with a certified 14C/12C isotope ratio was used as
system suitability sample with 14C/12C ratio =1.506110-12 dpm/g, C =20.42, and Bq/g
C =0.3403786. The sucrose was always analyzed at the start of each run of samples (in
triplicate). When the measured 14C/12C ratio (average of at least 2) deviated >15% from the
certified ratio, the run was rejected, and the samples were reprocessed and reanalyzed.
Two AMS QC samples were included in triplicate in each analysis. These QC samples were
blank pooled plasma spiked with a known amount of 14C-paracetamol. Thereafter, the 14C/12C
ratio (nominal ratio) was determined using AMS analysis. The average 14C/12C ratio of the QC
samples in an analytical run must be within 85 to 115% of the nominal ratio. In addition, the CV
should be ≤15%. The QC results fulfilled the requirements for all analytical runs.
All homogenized feces samples were analyzed in triplicate; if the CV is >30%, the sample was
reanalyzed. All plasma, blood and urine samples were analyzed in duplicate; if the CV is >20%,
the sample is reanalyzed. Thirty-seven samples (one urine, three plasma and thirty-three blood
samples of the oral arm and two blood samples of the intravenous arm) with CV% >20% were
reanalyzed. For a number of blood samples, the CV remained >20% after multiple reanalyses
and were reported, as agreed as not reportable. Overall, the AMS analytical runs met the
acceptance criteria.
Urine and Feces
The [14C] radioactivity concentrations in urine, feces, and vomitus (if applicable) were
determined using LSC (for feces and urine) or AMS (when radioactivity concentrations by LSC
dropped below the LLOQ). All samples and stock solutions and dilutions thereof were stored at a
nominal −20°C.
All QC samples (50, 5000, and 40000 dpm/g) in human urine were prepared by spiking human
urine with a reference standard [1-methyl-14C]-caffeine. QC samples (100, 6400, and
128000 dpm/g) in human feces were prepared by spiking human feces homogenate with the
reference standard. For each run, the absolute bias had to be ≤10% of the nominal value (≤15%
at the lowest level using the low-level count mode, LLCM) for at least four out of six QC
samples. At least one QC result had to be accepted for each QC level. The validated range for the
analysis of urine and feces samples using LSC is 10 to 1000000 dpm/mL and 40 to
1000000 dpm/g, respectively.
Measurements of urine samples were performed in duplicate and feces samples were analyzed in
triplicate. The samples were placed in the LSC for at least 30 minutes before counting. The CV
over the duplicate/triplicate analysis had to be ≤15% for samples with an activity level
>250 dpm/g or ≤20% for samples with an activity level ≤250 dpm/g. When the criteria for CV
over the duplicate/triplicate analysis were not reached, the duplicate/triplicate results were
rejected, and the samples were reanalyzed in duplicate. These criteria did not apply for study
samples with results below the LLOQ, as these samples were analyzed using AMS.
The method was acceptable for assessment of [14C]-remibrutinib in human urine and feces in
clinical samples obtained from Study X2104.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)

discordance identified.

14.5.1.2. Introduction
The primary objectives of the Applicant’s analysis were to:


To characterize the remibrutinib pharmacokinetics in subjects with CSU for total daily dose
between 10 mg and 200 mg using pooled concentration data at steady state from healthy
volunteers and adult subjects with CSU.



To assess the effects of baseline covariates (age, weight, race/region, creatinine clearance)
and usage of PPIs/antacids on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib in the studied population.
14.5.1.3. Model Development
14.5.1.3.1. Data

The PK analyses for remibrutinib were based on PK data from three phase 1 studies in healthy
subjects, one phase 2 study in subjects with CSU, and three phase 3 studies in subjects with
CSU. Brief descriptions of the studies included are presented in Table 85.
The overall percent of BLQ samples for remibrutinib was 12.6%. These observations were
included in the population PK analysis as interval-censored. The final NONMEM data file for
remibrutinib model development contained 7088 observations from 1152 subjects. Table 86
provides summary statistics of the baseline demographic covariates in the PK analysis dataset.
Table 85. Summary of Studies With PK Sampling Included in Population PK Analysis

14.5.1.3.2. Base Model
The population PK model was developed using a nonlinear mixed-effects modeling approach
with the stochastic approximation expectation-maximization algorithm implemented in
MONOLIX (version 2021R2).
A population PK model for remibrutinib was previously developed based on pooled steady-state
data of Studies X2101 and A2201 with total daily dose between 10 mg and 200 mg. The model
was described as a two-compartment, first-order absorption model with a lag time and linear
elimination. The previous structural popPK model was used in the current popPK model based
on the new pooled dataset and with full reevaluation of the correlation matrix.
Interindividual variability was modelled assuming a log-normal distribution for patient level
random effects. Residual variability was described using proportional error model. The quality of
the model fit was assessed by diagnostic plots.
14.5.1.3.3. Covariate Analysis
The effect of body size covariates (e.g., body weight, body mass index, FFM, and FM) on
apparent clearance (CL/F) or apparent central volume of distribution (V1/F) was evaluated as
part of the base model. Weight with both estimated allometric scaling and fixed allometric
scaling of 0.75 for clearances and 1 for volumes was tested. The impact of other prespecified
219
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
covariates were investigated: age (on CL/F), gender (on CL/F), race/region (on CL/F), renal
impairment (assessed as creatinine clearance on CL/F), and usage of PPI/antacids (on CL/F,
V1/F, apparent intercompartmental clearance and apparent peripheral volume of distribution
[V2/F]). The significance of the covariates after inclusion of the body size effect was assessed
using a full covariate modeling approach. The impact of usage of PPI/antacids was explored
graphically as inclusion of the PPI/antacid usage as time-dependent covariate led to unstable
models. Hepatic impairment was not assessed as a covariate in the model. According to
Applicant’s response to Information Request, subjects with hepatic impairment were not enrolled
in the studies included in the population PK analysis. In the full modeling dataset, a small
fraction of subjects showed mildly elevated levels of ALT (n=94, 8%), AST (n=34, 3%) or INR
(n=34, 3%) in the overall dataset, and only one subject had total bilirubin ≥1.5x ULN.
Primary covariates evaluated in the development of PK model was included in Table 86.
14.5.1.4. Final Model
A two-compartment model with first-order absorption with fixed lag time and constant clearance
described the PK data of remibrutinib (Figure 52, Figure 53). Statistically significant covariate
effects on PK model parameters were the followings: FFM and FM on CL/F, race effect on
CL/F, and FM effect on V1/F. Age, gender, and mild renal impairment are not significant
covariates. Based on the graphical exploration of observed data, the use of PPI/antacids does not
appear to have effect on remibrutinib exposure (Figure 51).
The parameter estimates for the final covariate model are listed in Table 87. The RSE for all
parameters was <40%. The intersubject variability for PK parameters was generally high
especially for V1/F, the apparent intercompartmental clearance, and V2/F, which ranges from
127% to 4386%. Moderate to high eta-shrinkage was observed for PK parameters. According to
the Applicant, the high intersubject variability is due to large variability observed in PK
concentrations.
The effects of FFM, FM, Mainland Chinese subjects, Japanese subjects, and other Asian
subjects, on the AUC of remibrutinib are explored in Table 88. The effects of evaluated intrinsic
factors (FFM, FM, Mainland Chinese subjects, Japanese subjects, and other Asian subjects) are
generally small (20 to 30%). According to the Applicant, potential explanation for the effect of
FFM and FM on remibrutinib may be remibrutinib is a lipophilic compound (LogP ~3), which
can distribute into human fat tissue and result in body composition as an important factor for
drug distribution and metabolism. The effect of body weight was small based on the results of
simulated AUC in subjects with weight of 49 kg (5th percentile) and 109 kg (95th percentile)
assuming the ratio of FFM to FM of 2:1. As the FDA requested, the Applicant performed
additional simulation to assess the effect of body weight on remibrutinib exposure by using
resampled demographic characteristics from the population PK data, which keeps the natural
correlations among body fat percentage, body weight, and gender and variability in body
composition. The results confirmed that the impact of body weight on remibrutinib exposures is
not clinically relevant (Table 89). Given the high intersubject variability of remibrutinib
pharmacokinetics, the effect of explored intrinsic factors is unlikely to be clinically meaningful.

14.5.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
14.5.2.1. Review Summary
There was a positive E-R relationship for both continuous and categorical efficacy endpoints
(UAS7, ISS7, and HSS7 response) with plateau of response achieved at the proposed
remibrutinib dose regimen.
The E-R analysis for safety did not identify a significant relationship between remibrutinib
exposure and AEs and AEs of special interest. The E-R analysis for remibrutinib and safety
laboratory and vital sign parameters is considered exploratory due to the limited range of
exposure from one dose level. Among the subjects with chronic spontaneous urticaria who
received remibrutinib 25 mg twice daily, there was no clinically relevant association between
remibrutinib exposure and changes in laboratory parameters of blood chemistry, selected blood
cell counts, immunoglobulin concentrations, selected coagulation panel parameters, diastolic and
systolic blood pressure (BP).
14.5.2.2. Introductions
The primary objectives of the Applicant’s E-R analysis were to:


To explore the exposure-response relationship between remibrutinib concentrations and
UAS7, ISS7, and HSS7 in adult subjects with chronic spontaneous urticaria at selected time
points.



To explore the effect of selected intrinsic and extrinsic factors on clinical outcome (UAS7,
ISS7, and HSS7 response) in adult subjects with CSU.
226

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)


To develop nonlinear mixed effects models characterizing the exposure-response relationship
to remibrutinib for efficacy and describing longitudinal changes in UAS7 (and possibly
ISS7/HSS7) in adult subjects with CSU.



To explore the exposure-response relationship between remibrutinib concentrations and
safety in adult subjects with CSU based on the selected adverse events and changes in
selected laboratory and vital sign parameters.

PK exposure metrices including average concentration at steady state (Cavg,ss), and individual
empirical Bayes estimates were predicted based on post hoc parameter estimates from the final
population PK model. Of note, 18% of subjects included in the current analyses did not have any
PK measurements and Cavg,ss in those individuals were imputed based on population CL/F
estimate adjusted for individual covariate values. A sensitivity analysis for the key exposureefficacy and exposure-safety was conducted by excluding individuals with missing PK
measurements, and the interpretation of its results was in line with the analyses presented in this
report.
14.5.2.3. E-R Analysis for Efficacy
The efficacy E-R analysis was based on data from one phase 2 (A2201) and two phase 3 trials
(A2301 and A2302). The primary efficacy endpoints explored in the analysis include the
followings: absolute change from baseline in HSS7, absolute change from baseline in ISS7,
absolute change from baseline in UAS7, achievement of complete absence of itch and hives
(UAS7 =0), and achievement of disease activity control (UAS7 ≤6).
The analysis data included 45230 measurements for each efficacy endpoint from 1221 subjects.
MONOLIX (version 2021R2) and R software (version 4.1.0) were used for the E-R analysis for
efficacy.
For continuous efficacy endpoints (changes from baseline in UAS7, ISS7, and HSS7), the results
from a nonlinear model with sigmoidal Emax function of exposure metrics and baseline value as a
covariate suggested a positive E-R relationship. The plateau of response was achieved at low
remibrutinib exposures, and the remibrutinib dose regimen investigated in phase 3 studies
(25 mg BID) provided PK exposures in the plateau range of the E-R relationship (Figure 54).
Similarly, for binomial efficacy variables, the results from a logistic regression model with
sigmoidal Emax function suggested that the remibrutinib dose regimen investigated in phase 3
studies (25 mg BID) provided PK exposures in the plateau range of the exposure-response
relationship (Figure 55). Longitudinal nonlinear mixed effects exposure-response model for
UAS7 was attempted but not developed due to issues with model convergence and identifiability
of parameters estimates.

14.5.2.4. E-R Analysis for Safety
The safety E-R analysis was based on data from one phase 2 (A2201) and two phase 3 trials
(A2301 and A2302). The safety endpoints included AEs monitoring, physical examinations,
monitoring of laboratory markers in blood and urine, and vital signs. The AEs of special interest
included infections, bleeding, and cytopenias. The laboratory and vital sign parameters included
laboratory parameters of blood chemistry panel, blood cell counts, immunoglobulin
concentrations, selected parameters of coagulation, diastolic BP, and systolic BP. R software
(version 4.1.0) were used for the E-R analysis for safety.
For AEs, three data pools were created based on differences in the design of the analyzed studies,
in particular duration of treatment and the placebo-controlled period. Logistic regression with
log-transformed Cavg,ss were conducted to test the influence of exposure on AEs in subjects on
active treatment in the three pooled safety datasets. Individual remibrutinib Cavg,ss were
comparable between subjects with and without occurrence of any AEs across all three E-R safety
data pools. Logistic regression fitted to all three E-R safety data pools did not show a statistically
significant relationship for occurrence of any AEs with increasing remibrutinib Cavg,ss. Similar
results were observed for occurrence of SAEs which had rates below 5%. Logistic regression
fitted to all three E-R safety data pools did not show a statistically significant relationship for
occurrence of AESIs (bleeding, cytopenia, and infection) with increasing remibrutinib Cavg,ss
(Figure 56).
For laboratory and vital-sign parameters, two data pools from Trials A2301 and A2302 with one
included placebo group and one excluded placebo group were explored. Exploratory and
MMRM analyses indicated no clear evidence of a relationship between remibrutinib PK
exposures (Cavg,ss) and changes in laboratory parameters of blood chemistry panel (ALT,
amylase, alkaline phosphatase, AST, bilirubin, creatine kinase, creatinine, gamma-glutamyl
transferase, and lipase), selected blood cell counts (erythrocytes, leukocytes, lymphocytes,
neutrophils, and platelets), IgE concentrations, selected laboratory parameters from coagulation
panel (activated partial thromboplastin time, prothrombin INR, and prothrombin time), diastolic
and systolic BP in Trials A2301 and A2302.
The absolute bilirubin levels, erythrocyte counts, creatinine and creatine kinase levels were
higher in subjects with low remibrutinib Cavg,ss (in 1st quartile of distribution) than in subjects
with higher remibrutinib Cavg,ss (2nd to 4th quartile) or placebo. According to the Applicant, this
could be attributed to confounding factors of body size (e.g., fat mass or fat free mass) which
affect both remibrutinib pharmacokinetics and listed safety parameters.
The trend of elevations in median lymphocyte counts and decrease in platelet counts with
increasing remibrutinib exposure is of limited clinical relevance given the transient character and
small magnitude of change with median levels remaining within limits of normal, and no signal
for AEs of lymphocytosis and bleeding AESIs. Similarly, the trend of elevation in median
bilirubin concentrations, and decrease in creatinine concentrations and neutrophil counts with
increasing remibrutinib exposure is of no clinical relevance given the marginal magnitude of
these changes. The observed trends for positive correlation between Cavg,ss and change from
baseline in IgA and IgG concentrations, and a negative correlation with change from baseline in
IgM concentration is of limited clinical relevance, given small magnitude of observed changes,
inconsistent direction of the remibrutinib Cavg,ss effect on those globulins and no increase in
infection rates in remibrutinib arms in Trials A2301 and A2302.

14.5.3. PBPK Modeling
Executive Summary
The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant’s PBPK modeling
analyses to predict the following:


The effect of strong (ketoconazole, itraconazole, and ritonavir), moderate (fluconazole,
erythromycin), and weak (fluvoxamine) CYP3A inhibitors on the pharmacokinetics of
remibrutinib at doses of 25 mg BID.



The effect of strong (carbamazepine and rifampicin), moderate (efavirenz), and weak
(dexamethasone) CYP3A4 inducers on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib at doses of
25 mg BID.



The effect of multiple doses of remibrutinib 25 mg BID on the pharmacokinetics of
substrates of CYP3A (midazolam), P-gp (digoxin), and BCRP (rosuvastatin).

233
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
The Divisions of Inflammation and Immune Pharmacology and Pharmacometrics have reviewed
the PBPK submission (Report DMPK R2301014, modeling and simulation files, and response to
Information Requests dated March 24, 2025 (supporting document number [SDN] 0007) and
March 14, 2025 (SDN 0005)) to conclude the following:


Simulations predicted 3.21- to 3.65-fold increase in AUC of remibrutinib when multiple
doses of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were coadministered with multiple doses of strong
CYP3A4 inhibitors.



Simulations predicted 1.89- to 2.27-fold increase in AUC of remibrutinib when multiple
doses of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were coadministered with multiple doses of moderate
CYP3A4 inhibitors.



Simulations predicted 74.3% to 90.5% reduction in AUC of remibrutinib when multiple
doses of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were coadministered with multiple doses of strong
CYP3A4 inducers. Simulations predicted 64% reduction in AUC of remibrutinib when
multiple doses of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were coadministered with multiple doses of
moderate CYP3A4 inducers.



Simulations, in combination with clinical DDI results, suggest a low potential for a clinically
relevant effect of remibrutinib 25 mg BID on CYP3A4 substrates.



The magnitude of the interaction effect of remibrutinib 25 mg BID on the pharmacokinetics
of substrates of P-gp and BCRP could not be confidently predicted by PBPK.

PBPK Model for Remibrutinib
Background
Remibrutinib binds to the BTK protein, thereby inhibiting its enzymatic activity. Notably,
remibrutinib demonstrates TMDD at lower doses, a characteristic that influences its
pharmacokinetic profile and therapeutic efficacy.
Remibrutinib is predominantly metabolized by CYP3A4, with an estimated fractional
contribution of CYP3A4 (fmCYP3A4) of approximately 0.43. Other non-CYP-mediated
pathways appear to also contribute to remibrutinib metabolic clearance, while renal clearance is
minimal (<2%).
In vitro studies have demonstrated that remibrutinib acts as a CYP3A-reversible inhibitor
(Ki,u =14.1μM) and time-dependent inhibitor (KI,u =6.9μM, Kinact =0.612 1/h). Furthermore, in
vitro investigations have revealed that remibrutinib induces CYP3A4.
Remibrutinib exhibits high passive permeability at concentrations of 50µM and above, while at
lower concentrations, its permeability is diminished due to an apically directed P-gp-mediated
efflux process. This efflux is not significantly influenced by BCRP or multidrug resistanceassociated protein 2. Additionally, remibrutinib is not a substrate for the hepatic uptake
transporters OATP1B1 and OATP1B3. As a precipitant, remibrutinib demonstrates clinically
relevant potential to inhibit intestinal P-gp (Ki,u =0.382µM, R =1.02 [hepatic], R =517
[intestinal]), and BCRP (Ki,u =0.467µM, R =1.01 [hepatic], R =423 [intestinal]), The R value
denotes the static DDI risk assessment for remibrutinib at a dosage of 25 mg twice daily (see
Section 8.2).
234
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Multiple oral doses of remibrutinib at 100 mg BID did not significantly alter the PK parameters
of a single oral dose of combined OCs containing EE 30 μg and LVG 150 μg. Similarly,
remibrutinib did not significantly impact the PK parameters of midazolam (a CYP3A4 substrate)
or rosuvastatin (a substrate for BCRP, OATP1B1, and OAT3) (see Section 14.2.6.2 and
Section 14.2.6.3). The increases in PK parameters for these substrates were less than 1.7-fold
when compared to their administration alone. However, coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg
BID with digoxin 0.25 mg resulted in a 2.08-fold increase in Cmax, a 1.45-fold increase in
AUClast, and a 1.4-fold increase AUC0-inf of digoxin (see Section 14.2.6.2). Additionally,
remibrutinib did not exhibit the potential to inhibit the hepatic uptake of coproporphyrin I, a
biomarker for OATP1B1 substrate.
Grapefruit juice, a moderate to strong inhibitor of gut CYP3A4, did not increase the exposure to
remibrutinib (Study 2103). The Fgut was estimated from the dose-normalized AUC0-24h values of
4.89 h*ng/mL/mg and 6.28 h*ng/mL/mg for remibrutinib when administered alone and in the
presence of grapefruit juice, respectively. The Fgut was estimated to be 0.78, indicating a lesser
contribution of gut CYP3A4 in the metabolism of remibrutinib compared to hepatic CYP3A4
(see Section 14.2.6.1).
Ritonavir, a strong inhibitor of both gut and hepatic CYP3A4/P-gp, significantly increased the
dose-normalized Cmax and AUC0-24h of orally administered remibrutinib by 3.32-fold and 4.27fold, respectively. Conversely, carbamazepine, a strong inducer of CYP3A4, decreased Cmax,ss,
AUCtau,ss, and AUClast,ss of remibrutinib 100 mg BID by 74%, 77%, and 78%, respectively (see
Sections 14.2.6.1 and 14.2.6.2).

Software
Simcyp Population-based Simulator, version 21, release 1 (V21R1) and Simcyp® In Vitro Data
Analysis (SIVA) Toolkit© version 4 (Certara UK Limited, Sheffield, United Kingdom) was used
for the simulations.

Distribution, Metabolism, and Clearance
The full PBPK model was used to describe the distribution of remibrutinib. The scalar for the
tissue-partition coefficient was optimized from 1 to 0.17, resulting in a final Vss of 0.63 L/kg.
This value is generally comparable to the Vss of 0.81±0.22 L/kg observed following IV
administration in Study X2103.
CYP3A4 was estimated to contribute to 97.8% of the oxidative hepatic metabolism of
remibrutinib based on findings from the study in human liver microsomes (DMPK R1600559).
Clinically, the remibrutinib fmCYP3A4 was determined to be 43% at steady-state exposure based
on the findings from the clinical DDI with ritonavir (see Section 14.2.6.1). Additionally,
following IV administration in the human ADME study, 40% of the detected drug-related
material in the excreta (urine and feces) were identified as oxidative metabolites, while
approximately 17% were classified as mixed metabolites from glucuronidation, hydrolysis, or
glutathione conjugation with an oxidative metabolism step (unknown as primary and/or
secondary step) (see Section 14.2.2).
The retrograde method was employed to calculate the CYP3A4-mediated intrinsic clearance,
utilizing a fmCYP3A4 value of 0.43 and an estimated IV CL of 72 L/h. This approach predicted a
steady-state IV clearance of 70.1 L/h (uninhibited IV CL) in Study X2103. To consider the
potential for CYP3A4 saturation, the parameters Vmax =3.9 pmol/min/pmol of CYP3A4 and
Km,u =1.5μM were used in the model instead of the intrinsic clearance value. The CLr was
estimated to be approximately 1.224 L/h based on Study X2101. The remaining clearance
attributed to non-CYP3A4 metabolic pathways (hydrolysis and glutathione conjugation) was
entered into the model as additional systemic clearance. The disposition scheme of remibrutinib,
with estimates for pathways contribution (observed and predicted) at steady state (50-mg daily
oral dose) is shown in Figure 57.

Abbreviations: CYP, cytochrome P450; DDI, drug-drug interaction; PBPK, physiologically based pharmacokinetic

TMDD
Remibrutinib is an inhibitor of BTK and exhibits TMDD. The PBPK model for remibrutinib was
integrated with a PD model (scripted in Lua within the Simcyp Simulator v 21R1) to account for
the time-dependent changes in systemic clearance of remibrutinib due to target binding
saturation following repeated dosing. This PD model represents the "net effect" of the
irreversible binding of remibrutinib to BTK, aggregating binding events and target expression
across all tissues within the systemic compartment. This semimechanistic approach describes the
increased clearance of remibrutinib on Day 1 due to TMDD and the target saturation observed at
steady state and/or at higher remibrutinib doses. Importantly, the PD model was implemented to
describe the TMDD process and its effect on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib but was not
designed to elucidate remibrutinib-BTK binding kinetics across tissues or to be linked to
remibrutinib efficacy due to its limited parameters. The PD model is described by the following
equations:

242
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Equation 1. PD Model

Source: Study 2301014: Final physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) model and simulations for remibrutinib
Abbreviations: BTK, Bruton’s tyrosine kinase; Cblood,free unbound blood concentration of remibrutinib; dComplex, change in BTKremibrutinib complex; dt, change in time; dTarget, change in the concentrations of BTK; kdeg, target turnover; kirr, irreversible binding
constant; ksyn, zero order target synthesis rate; Target_occ,

In response to an FDA request (dated March 12, 2025) to justify the necessity of accounting for
the TMDD process in the remibrutinib PBPK model, the Applicant clarified that the exposure
differences between Day 1 and Day 12 in Study X2101 were up to 4.86-fold at the dose of 10 mg
once daily, which then decreased to 1.14-fold at the higher dose of 600 mg once daily (see
Section 14.2.1.1)) due to the time-dependent changes in systemic clearance of remibrutinib by
target binding saturation following repeated dosing.
The FDA also requested the Applicant to demonstrate the performance of the PD model for
different sets of PD parameters evaluated within the model. The initial TMDD parameters
(degradation constant [kdeg], irreversible binding constant [kirr], and synthesis rate constant [ksyn])
were manually adjusted in a “top-down” manner to predict remibrutinib pharmacokinetics in
Study X2101 after a single dose at 1.5, 5, 15, 30, 60, and 100 mg, and subsequently the PK
parameters after 10 and 25 mg once daily. The degradation rate constant (kdeg) was maintained at
0.01 h-1, consistent with the estimated kdeg value of 0.00976 h-1 obtained from the PK/PD
analysis of Study X2101. The PBPK input values for the kirr and ksyn of 500 1/µM*h and
0.045μM/h, respectively, demonstrated good performance among other evaluated values (data
not shown). Of note, parameter estimation or sensitivity analysis functions for the PD parameters
were not feasible in the current version of the simulator (Simcyp v 21R1). Therefore, statistical
analysis and visual predictive checks were employed to assess the PD model performance.
Further, the Applicant demonstrated the sensitivity of Cmax and AUC to target abundance (Rmax =
ksyn/kdeg) and kirr (=kon) to support the manually optimized kirr and ksyn values (Figure 58).

Drug Interactions
The in vitro parameters for CYP enzymes inhibition and induction and transporters inhibition are
listed in Table 90.
CYP3A4
Remibrutinib exhibits characteristics of both a reversible and time-dependent inhibitor and
inducer of CYP3A4 in vitro. An initial PBPK model using the in vitro estimates of CYP3A4
interaction parameters did not accurately estimate the observed magnitude of the DDI between
remibrutinib (100 mg BID) and midazolam (a CYP3A4 substrate) in Study X2102. Any of the
induction parameters (i.e., maximal fold induction of an enzyme or gene expression [Indmax],
substrate concentration that induce enzyme by 50% [IndC50]) or time-dependent inhibition (TDI)
parameters (i.e., inhibition constant [KI] and enzyme inactivation constant [kinact]) could be
adjusted accordingly to recover a clinical DDI data. Due to identifiability challenges associated
with the optimization of TDI and induction parameters, local and global sensitivity analyses
(GSA) were then performed to evaluate the impact of individual parameter variations on the
predicted DDI and guide the rationale for parameter optimization. The GSA revealed that the
most influential parameters affecting the AUC and Cmax ratios were, in order of significance, KI
(=Kapp), Indmax, kinact, and IndC50 (Figure 59).
The local sensitivity analysis was subsequently conducted using a range that spanned three times
the standard deviation of the in vitro values (0.9 to 12.90μM for KI and 1 to 67.7-fold for Indmax),
predicted AUC ratios ranging from 0.7 to 1.6 and Cmax ratios from 0.7 to 1.48 (Figure 60). Based
on these findings, the in vitro KI value was reduced from 6.9 to 1.38μM to best align with the
observed AUC and Cmax ratios of the DDI with midazolam. This optimization was achieved
without altering the in vitro estimated values for the reversible inhibition, induction, or the TDI
kinact.

Results and Discussion
Remibrutinib PBPK Model Performance for Pharmacokinetics
The developed PBPK model for remibrutinib effectively captured the PK profiles in healthy
subjects under various administration scenarios, including steady-state intravenous dosing
(0.1 mg following a 50-mg oral dose), single oral doses (1.5 to 200 mg), and multiple oral doses
(10 to 100 mg once daily or 25 to 200 mg twice daily for 12 days) (Figure 61 and Table 93).

245
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Considering the high PK variability reported for remibrutinib, the predicted PK parameters
demonstrated an adequate degree of accuracy, with 73% falling within 2-fold of the mean
observed values and 50% within 0.7- to 1.4-fold. Additionally, the model accurately predicted
the PK profile of remibrutinib at a dose of 25 mg twice daily in subjects with chronic
spontaneous urticaria (Figure 62 and Table 93).
The predicted CL/F of remibrutinib at 100 mg twice daily was 264 L/h, which is comparable to
the observed CLss/F of 255 L/h from Study X2101. The model-predicted volume of distribution
for remibrutinib at 100 mg twice daily for 12 days was 562 L (calculated from CL =264 L/h and
lambda-Z =0.47 h-1). This value is lower than the observed Vss/F of 2040 L in Study X2101 but
aligns closely with the observed volume of 576 L from the mass balance Study X2104 when
remibrutinib was administered at 100 mg BID for 3 days. The estimated peripheral volume of
distribution (V2) from the population pharmacokinetic modeling was 1180 L. Clinical study
results indicate that the volume of distribution increases with higher doses of remibrutinib, for
example, following the administration of once daily doses of remibrutinib (10 to 600 mg) for 12
days in Study X2101, the Vss/F increased from 554 L at 10-mg dose to 7130 L at 600-mg dose
(see Section 14.2.1.1).
