RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
PBPK Model Performance for DDI
DDI simulations were conducted to evaluate the adequacy of the model to recover the clinical
DDI data of remibrutinib with the strong CYP3A modulators ritonavir and carbamazepine,
confirming the fmCY3A4 value (43%) for remibrutinib included in the PBPK model.
Simulations were conducted for remibrutinib both oral (20 mg) and intravenous (0.1 mg)
administration with ritonavir, as observed in the Study X2103. The predicted geometric mean
ratios of Cmax and AUClast for oral remibrutinib 20 mg, when coadministered with ritonavir, were
3.03 and 4.09, respectively. For intravenous remibrutinib, the predicted geometric mean ratios of
Cmax and AUClast were 1.84 and 1.07, respectively. The predicted DDI between ritonavir and
remibrutinib was found to be comparable to the observed PK ratios, with a predicted/observed
ratio range of 0.91 to 1.21 (Figure 64 and Table 94).
Similarly, simulations were conducted for remibrutinib 100 mg BID (five oral doses) with
carbamazepine, as observed in the Study A02103. The predicted geometric mean ratios for Cmax
and AUC for remibrutinib, when coadministered with carbamazepine, were 0.369 and 0.312,
respectively, which were slightly higher than the observed values of 0.26 and 0.23, respectively.
The predicted/observed ratio were 1.42 for Cmax and 1.36 for AUC (Figure 64 and Table 94).
Overall, the PBPK model of remibrutinib adequately predicted the magnitude of the DDI
between remibrutinib 20 mg steady state and ritonavir observed in Studies X2103, and slightly
overpredicted the induction effect of carbamazepine on remibrutinib 100 mg steady state
observed in Study A02103.
PBPK Model Performance Using PD Model
The reviewer conducted independent simulations for remibrutinib 10 mg QD, 25 mg QD, and
200 mg QD to assess the impact of integrating the PD model into the PBPK model. The PD
model, which accounts for TMDD, demonstrated a notable advantage in accurately describing
the observed PK data at lower doses (e.g., 10 mg and 25 mg) compared to the PBPK model
without the PD component (Figure 63). However, at a higher dose of 200 mg, the PD model did
not provide additional benefits in describing the observed PK data, as the simulated PK profiles
were comparable with and without the PD model implementation.

249
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Figure 63. Simulated Remibrutinib PK Profiles Following the Administration of Remibrutinib 10,
25, and 200 mg QD With and Without Implementing the PD Model

Source: Reviewer-generated figures using remibrutinib model submitted by the Applicant.
Abbreviations: b.i.d., twice daily; hr, hour; PD, pharmacodynamic; PK, pharmacokinetic; QD, once daily

To further assess the impact of incorporating the PD model into the PBPK model for
remibrutinib, the reviewer conducted DDI simulations to predict the influence of the PD model
on key model applications, namely the DDI magnitude between remibrutinib administered at
25 mg BID and a moderate CYP3A inhibitor (erythromycin) or inducer (efavirenz). PBPK
simulations that included the PD model predicted Cmax and AUC ratios for the interaction
between remibrutinib 25 mg BID and erythromycin 500 mg to be 1.9 (90% CI: 1.84, 1.97) and
2.27 (90% CI: 2.18, 2.36), respectively. The PBPK simulations without the PD model predicted
Cmax and AUC ratios for the same interaction to be 1.83 (90% CI: 1.78, 1.89) and 2.20 (90% CI:
2.12, 2.29), respectively. Similarly, PBPK simulations that included the PD model predicted Cmax
and AUC ratios for the interaction between remibrutinib 25 mg BID and efavirenz 600 mg to be
0.405 (90% CI: 0.384, 0.428) and 0.361 (90% CI: 0.34, −0.384), respectively. PBPK simulations
without the PD model predicted Cmax and AUC ratios for the same interaction to be 0.44 (90%
CI: 0.42, 0.46) and 0.39 (90% CI: 0.37, 0.41), respectively. The DDI magnitudes between
remibrutinib 25 mg BID and either a moderate CYP3A inhibitor or inducer, with and without the
inclusion of the PD model, were found to be comparable. Therefore, it can be concluded that the
PD model does not significantly impact the prediction of drug-interaction magnitudes.
PBPK Model Applications
Remibrutinib PBPK model was used to predict drug interactions for scenarios that were not
tested clinically at the proposed therapeutic dose of 25 mg BID.

250
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Effects of CYP3A Inhibitors and Inducers on Remibrutinib
The net effects of induction, reversible inhibition, time-dependent inhibition, and saturation of
CYP3A4 are expected to collectively influence the elimination of remibrutinib. The model
predicted a net effect of 10 to 20% reduction in both hepatic and intestinal clearance when the
dose is increased from 25 mg BID to 100 mg. Parameter sensitivity analysis has demonstrated
that escalating the dose of remibrutinib from 20 mg to 200 mg results in a decrease in the
fmCYP3A4. This decrease leads to a reduction in the magnitude of drug interactions with strong
CYP3A4 inhibitors and an increase in the magnitude of drug interactions with strong CYP3A4
inducers (data not shown). Time-dependent inhibition and CYP3A4 saturation were found to
contribute to the reduction of both hepatic and intestinal intrinsic clearance by CYP3A4,
particularly at doses ranging from 25 mg to 100 mg BID (data not shown). Notably, CYP3A4
saturation is more prevalent in the gut due to higher drug concentrations in this region.
Additionally, auto-induction in the gut is more pronounced for remibrutinib at a dose of 100 mg
BID compared to 25 mg BID.
Strong CYP3A Inhibitors
DDI simulations were conducted to extrapolate the outcome of the clinical DDI effect of
ritonavir with remibrutinib at 20 mg to the proposed therapeutic dose of 25 mg BID, using
ritonavir, itraconazole, and ketoconazole as strong CYP3A inhibitors. Following
coadministration of ritonavir (100 mg BID for 7 days), itraconazole (200 mg QD for 7 days) or
ketoconazole (200 mg BID for 7 days), the predicted geometric mean ratios for Cmax and AUCtau
of remibrutinib 25 mg BID ranged from 2.47 to 2.67 and 3.21 to 3.65, respectively, in healthy
subjects (Table 97). These data suggest that a strong CYP3A inhibitor is expected to cause a
moderate inhibition effect on the exposure to remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
Moderate CYP3A Inhibitors
DDI simulations were conducted to predict the clinical DDI effect of the moderate CYP3A
inhibitors fluconazole and erythromycin on remibrutinib 25 mg BID. The predicted geometric
mean ratios for Cmax and AUCtau of remibrutinib 25 mg BID following oral administration of
erythromycin (500 mg four times a day for 7 days) in healthy subjects, were 1.90 and 2.27,
respectively (Table 97). Similarly, the predicted geometric mean ratios for Cmax and AUCtau of
remibrutinib 25 mg BID following oral administration of fluconazole (200 mg QD for 7 days) in
healthy subjects were 1.67 and 1.89, respectively (Table 97). These data suggest a moderate
CYP3A inhibitor is expected to cause a weak to moderate effect on the exposure to remibrutinib
25 mg BID.
Weak CYP3A4 Inhibitor
Following coadministration of the weak CYP3A inhibitor fluvoxamine (50 mg QD for 7 days),
the predicted geometric mean ratios for Cmax and AUCtau of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were 1.04
and 1.04, respectively (Table 97), in healthy subjects. These data suggest that a weak CYP3A
inhibitor is not expected to impact the exposure to remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
Strong CYP3A Inducers
DDI simulations were conducted to predict the clinical DDI effect of the strong CYP3A inducers
carbamazepine and rifampicin on remibrutinib 25 mg BID. The predicted geometric mean ratios
251
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
for Cmax and AUCtau of remibrutinib 25 mg BID with carbamazepine (up to 300 mg BID for 22
days) were 0.358 and 0.257, respectively (Table 97). Similarly, the predicted geometric mean
ratios for Cmax and AUCtau of remibrutinib 25 mg BID following oral administration of
rifampicin (600 mg QD for 7 days) in healthy subjects were 0.122 and 0.095, respectively (Table
97). These data suggest that a strong CYP3A inducer is expected to cause a strong effect (≥80%
decrease) on the exposure to remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
Moderate CYP3A4 Inducer
Following coadministration of the moderate CYP3A inducer efavirenz (600 mg QD for 7 days),
the predicted geometric mean ratios for Cmax and AUCtau of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were 0.405
and 0.361, respectively (Table 97), in healthy subjects. These data suggest that a moderate
CYP3A inducer is expected to cause a moderate effect (50 to 80% decrease) on the exposure to
remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
Weak CYP3A4 Inducer
Following coadministration of the weak CYP3A4 inducer dexamethasone (8 mg BID for 7 days),
the predicted geometric mean ratios for Cmax and AUCtau of remibrutinib 25 mg BID in healthy
subjects were 0.672 and 0.626, respectively (Table 97). These data suggest that a weak CYP3A
inducer is expected to cause a weak effect (20 to 50% decrease) on the exposure to remibrutinib
25 mg BID. Moreover, the model predicted no impact of remibrutinib 25 BID on the
pharmacokinetics of dexamethasone, as a CYP3A4 substrate (Table 96).

Effect of Remibrutinib on Midazolam (Sensitive CYP3A4 Substrate)
To simulate the results from Study X2102, the predicted geometric mean ratios of Cmax and AUC
for midazolam 2-mg single oral dose on Day 3 and Day 11 following oral administration of
remibrutinib 100 mg BID in healthy subjects were 1.29 and 1.35 on Day 3 and 1.35 and 1.46 on
Day 11, respectively. These predicted interaction magnitudes were comparable to the observed
ratios in Study X2102 with a predicted/observed ratios range of 0.93 to 1.06 (Figure 64 and
Table 95). The assessment of DDI between remibrutinib and midazolam on Day 3 and 11 takes
in consideration the net effect of remibrutinib on CYP3A4 function which may exhibit a timedependent characteristics. The predicted reversible inhibition effect is likely more pronounced at
the initiation of treatment (Day 3), while the induction effect may be most evident after the
cessation of treatment (Day 11).
The geometric mean ratios for Cmax and AUCtau of midazolam 2-mg single oral dose on Day 3
and Day 11 following oral administration of remibrutinib 25 mg BID in healthy subjects were
predicted to be 1.18 and 1.20 on Day 3 and 1.22 and 1.25 on Day 11, respectively (Table 96).
Based on the predicted effects of remibrutinib 25 mg BID on midazolam exposure on Days 3 and
11, it can be inferred that remibrutinib has a low potential to inhibit or induce CYP3A4.
Effect of Remibrutinib on Ethinylestradiol (Oral Contraceptive, CYP3A4 Substrate)
To simulate the results from Study X2102, the predicted geometric mean ratios of Cmax and AUC
for ethinylestradiol 0.03-mg single oral dose on Day 11 following oral administration of
remibrutinib 100 mg BID in healthy subjects were 1.09 and 1.12, respectively. These predicted
interaction magnitudes were comparable to the observed ratios in Study X2102 with a
predicted/observed ratio of 0.852 for Cmax and 0.966 for AUC (Figure 64 and Table 95).
The predicted geometric mean ratios for Cmax and AUCtau of ethinylestradiol 0.03-mg single oral
dose on Day 11 following oral administration of remibrutinib 25 mg BID in healthy subjects,
were predicted to be 1.07 and 1.08, respectively (Table 96). Therefore, remibrutinib lacks the
potential to induce or inhibit CYP3A4.
Effect of Remibrutinib on Rosuvastatin (OATP1B and BCRP Substrate)
The in vitro inhibition constants for OATP1B and BCRP were not directly applied in the PBPK
model of remibrutinib. Instead, the Applicant aimed to establish scaling factors based on their
analysis of available clinical drug interactions of various OATP1B/BCRP perpetrators with the
biomarker coproporphyrin I and several statins, including
rosuvastatin.(b)Based(b) on

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Conclusion
The pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib are significantly influenced by drugs that modulate
CYP3A activity. PBPK simulation studies have predicted clinically significant changes in
remibrutinib’s Cmax,ss and AUCtau when coadministered with strong or moderate inhibitors or
inducers of CYP3A, which agreed with the observed clinical DDI results of remibrutinib with
strong CYP3A modulators.
When remibrutinib 25 mg BID is coadministered with multiple doses of strong CYP3A
inhibitors such as ritonavir, itraconazole, or ketoconazole, the Cmax,ss and AUCtau are predicted to
increase by approximately 2.47- to 2.67-fold and 3.21- to 3.65-fold, respectively. Similarly,
moderate CYP3A inhibitors like erythromycin and fluconazole are predicted to elevate the Cmax,ss
and AUCtau by around 1.67- to 1.9-fold and 1.89- to 2.27-fold, respectively. In contrast, weak
CYP3A4 inhibitors such as fluvoxamine do not significantly affect the PK parameters of
remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
Conversely, strong CYP3A inducers like carbamazepine and rifampicin can markedly decrease
the Cmax,ss and AUCtau of remibrutinib by approximately 62.4% to 87.8% and 74.3% to 90.5%,
respectively. Moderate CYP3A inducers such as efavirenz reduce these PK parameters by about
59.5% and 64%, respectively.
In clinical Study A02103, remibrutinib 100 mg BID increased the Cmax and AUC of the P-gp
substrate digoxin by 2.08-fold and 1.45-fold, respectively, and the Cmax and AUC of the BCRP
substrate rosuvastatin by 1.56-fold and 1.65-fold, respectively. PBPK simulations tended to mispredict the observed magnitude of the interaction between remibrutinib 100 mg BID and these
transporter substrates. Therefore, due to the inherent limitations of model predictions and
confidence in the predicted results, the clinical findings from Study A02103, conducted with the
100-mg BID dosage of remibrutinib, should be applicable to subjects receiving the lower dosage
of 25 mg BID.
PBPK simulations and clinical DDI studies have demonstrated the absence of an impact of
remibrutinib coadministration on the exposure to CYP3A4 substrates, such as midazolam and
oral contraceptives.

14.6. Pharmacogenetics
Not applicable.

15.1.4. Statistical Analysis Plan, Trials A2301 and
A2302
Additional details regarding the statistical analysis plan for the individual trials and prespecified
pooling of data from the two trials are provided below.

268
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
15.1.4.1. Sample Size Determination, Trials A2301 and
A2302
Based on the primary endpoints analysis of phase 2b Study CLOU064A2201, the Applicant
assumed a common standard deviation of 6 and treatment difference between remibrutinib and
placebo of 4 in the mean change from baseline of ISS7 at Week 12. A t-test with type I error of
one-sided 0.025 had more than 99% power to detect a treatment difference of 4 with a sample
size of 270 subjects in the remibrutinib arm and 135 subjects in the placebo arm. Assuming
approximately 10% dropout by Week 12, a sample size of 300 subjects in the remibrutinib arm
and 150 subjects in the placebo arm was targeted.
15.1.4.2. Supplementary Analysis at Week 24, Trials A2301
and A2302
An analysis of the change from baseline in HSS7 and ISS7 at Week 24 was conducted by the
Applicant as an exploratory analysis. The analysis used the same methods as the primary
analysis at Week 12, with intercurrent events handled similarly. However, the intercurrent event
of intake of strongly confounding prohibited medication was defined as biologics treatment at
any time before Week 24, cyclosporin after Week 8 to Week 24, or systemic corticosteroids after
Week 8 to Week 12. As with the analyses at Week 12, the treatment policy strategy was
recommended to the Applicant during the planning and is of primary regulatory interest. The
results presented in this review correspond to the Applicant’s prespecified intercurrent event
handling strategy and comparisons of the analyses of the two estimands are discussed where
relevant. Further, oral corticosteroids were allowed as an additional rescue therapy for CSU only
after Week 12 on an as-needed basis for unbearable symptoms.
15.1.4.3. Sensitivity and Supplementary Analyses, Trials
A2301 and A2302
A prespecified supplementary analysis varied the intercurrent event handling strategies of the
primary estimand. The change from baseline in ISS7 and HSS7 score up to Week 12 was
replaced with zero (i.e., no clinical improvement from baseline) for the intercurrent event of
“Intake of strongly confounding prohibited medication (e.g., biologics treatment at any time
before Week 12, cyclosporin after Week 8 to Week 12, systemic corticosteroids after Week 8 to
Week 12)” handled with composite strategy.
Subjects who did not meet the inclusion criteria of baseline ISS7 ≥6 and HSS7 ≥6 were noted by
the review team. A sensitivity analysis was performed for the coprimary endpoints which
excluded such subjects and methods were the same as for the primary analysis.
At the IND stage, the Agency recommended that the Applicant conduct sensitivity analyses to
investigate the robustness of the assumption in the primary analysis that data were MAR.
Although no such analysis was prespecified by the Applicant prior to submission, the Agency
requested a tipping-point analysis be performed in line with the prespecified Agency
recommendation. Assumptions were varied on the missing outcomes handled through treatment
policy strategy for discontinuations of study treatment prior to Week 12 due to any reasons and

269
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
the missing outcomes handled under jump-to-reference or MAR assumption. The steps were as
follows.


Step 1: As per the primary analysis, all missing ISS7/HSS7 scores at Week 12 were imputed
following the same imputation method specified for primary estimand.



Step 2: In each imputed dataset 𝑖 (𝑖 = 1, … 100), in the remibrutinib arm, positive shift 𝛿𝑗
was added to the imputed missing scores and in the placebo arm, negative shift 𝛿𝑘 was added
to the imputed missing scores. This imputed a worse outcome in the remibrutinib arm and a
better outcome in the placebo arm. This was done so that the resulting values cannot exceed
possible range of variables (minimum 0 and maximum 21). Observed data and the worst
scores used under the composite strategy remained. Then the same MMRM as for primary
analysis was fitted.



Step 3: The shift values 𝛿𝑗 and 𝛿𝑘 were varied over a plausible range for both arms. For ISS7
and HSS7, 𝛿𝑗 varied between 0 to 21 (0, 5, 10, 15, 21) for the remibrutinib arm and 𝛿𝑘 varied
between 0 to −21 (0, −5, −10, −15, −21) for the placebo arm.
15.1.4.4. Subgroup Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302

Subgroup analyses performed within study using an MMRM with the same covariates as the
primary analysis, as well the subgroup and interaction of subgroup with treatment.
15.1.4.5. Secondary Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302
For the secondary endpoints of proportion of subjects with UAS7 ≤6 at Week 2, proportion of
subjects with UAS7 ≤6 at Week 12, and proportion of subjects with UAS7 =0 at Week 12, the
prescribing information presents the risk difference and its 95% confidence interval, rather than
the conditional odds ratio as specified in the primary analyses of these endpoints. The Applicant
specified the methodology for calculating the risk difference and its confidence interval as
follows.
1. A logistic model was fit with maximum likelihood that regresses the outcome on treatment
assignments and prespecified baseline covariates, including an intercept term.
2. For each subject, the model-based prediction of the probability of response under treatment
was computed in both the treatment group and placebo group using each subject’s specific
baseline covariates.
3. The average response under treatment was estimated by averaging (across all subjects in the
trial) the probabilities estimated in Step 2.
4. For each subject, the model-based prediction of the probability of response under placebo
was computed in both the treatment group and placebo group using each subject’s specific
baseline covariates.
5. The average response under placebo was estimated by averaging (across all subjects in the
trial) the probabilities estimated in Step 4.
6. The estimates of average responses rates in the two treatment groups from Steps 3 and 5 were
used to estimate the risk difference.

270
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
The delta method was applied to calculate the standard error of the risk difference. The steps
above were done for each imputed data set separately and the results were combined using
Rubin’s rules, leading to a pooled estimate of the marginal difference and of the standard error.
Based on these estimates, a 95% confidence interval for the marginal difference was calculated.
15.1.4.6. Pooled Subgroup Analyses, Trials A2301 and
A2302
Pooled analyses were prespecified by combining data from Trials A2301 and A2302. For the
coprimary endpoints, LS means for within-treatment and treatment-comparison (within
subgroups) were evaluated using an MMRM including treatment group, baseline score
(respectively HSS7/ISS7) score, study, geographical region, previous exposure to anti-IgE
biologics for CSU, week, the interaction of treatment by week, and the interaction of baseline
score (respectively HSS7/ISS7) by week as fixed effects, in addition to the respective subgroup
variable and interaction between the respective subgroup, treatment and week, with estimand
related multiple imputation data.
For the cases where the computation of the MMRM took extreme amount of time, the ddfm = bw
option was considered in the corresponding SAS procedure. When the MMRM with an
unstructured covariance matrix did not converge, the compound-symmetry structure was used.
When this also failed, the analysis of covariance model was used including treatment group,
baseline score (respectively HSS7/ISS7) score, study, geographical region, previous exposure to
anti-IgE biologics for CSU, the respective subgroup variable, and interaction of treatment with
the respective subgroup variable.
Rubin’s rule was applied to combine the multiple imputed datasets of estimates to produce the
overall estimates, standard errors, and confidence intervals. If no data were missing, there was no
imputation; the imputed datasets were all identical, and calculations were based on the first
imputed dataset only.
The following subgroups were prespecified:


Randomization strata:
– Previous exposure to anti-IgE biologics (yes/no)
– Geographic region (Latin America, The Caribbean, and Canada; Region Europe; Japan;
United States; Asia, Middle East and Africa)



Age (18-<65, 65-<85, 85+)



Sex (male, female)



Race (White, Black or African American, Asian, American Indian or Alaska Native, Other)



Ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino, Non-Hispanic or Latino)



Baseline UAS7 score (16-<28 moderate, 28+ severe)



Previous experience of angioedema (defined based on prior urticaria history with
angioedema occurrence; yes/no)



Baseline chronic urticaria index status (10+ positive, <10 negative)

15.2. Study A2201
15.2.1. Study Design, Study A2201
The primary objective was to characterize the remibrutinib dose-response on the presence of
hives and itch. Study A2201 was a phase 2 multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebocontrolled, dose-finding study in adults with CSU inadequately controlled by H1-antihistamines
(see Sections 6.2 and 14.2.7 for additional details on design and conduct). The study employed a
1:1:1:1:1:1:1 randomization scheme across seven treatment arms, including six different
remibrutinib dosing regimens and placebo. Conducted across 82 centers in 17 countries from
June 2019 to April 2021, the study enrolled 311 subjects who underwent a 12-week treatment
period followed by a 4-week treatment-free follow-up period.

15.2.2. Eligibility Criteria, Study A2201
The study population consisted of male and female adults aged ≥18 years with a confirmed CSU
diagnosis for ≥6 months prior to screening and disease inadequately controlled by secondgeneration H1-antihistamines, as evidenced by the presence of itch and hives for ≥6 consecutive
weeks despite appropriate antihistamine treatment according to local guidelines. Key inclusion
criteria required subjects to have a UAS7 score ≥16 and HSS7 score ≥8 during the 7 days prior to
randomization, with documented presence of hives within 3 months before randomization. The
study excluded subjects with chronic inducible urticaria (having clearly defined triggers), other
diseases with urticaria or angioedema symptoms (such as urticaria vasculitis or mastocytosis),
other chronic itching skin diseases that could confound study evaluations, significant ECG
abnormalities, bleeding disorders, or ongoing chronic/recurrent infectious diseases.

RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)

Systemic corticosteroids





Antihistamines (except defined background and rescue medications)

15.2.4. Study Endpoints, Study A2201
All subjects were provided with an eDiary that contained the UPDD, AAS, and DLQI. The UAS7 was
recorded twice daily, while the AAS was recorded once daily. All PRO instruments were the same as
those used in the phase 3 trials (Section 15.1.3).

Safety Assessments
The primary safety variables were AE, SAEs, study discontinuations, laboratory test results, vital
signs, EKG, and physical exams.

15.3. Study A2201E1
15.3.1. Study Design, Study A2201E1
The primary objective of Study A2201E1 was to assess the long-term safety and tolerability of
remibrutinib in subjects with CSU who had participated in Study A2201. Study A2201E1 was an
open-label, single-arm, multicenter, long-term safety, and tolerability extension study designed
to evaluate the extended use of remibrutinib in subjects with CSU who had previously
participated in the core Study A2201. The study was conducted across 72 sites in 15 countries
and enrolled 230 subjects between October 2019 and September 2022.
The study consisted of three distinct periods. First, an observational period where subjects rolling
over from the core study with controlled disease (defined as UAS7 <16) were monitored without
receiving remibrutinib for up to 12 weeks to assess disease stability. If subjects experienced a
relapse (defined as UAS7 ≥16), they would transition to the treatment period. Second, a
treatment period where subjects with UAS7 ≥16 either from the core study rollover or from
relapse during the observational period received open-label remibrutinib 100 mg twice daily for
52 weeks. Notably, background H1-antihistamine therapy was prohibited for the first 4 weeks of
treatment to assess the drug's efficacy as monotherapy. Finally, a treatment-free follow-up period
provided post-treatment monitoring for a minimum of 4 weeks, with extended follow-up up to 16
weeks for subjects who achieved disease control (defined as UAS7 ≤6) at the end of treatment to
monitor for relapse.
The study allocation was based on disease activity scores at specific timepoints from the
preceding core study, with subjects having UAS7 ≥16 at Week 12 or 16 entering the treatment
period directly, while those with UAS7 <16 at Week 16 entering the observational period first.
This design allowed for evaluation of both the long-term safety profile and the durability of
treatment response in subjects with CSU as depicted in Figure 67.

15.3.2. Eligibility Criteria, Study A2201E1
Key inclusion criteria consisted of male and female adult subjects with CSU who had received
various doses of remibrutinib or placebo in the core study (Study A2201) and who had
completed either the Week 12 visit (end of treatment period) OR the Week 16 visit (end of
follow-up period) of Study A2201.

15.3.3. Study Treatment and Concomitant
Medications, Study A2201E1
IMP consisted of open-label 100 mg remibrutinib twice daily. Permitted and prohibited
concomitant medications were the same as in Study A2201 (see Section 15.3.3).

15.3.4. Study Variables and Assessments, Study
A2201E1
The primary study variables focused on safety parameters, specifically the occurrence of TEAEs
during the extension study period. Although efficacy assessments included various outcomes
based on UAS7 (e.g., change from baseline, proportion achieving UAS7 ≤6 and UAS7 =0), these
results are not presented or discussed in this review because the interpretability of uncontrolled,
unblinded efficacy results for a PRO in a condition with relatively high spontaneous remission
rate is extremely limited.

15.4. Study A1301
15.4.1. Study Design, Study A1301
The primary objective was to evaluate safety through assessment of TEAEs during the 52-week
treatment period. Study A1301 was a multicenter, open-label phase 3 clinical study conducted
across 13 centers in Japan from January 2022 to December 2023. The study investigated the
safety, tolerability, and efficacy of remibrutinib at a dose of 25 mg twice daily in 71 adult
Japanese subjects with CSU who remained inadequately controlled despite treatment with H1antihistamines at locally approved doses.
The study design consisted of three periods totaling up to 60 weeks: a screening period of up to 4
weeks, an open-label treatment period of 52 weeks with remibrutinib, and a treatment-free
follow-up period of 4 weeks.
Figure 68. Study Design Schema, Study A1301

Source: Applicant provided CSR for Study A1301, p. 28
Abbreviations: b.i.d., twice daily; Scr, Screening; W, Week

15.4.2. Eligibility Criteria, Study A1301
Eligible subjects were adults aged 18 years or older with CSU for at least 6 months prior to
screening. Key eligibility criteria included inadequately controlled disease despite treatment with
second-generation H1-antihistamines at locally approved doses defined as having persistent itch
and hives for at least 6 consecutive weeks prior to screening despite ongoing antihistamine
therapy, a UAS7 score of at least 16, an ISS7 score of at least 6, and an HSS7 score of at least 6.
Additionally, subjects were required to have documented evidence of hives within 3 months
before baseline and be on a stable, locally approved dose of second-generation H1antihistamines at the time of study entry.

15.4.3. Study Treatment and Concomitant
Medications, Study A1301
The IMP consisted of remibrutinib 25-mg film-coated tablets. Subjects were instructed to
administer one tablet in the morning and one tablet in the evening.

15.4.4. Study Variables and Assessments, Study
A1301
Safety Assessments
The primary safety variables were TEAEs during the 52-week treatment period including
number and proportion of subjects with at least one TEAE. Although efficacy assessments
included various outcomes based on UAS7 (e.g., change from baseline, proportion achieving
UAS7 ≤6 and UAS7 =0), AAS7 (cumulative weeks with AAS7 =0), and DLQI (proportion with
DLQI =0 to 1), these results are not presented or discussed in this review because the
interpretability of uncontrolled, unblinded efficacy results for a PRO in a condition with
relatively high spontaneous remission rate is extremely limited.

15.5. Study A2305
15.5.1. Study Design, Study A2305
The primary objective focused on cardiovascular safety assessed by ABPM. Study A2305 was a
global, multicenter, open-label phase 3 study evaluating remibrutinib 25 mg twice daily in adult
subjects with CSU inadequately controlled by second-generation H1-antihistamines. The study
was conducted across 45 centers in 10 countries including Argentina, Canada, France, Germany,
South Korea, Singapore, Slovakia, Spain, Turkey, and the United States.
The study design consisted of three distinct periods: a screening period of up to 4 weeks, a 12week open-label treatment period where all subjects received remibrutinib 25-mg film-coated
tablets administered orally twice daily, and a treatment-free follow-up period of 4 weeks,
resulting in a total study duration of up to 20 weeks. The planned sample size was approximately
136 subjects, with 144 subjects ultimately completing the screening and enrollment process.
The primary outcome evaluated whether the 24-hour weighted average systolic blood pressure as
measured by ABPM increased by more than 3 mm Hg at steady state (Week 4) compared to
baseline. Secondary outcomes included change in both systolic and diastolic blood pressure
during daytime and nighttime periods, as well as overall safety and tolerability. Exploratory
outcomes encompassed various efficacy measures related to urticaria disease activity, including
changes in urticaria activity scores, quality of life assessments, and pharmacokinetic parameters.
Refer to the cardiac safety integrated review team consult memo dated June 13, 2025 (DARRTS
Reference ID 5608166) for additional details on the design and results.

15.6. Study F12101
15.6.1. Study Design, Study F12101
The primary objective was to evaluate if immune responses following influenza and PPV-23
vaccinations in healthy subjects with interrupted remibrutinib treatment are noninferior to
placebo. Study F12101 was a phase 1, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallelgroup study that evaluated the effect of remibrutinib 100 mg twice daily on immune responses to
three different types of vaccines in healthy subjects aged 18 to 55 years. Approximately 90
subjects were randomized 1:1:1 into three treatment arms over a 43-day treatment period: Arm A
received concomitant remibrutinib treatment (placebo for Days 1 to 7, then remibrutinib Days 8
to 42), Arm B received interrupted remibrutinib treatment (remibrutinib Days 1 to 7, placebo
Days 8 to 28, then remibrutinib Days 29 to 42), and Arm C received placebo throughout Days 1
to 42. All subjects received three vaccines simultaneously on Day 15: quadrivalent seasonal
influenza vaccine (intramuscular, dominant arm), PPV-23 pneumococcal vaccine (intramuscular,
nondominant arm), and KLH neoantigen vaccine (subcutaneous, nondominant lower arm). The
vaccines were administered 3 hours after study drug dosing, with subjects domiciled at the
clinical site from Days −1 to 1 and Days 14 to 17. The study included a 28-day screening period,
followed by the 43-day treatment period, an end-of-study visit on Day 57, and a safety follow-up
call on Day 72, for a total study duration of approximately 85 days per subject. The intent of this
design was to determine whether temporarily interrupting remibrutinib treatment around
278

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
vaccination could preserve immune responses compared to continuous treatment or placebo
control. The primary endpoints were the proportion of ‘responders’ to T-cell-dependent influenza
quadrivalent vaccine and T-cell-independent PPV-23 vaccine in the interrupted remibrutinib
group compared to placebo. Response was defined as ≥4-fold increase in hemagglutinin antibody
titers (or ≥1:40 if baseline <1:10) for influenza, and ≥2-fold increase in IgG titers for at least
50% of PPV-23 serotypes (≥12 out of 23) at 28 days postvaccination. Noninferiority to placebo
was tested applying a noninferiority margin of 0.30. The primary analysis was performed on the
PD analysis set, which included all randomized subjects who had no relevant protocol deviations
and had completed the planned study treatment. Secondary endpoints assessed immune
responses with concomitant remibrutinib treatment, KLH vaccine responses with interrupted
treatment, and antibody titers/concentrations in the concomitant and interrupted remibrutinib
groups relative to placebo over time. For additional details regarding the design of Study
F12101, refer to the CBER consult memo (Inter-Center Consult #: 01066955) dated June 2,
2025, in DARRTS (Reference ID 5601342).

16. Efficacy
Results of supplementary analyses performed on data from the individual pivotal trials as well as
pooled data from the pivotal trials are presented in the sections below.

16.1. Trials A2301 and A2302
16.1.1. Results of Analyses at Week 24, Trials A2301
and A2302
Table 100 and Table 101 summarize the results for Trials A2301 and A2302 for efficacy
endpoints of change from baseline to Week 24 in HSS7 and ISS7, respectively. After adjusting
for baseline HSS7 score, geographical region, prior exposure to anti-IgE biologics, visit week,
treatment by visit interaction, and baseline HSS7 score by visit interaction, the estimated leastsquares mean change from baseline in HSS7 score at Week 24 was greater in magnitude in the
remibrutinib group (LS means =−10.88 [SE 0.40] and −10.92 [SE 0.41]) compared to placebo
(LS means =−7.97 [SE 0.55] and −7.31 [SE 0.56]) in Trials A2301 and A2302, respectively. For
HSS7, the estimated difference in LS means relative to placebo was −2.91 (95% CI: −4.16,
−1.66) and −3.61 (95% CI: −4.92, −2.29) in Trials A2301 and A2302, respectively. Similarly,
after adjusting for similar covariates, the estimated least-squares mean change from baseline in
ISS7 score at Week 24 was greater in magnitude in the remibrutinib group (LS means =−9.81
[SE 0.34] and −9.49 [SE 0.35]) compared to placebo (LS means =−8.00 [SE 0.47] and −6.46 [SE
0.48]) in Trials A2301 and A2302, respectively. For ISS7, the estimated difference in LS means
relative to placebo was −1.81 (95% CI: −2.88, −0.73) and −3.03 (95% CI: −4.16, −1.90) in Trials
A2301 and A2302, respectively. Both endpoints were nominally significant in both trials.
Up to Week 24, one subject in the remibrutinib 25-mg group experienced the intercurrent event
of intake of strongly confounding medication in Trial A2301. In Trial A2302, one subject in the
remibrutinib 25-mg group and two subjects in the placebo group experienced the intercurrent
event of intake of strongly confounding medication. Results of analyses which correspond to the
279

estimand with treatment policy strategy for all intercurrent events were substantially similar,
with p-value<0.001 and small differences in point estimates and confidence intervals which do
not impact the interpretation of the results.

16.1.3. Results of Sensitivity and Supplementary
Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302
Results for the prespecified supplementary analysis which varied the intercurrent event handling
strategies of the primary estimand were similar to and supportive of the primary analysis.
One subject in the placebo group did not meet inclusion criteria of baseline ISS7 ≥6 and
HSS7 ≥6 in Trial A2301. Three subjects in the FAS (two remibrutinib, one placebo) did not meet
this inclusion criteria in Trial A2302. Results from the sensitivity analyses which excluded these
subjects were consistent with the primary analysis.
In both Trials A2301 and A2302, the tipping-point analysis for HSS7 did not identify a tipping
point across the explored plausible delta values, supporting the robustness of the primary
analysis to violations of its missing data assumptions. Tipping points were identified for ISS7 for
both trials, as shown in Table 104 and Table 105. The assumptions at which one-sided pvalue >0.025 vary from the most extreme assumptions (all subjects with missing data in the
placebo group experiencing the best possible score and simultaneously all subjects with missing
data in the remibrutinib group experiencing the worst possible score), to somewhat less extreme
assumptions. These analyses reasonably support the robustness of the primary analysis with
respect to missing data assumptions in both trials.

and A2302
Subgroup analyses within Trial A2301 and A2302 were generally consistent with the primary
analyses results, as well as the results of the pooled analyses. The results of the pooled subgroup
analyses provide more precise estimates than estimates within each trial, and pooled results are
presented in Section 16.2.1.

Although the incidence of common TEAEs generally appears similar across demographic
subgroups, there are some imbalances, such as an apparent increased risk for bleeding-related
TEAEs in females (versus males), geriatric subjects ≥65 or 75 years of age (versus <65 or 75
years), and subjects of non-White race or Non-Hispanic ethnicity (versus Asian/Black/Multiple
or Hispanic ethnicity) who were treated with remibrutinib. Some of the differences are likely
artifacts caused by the small number of subjects within particular subgroups; however, the reason
for an imbalance between females and males is unclear.

17.2. Open-Label Period, Trials A2301 and A2302
The open-label (OL) periods for Trials A2301 and A2302 provided an additional 28 weeks of
exposure to subjects who had participated in the double-blind period (see Section 7.5 for
additional exposure details).
During the OL period of Trial A2301, 43.7% of subjects in the remibrutinib-to-remibrutinib
group and 46.6% of subjects in the placebo-to-remibrutinib group experienced at least one
TEAE. The majority of TEAEs were mild-to-moderate in severity. SAEs occurred in 1.5% and
0.8% of subjects in the respective groups during the OL period.
The overall TEAE incidence in the OL period of Trial A2302 was 52.9% in the remibrutinib-toremibrutinib group and 55.0% in the placebo-to-remibrutinib group. The most frequently
reported TEAEs (≥2% frequency) included COVID-19 (7.3% and 10.1%, respectively), upper
respiratory tract infection (5.0% and 6.2%), nasopharyngitis (3.9% and 2.3%), and suspected
295
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
COVID-19 (2.7% and 3.1%). These findings are consistent with common respiratory infections
and may reflect the study conduct period during the COVID-19 pandemic and do not raise any
new or unexpected safety signals.
Bleeding
During the OL period of Trials A2301 and A2302, the bleeding risk profile demonstrated
specific patterns when analyzed through EAOR. In the remibrutinib group during the entire study
period, 11.7% of subjects experienced bleeding AESIs compared to 10.6% during the placebocontrolled period. The EAOR analysis showed a decrease from 43.4 events per 100 patient-years
during the placebo-controlled period to 25.4 events per 100 patient-years during the entire study
period. For subjects who transitioned from placebo to remibrutinib during the OL phase, 6.9%
experienced bleeding AESIs. The median time-to-event onset was 47.5 days with a median event
duration of 43.0 days. All events in this population were mild or moderate in severity, with no
severe or serious bleeding events reported. The temporal analysis revealed that bleeding AESIs
occurred predominantly within the first 4 to 12 weeks of treatment initiation. During the OL
extension period, no additional serious bleeding AESIs were reported beyond those occurring in
the placebo-controlled phase. The types of bleeding events remained consistent throughout the
study duration, consisting primarily of cutaneous bleeding manifestations including petechiae,
contusion, and ecchymosis. Most bleeding events resolved during the study period, and most did
not require treatment modifications. See Section 7.7.1 for details on the bleeding risk during the
placebo-controlled period.
Infection
During the entire study period, 45.7% of subjects in the remibrutinib 25-mg BID group
experienced infection AESIs, with the EAOR decreasing to 82.7 events per 100 patient-years
compared to the placebo-controlled period, suggesting no increase in infection risk with longer
treatment duration. For subjects who transitioned from placebo to remibrutinib, 26.3%
experienced infection AESIs with an EAOR of 66.9 per 100 patient-years. The most frequently
reported infections were respiratory tract infections, including COVID-19 (10.7% versus 11.4%
in remibrutinib versus placebo groups), nasopharyngitis (6.6% versus 4.6%), and upper
respiratory tract infections, similar to the placebo-controlled period. No opportunistic infections
were reported using the standardized MedDRA query, and bacterial encapsulated infections were
not observed. See Section 7.7.2 for details on the infection risk during the placebo-controlled
period.
Cytopenia
During the entire study period, 5.9% of subjects in the remibrutinib 25-mg BID group
experienced cytopenia AESIs, with the EAOR decreasing from 12.1 per 100 patient-years during
the placebo-controlled period to 8.9 per 100 patient-years during the entire study period,
suggesting no increase in cytopenia risk with longer treatment duration. For subjects who
transitioned from placebo to remibrutinib, 1.5% experienced cytopenia AESIs and the majority
did not require treatment modifications. No serious cytopenia AESIs were reported, and none
were associated with increased infections or bleeding events. See Section 7.7.3 for details on the
cytopenia risk during the placebo-controlled period.

296
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
DILI
Liver enzyme elevations continued to occur at low frequencies with patterns similar to those
observed during the placebo-controlled period. In the remibrutinib group during the entire study
period, ALT or AST elevations greater than three times the ULN occurred in 1.5% of subjects
compared to 1.3% during the placebo-controlled period alone. One additional subject in the
remibrutinib group developed an asymptomatic AST elevation between 3x and 5x ULN after
completion of the placebo-controlled period (Table 112). For subjects who transitioned from
placebo to remibrutinib during the OL phase, liver enzyme elevations occurred in 1.2% of
subjects for ALT or AST greater than 3x ULN. This population experienced two asymptomatic
ALT elevations between 3x and 5x ULN and one ALT elevation between 10x and 20x ULN
combined with AST elevation between 5x and 8x ULN. Notably, the latter case occurred 14 days
after the last dose of remibrutinib and was attributed to excess antihistamine use in a subject with
underlying fatty liver disease who had previously experienced spontaneous ALT/AST elevation
during placebo treatment. Throughout the entire study period, no potential Hy's Law cases were
identified. See Section 7.6.1.8 for details on the risk of elevated liver enzymes during the
placebo-controlled period.

In conclusion, the safety data obtained during the OL periods of Trials A2301 and A2302 were
similar to the safety data from the initial 24-week controlled periods. The safety profile remained

consistent over time with no new signals identified and no significant change in the frequency or
severity of TEAEs with longer term exposure.

17.3. Study A2201
The results from Study A2201 provide a limited assessment of the dose-response for safety
across remibrutinib dosages of 10 mg QD up to 100 mg BID. Similar to the efficacy results from
this study, there were no apparent dose-related patterns observed in TEAEs, SAEs, TEAEs
leading to treatment discontinuations, laboratory abnormalities, vital signs, or ECG parameters.
The overall incidence of TEAEs was higher in the combined remibrutinib groups (58.1%)
compared to placebo (42.9%) as shown in Table 113.

Of the five remibrutinib-treated subjects who experienced SAEs, two reported CSU (remibrutinib 25-mg and 10-mg BID arms), and
the remaining SAEs were reported in one subject each: lymphadenopathy (remibrutinib 10 mg QD), renal abscess (remibrutinib 25 mg

dose-response relationship for the events: 10 mg BID (CSU, COVID-19 and renal pain), 25 mg BID (urinary tract infection/renal
abscess), and 100 mg BID (bradycardia, hematuria, and petechiae).
The most commonly reported adverse events were headache, nasopharyngitis, and CSU, the majority of which were mild-to-moderate
in severity.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
Regarding adverse events of special interest, infection rates were comparable between
remibrutinib (25.1%) and placebo (21.4%) groups, as were cytopenias (3.0% versus 2.4%).
Bleeding events were slightly more frequent in remibrutinib groups (6.7% versus 2.4%) but were
mostly mild, with only two moderate cases of gingival bleeding and hematuria, and two bleeding
events leading to discontinuation. Overall, there did not appear to be a dose-response pattern for
AESIs suggesting the safety profile of remibrutinib was consistent across the tested dose range.
Laboratory assessments revealed notable liver enzyme abnormalities in three subjects (all in
once-daily arms), but these were isolated elevations that did not meet Hy's Law criteria and did
not require treatment discontinuation. Hematology, clinical chemistry, and vital signs showed no
significant drug-related safety concerns. During the placebo-controlled period, four female
subjects experienced isolated QTcF values in the 10 mg BID, 25 mg BID, and 100 mg BID
groups. Notably, no subject exceeded the critical 500 ms threshold, and one case occurred during
the extension period, suggesting no treatment relationship. The observed QTcF elevations were
transient and were not associated with clinical adverse events nor was there a dose-response
relationship.

17.4. Study A2202E1
The safety profile of chronic treatment with remibrutinib 100 mg BID was consistent with the
preceding 12-week core study and did not reveal any new safety concerns. TEAEs were reported
in 139 out of 194 subjects (71.6%), with the majority being mild (35.1%) or moderate (32.5%) in
severity. The most commonly reported TEAEs included chronic spontaneous urticaria (11.3%,
primarily occurring during the treatment-free follow-up period), COVID-19 (8.2%), headache
(6.7%), and eczema (5.2%). Only 23 subjects (11.9%) experienced TEAEs suspected to be
related to study treatment, with all individual treatment-related events occurring in two or fewer
subjects each.
Serious adverse events were infrequent, occurring in only six subjects (3.1%) with seven total
SAEs reported, and none were considered related to study treatment. COVID-19 pneumonia was
the only SAE reported in more than one subject (two subjects, 1.0%). No deaths occurred during
the study. Treatment discontinuation due to adverse events was relatively uncommon, affecting
11 subjects (5.7%), with diarrhea being the only AE leading to discontinuation in more than one
subject (two subjects, 1.0%).
Regarding adverse events of special interest, infections were reported in 61 subjects (31.4%),
with COVID-19 and nasopharyngitis being the most frequent. Bleeding events occurred in 12
subjects (6.2%), and cytopenias were rare, affecting only 2 subjects (1.0%). Notable changes in
vital signs included sitting diastolic blood pressure abnormalities in 20.6% of subjects and sitting
systolic blood pressure abnormalities in 19.1%, though subjects with hypertension history were
permitted in the study. Laboratory abnormalities and ECG changes, including QTcF
prolongation, were infrequent and did not indicate new safety concerns. One pregnancy occurred
during the study with a normal course and healthy delivery, with the infant showing normal
development at 12-month follow-up.

301
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)

17.5. Study A1301
The safety results from Study A1301 were similar to the findings in Trials A2301 and A2302,
revealing no additional safety concerns with remibrutinib 25 mg BID over 52 weeks of
treatment. No deaths occurred during the study, and the overall completion rate was high with 68
of 71 subjects (95.8%) completing the full 52-week treatment period.
Adverse events were reported in 62 subjects (87.3%), but all were either mild (52.1% of
subjects) or moderate (35.2% of subjects) in severity, with no severe adverse events reported.
The most commonly affected system organ classes were infections and infestations (57.7% of
subjects) and skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders (53.5% of subjects), with COVID-19
(19.7%) and headache (12.7%) being the most frequent individual adverse events. Treatmentrelated adverse events occurred in 19 subjects (26.8%), primarily consisting of diarrhea,
petechiae, and purpura, each reported in 3 subjects (4.2%).
Three SAEs were reported (Meniere's disease, worsening epiretinal membrane, and
rhegmatogenous retinal detachment), none of which were considered related to study treatment.
Treatment discontinuation due to adverse events occurred in one subject (1.4%) due to
worsening atopic dermatitis, which was assessed as unrelated to study treatment. AESIs included
infections in 42 subjects (59.2%), bleeding events in 13 subjects (18.3%), and cytopenia in 5
subjects (7.0%), all of which were nonserious and predominantly mild in severity. Laboratory
evaluations, vital signs, and ECG parameters showed no clinically relevant abnormalities or
drug-related safety concerns, with the majority of laboratory abnormalities being CTCAE Grade
1 or 2 with no clinical consequences.

17.6. Study A2305
The safety profile of remibrutinib in this study was based on 33.51 subject-years of cumulative
exposure across 144 subjects and demonstrated consistency with the known safety profile for
remibrutinib, with no new or unexpected safety signals identified and no deaths reported during
the study period. Overall, 86 subjects (59.7%) experienced at least one AE, with the majority
being mild (41.7% of subjects) or moderate (16.0% of subjects) in severity, while only three
subjects (2.1%) reported severe AEs. The most commonly reported system organ classes were
infections and infestations (22.2% of subjects) and skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders
(15.3%), with the most frequent individual AEs being headache (9.0%), nasopharyngitis (8.3%),
and chronic spontaneous urticaria (6.3%). Treatment-related AEs were reported in 21 subjects
(14.6%), with purpura, nausea, headache, and heavy menstrual bleeding being the most common
(2.1% each).
SAEs occurred in four subjects (2.8%), including one case each of acute cholecystitis, dizziness,
chronic spontaneous urticaria, and aortic dissection. Two subjects (1.4%) discontinued treatment
due to AEs (increased blood pressure and aortic dissection). Infection AESIs were reported in
22.9% of subjects, with all 51 events being nonserious and mild-to-moderate in severity;
bleeding AESIs occurred in 8.3% of subjects with all 21 events being nonserious and mild; and
cytopenia AESIs were reported in 4.2% of subjects with all 6 events being mild-to-moderate and
nonserious. Laboratory analyses, vital signs, and ECG monitoring revealed no clinically relevant
abnormalities or drug-related safety concerns, with all newly occurring laboratory abnormalities
302
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5668341

NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
being CTCAE Grade 1 or 2 except for a single Grade 3 triglyceride increase in a subject with
preexisting elevated triglycerides at baseline. There were no liver enzyme abnormalities
exceeding three times the upper limit of normal and no cases meeting potential Hy's Law criteria.
The clinical pharmacologist within the Division of Cardiology and Nephrology reviewed the
study with regard to hypertension-related adverse events and concluded that the study supported
that 25-mg remibrutinib is not associated with clinically significant changes in blood pressure
(see review dated June 13, 2025).

17.7. Study F12101
The safety profile of remibrutinib 100 mg BID administered to healthy subjects was consistent
with prior remibrutinib clinical studies and comparable to placebo, with no new safety signals
identified. The overall incidence of adverse events was similar across treatment groups, with
84.2% in the concomitant remibrutinib group, 88.6% in the interrupted remibrutinib group, and
82.4% in the placebo group. The majority of adverse events were mild-to-moderate in severity,
with 83.2% classified as Grade 1 and 14.0% as Grade 2.
No deaths or serious adverse events were reported during the study. Adverse events leading to
study treatment discontinuation were more frequent in the concomitant remibrutinib group
(13.2%) compared to the interrupted remibrutinib group (2.9%) and placebo group (5.9%), with
COVID-19 being the most common reason for discontinuation.
The overall incidence of infections was numerically higher in the concomitant remibrutinib
group (23.7%) compared to the interrupted remibrutinib (14.3%) and placebo (17.6%) groups,
though no specific infectious condition drove this difference. Bleeding events were reported in
the concomitant remibrutinib group (7.9%) and placebo group (8.8%) but not in the interrupted
remibrutinib group; all bleeding events were Grade 1 in severity, and none led to treatment
discontinuation. No cytopenia adverse events were reported in any treatment group. Laboratory
parameters, vital signs, and ECG remained generally comparable between treatment groups with
no clinically meaningful changes observed throughout the study. For additional details regarding
the significance of this study, refer to Section 7.7.5.

the healthcare practitioner.

24. Postmarketing Requirements and
Commitments
PMR 1 (4896-1)
Conduct a 24-week randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group study in
pediatric patients aged 12 to less than 18 years with chronic spontaneous urticaria who remain
symptomatic despite H1-antihistamine treatment to assess the efficacy, safety, and
pharmacokinetic responses to remibrutinib.


Final Protocol Submission:

September 2022 (complete)



Study Completion:

September 2027



Final Report Submission:

February 2028

PMR 2 (4896-2)
Conduct a 24-week, open-label, pharmacokinetic study in pediatric patients aged 6 to less than
12 years with chronic spontaneous urticaria who remain symptomatic despite H1-antihistamine
treatment.


Final Protocol Submission:

December 2027



Study Completion:

October 2030



March 2031

PMR 3 (4896-3)
Conduct an additional oral (gavage) juvenile toxicity study in the rat. The age of rats at study
start should be equivalent to pediatric patients approximately 6 years old. The study should
include ophthalmic examinations performed prestudy, at the end of the dosing period, and at the
end of the recovery period. Potential neurobehavioral effects should also be evaluated in animals
at the end of the dosing and recovery periods.


Draft Protocol Submission:

September 2026

August 2027

PMR-4 (4896-4)
Collect data from a prospective pregnancy exposure registry, preferably a disease-based
multiproduct pregnancy registry, using a cohort analysis that compares the maternal, fetal, and
infant outcomes of women exposed to remibrutinib during pregnancy with an appropriate
comparator population(s). Collect data outside the United States to reach the target sample size,
if feasible. The registry will identify and record major and minor congenital malformations,
pregnancy complications, spontaneous abortion, stillbirths, neonatal deaths, pregnancy
terminations, preterm births, small-for-gestational-age births, and any other adverse outcomes,
including postnatal growth and development. These outcomes should be assessed throughout
pregnancy. Infant outcomes, including effects on postnatal growth and development, will be
assessed through at least the first year of life.


when administered by oral gavage once daily for 104 weeks.
Rats Study:

1.

For either male or female rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically
significant dose response relationship in mortality across the water control group
and treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically
significant differences in mortality between the water control group and any of the
treated groups.

2.

For either male or female rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically
significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group
and treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically
significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control group and any of the
treated groups.

1. Background
In this submission the sponsor included the report of one animal carcinogenicity study in
Crl:WI (Han) rats. This study was intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of LOU064,
when administered by oral gavage once daily for 104 weeks.
In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the
effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor
incidence rate as the dose increases.

Rat Study
Two separate experiments were conducted, one in males and one in females. In each of
these two experiments there were three treated groups, one water control, and one vehicle
control group. Two hundred and fifty rats of each sex were randomly assigned to the treated
groups, water control group, and vehicle control group in equal size of 50 rats per group.
The study was designed to continue for up to 104 weeks for both sexes. Rats in water
control group received purified water. Rats in vehicle control group received 2% w/w
Kolliodon VA64, 0.1% w/w Sodium Lauryl Sulfate in water.

Sponsor's analyses

1.1.1. Survival analysis
Tests to compare survival were performed using two-sided risk for increasing and decreasing
mortality with dose. Tests were performed for dose response (vehicle control and dosed
groups only) and for pairwise comparisons of the water control and each dosed group against

Reference ID: 5560172

NDA218436

Page 8 of 37

vehicle control using Kaplan-Meier product-limit estimation curves, along with Log-Rank and
Wilcoxon tests. These were performed using the LIFETEST procedure in SAS (SAS 20022008). The time to death or euthanasia (in weeks, calculation detailed in the following) was the
dependent variable. The groups were included as the strata. Animals with a death or
euthanasia status recorded as a scheduled euthanasia (interim or terminal) or accidental
death were censored in the analysis.
Sponsor’s findings: The sponsor’s analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival
were 26 (52%), 27 (54%), 25 (50%), 25 (50%) and 23 (46%) male rats, and 28 (56%), 29 (58%),
24 (48%), 26 (52%) and 25 (50%) in female rats in water control, vehicle control, low, mid and
high dose groups, respectively.
The sponsor concluded that oral (gavage) administration of 30, 100, or 300 mg/kg/day
LOU064 to Crl:WI (Han) rats for up to 104 weeks resulted in no LOU064-related effects on
mortality in males or females.

1.1.2. Tumor data analysis
Tests to compare tumor incidence were performed using a one-sided risk for increasing
incidence with dose. Tests were performed for dose response (vehicle control and dosed
groups only) and for pairwise comparisons of the water control and each dosed group
against the vehicle control group. The water and vehicle control groups were compared
using a two sided tests.
For tumors occurring in animals dying spontaneously or euthanized in a moribund condition
during the study, the pathologist classified the context of observation as one of the
following.
Fatal: The tumor was a factor in the demise of the animal.
Nonfatal: The tumor was not a factor in the demise of the animal.
Uncertain: Not clear if the tumor was a factor in the demise of the animal.

Reference ID: 5560172

NDA218436

Page 9 of 37

Non-palpable non-fatal and fatal tumors were analyzed by the IARC asymptotic fixed
interval-based prevalence and death rate tests respectively (Peto et al 1980).
The cut-off points for the interval-based test were Weeks 0 to 52, 53 to 78, 79 to 92, 93 to
before terminal euthanasia, and the terminal euthanasia. Fatal and non-fatal tumors were
analyzed together, with separate stratum for each. No tumors of uncertain context were
noted. The test was implemented using PROC MULTTEST in the SAS system (SAS 20022008). In the case of sparse tables (<10 total in a stratum), the exact form of the test was
used for that stratum. Otherwise, the asymptotic version of the test was used.
Sponsor’s findings: Sponsor concluded that oral (gavage) administration of 30, 100, or 300
mg/kg/day LOU064 to Crl:WI (Han) rats for up to 104 weeks resulted in no LOU064-related
neoplastic findings in males or females.

1.2.

Reviewer's analyses

To verify sponsor’s analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing
pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses.

1.2.1. Survival analysis
The survival distributions of three treated groups, vehical control group, and water control
group were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response
relationship in survival was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of
survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival
rates are given in Figures 1 and 2 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The
intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 6 and 7 in the appendix for male and female
rats, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of
survivals among the water control and the treated groups, and among the vehicle control and
three treated groups are given in Tables 8 to 11 in the appendix for male and female rats,
respectively.

Reference ID: 5560172

NDA218436

Page 10 of 37

Reviewer’s findings: The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival
were 26 (52%), 27 (54%), 25 (50%), 25 (50%) and 23 (46%) male rats, and 28 (56%), 29 (58%),
24 (48%), 26 (52%) and 25 (50%) in female rats in water control, vehicle control, low, mid and
high dose groups, respectively.
For either male or female rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically significant
dose response relationship in mortality across the water control group and treated groups. The
pairwise comparisons also did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality
between the water control group and any of the treated groups in either sex.
For either male or female rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically significant
dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups.
The pairwise comparisons also did not show any statistically significant differences in
mortality between the vehicle control group and any of the treated groups in either sex.

1.2.2. Tumor data analysis
The tumor data were analyzed for the positive dose response relationships and the positive
pairwise comparison increases between each of the treated groups with control group. Both
the dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the
Poly-K method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams
(1993). In this method an animal that lives the full study period ( wmax ) or dies before the
terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of sh =1. An animal

 w 
that dies at week wh without a tumor before the end of the study gets a score of sh =  h 
 wmax 
< 1. The adjusted group size is defined as Σ sh . As an interpretation, an animal with score sh

k

=1 can be considered as a whole animal while an animal with score sh < 1 can be considered
as a partial animal. The adjusted group size Σ sh is equal to N (the original group size) if all
animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice
develops at least one tumor, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted
group sizes were then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the
Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value
of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104

Reference ID: 5560172

NDA218436

Page 11 of 37

week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this
reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values the exact
permutation method was used.
The tumor rates and the p-values for the positive dose response relationship tests and
pairwise comparisons between the water control and the treated group, and between the
vehicle control and three treated groups are listed in Tables 12 to 15 in the appendix for male
and female rats, respectively.
Adjustment for multiple testing: For the chronic study in rats, the adjustment of multiple
testing of the dose response relationship for a submission with one chronic rat study, the
more recently revised draft (January, 2013) FDA guidance for the carcinogenicity studies
suggests the use of test levels α =0.005 for common tumors and α=0.025 for rare tumors
for the chronic rat study. For pairwise comparisonsfor the chronic rat study in the above
type of submission with one chronic rat study, the same guidance document suggests the
use of test levels α =0.01 for common tumors and α =0.05 for rare tumors for the chronic
rat study.
It should be noted that the FDA guidance for multiple testing for dose response relationship
is based on a publication by Lin and Rahman (1998). In this work the authors investigated
the use of this rule for Peto analysis. However, in a later work Rahman and Lin (2008)
showed that this rule for multiple testing for dose response relationship is also suitable for
Poly-K tests.

Reviewer’s findings: The tumor types in Tables 2 to 5 below showed p-values less than or
equal to 0.05 in the tests between water control or vehicle control and the treated groups
for males and females.
Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed in the rats data analysis
section, we make the following statistical conclusions:
1.

For male rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose
response relationship in tumor incidence across the water control groups and the
treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant

Reference ID: 5560172

NDA218436

Page 12 of 37

differences in tumor incidence between the water control groups and any of the
treated groups.
2.

For male rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose
response relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and the
treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant
differences in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and any of the
treated groups.

3.

For female rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose
response relationship in tumor incidence across the water control groups and the
treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant
differences in tumor incidence between the water control groups and any of the
treated groups.

4.

For female mice, the trend test showed a statistically significant positive dose
response relationships in incidence of endometri adenocarcinoma in uterus (pvalue=0.0021<0.005). The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically
significant differences in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and
any of the treated groups.
